## How to use this backlog

- **Hierarchy:** Phase → Epic → Feature (FEAT-###) → User story (US-###) → Tasks (checkboxes).
- **Order:** phases are sequential by exit criteria; epics inside a phase can run in parallel unless a dependency is listed.
- **No dates.** The source pack asks that delivery durations not be used as commitments. Sizes are relative.
- **Size (T-shirt, per story):** **S** = a few days · **M** = 1–2 weeks · **L** = 3–6 weeks · **XL** = more than 6 weeks (small experienced team; rough and provisional).
- **Status:** `[ ]` not started · `[~]` in progress (edit manually) · `[x]` done. Update [implement_log.md](implement_log.md) after every execution.
- **Traceability:** every story cites its FEAT, REQ, RULE, API, DATA and TEST IDs from `project-docs/`. Acceptance criteria are the TEST IDs in `project-docs/function_document.md` (TEST-001–070) and `project-docs/technical_document.md` (TEST-071–099).
- **Open decisions:** the pack's working positions are adopted (see [summary.md §10.2](summary.md#102-open-questions-and-adopted-working-positions)). Each one has a `DEC-` task in Phase D; if a decision changes, update the dependent stories.

### Definition of Done (every story)

- [ ] Code merged through a pull request with review; CI green (lint, types, unit, security suites).
- [ ] Acceptance tests named in the story are automated and passing.
- [ ] Scope and read-only guarantees untouched or re-verified (scope isolation suite passes).
- [ ] No secrets, prompt text, result rows or SQL literals in logs (log redaction test passes).
- [ ] API changes reflected in the OpenAPI spec and regenerated TypeScript types.
- [ ] Audit events emitted for any governance or security action the story introduces.
- [ ] Migrations (if any) upgrade and downgrade cleanly; expand → contract respected.
- [ ] Docs updated (feature notes, runbook entries) and the story ticked here.

## Phase P0 — Foundation

**Goal:** a safe, observable skeleton. **Exit:** a user signs in with Microsoft; role and scope are resolved; every container is healthy with restart policies; migrations are repeatable; CI is green.

> **Status (EXEC-002, 2026-10-21): P0 delivered, with open follow-ups.** Sign-in works end to end (Keycloak dev realm,
> real PKCE flow tested in Chromium), role and scope resolve, all 12 long-running containers are healthy, and migrations
> cycle up/down/up. Open items are marked `[ ]` or `[~]` below with the reason. The biggest are: the Entra broker for
> a real tenant (PRE-01), CI not yet run on a hosted runner (no remote), observability configs, and OpenAPI type generation.

### EPIC-00 Platform foundation

#### US-001 · Monorepo and tooling · M — `[x]` done
As a developer, I want one repository with the agreed layout and tooling, so that every service builds and tests the same way.
Refs: technical_document §Repository structure, §Coding conventions.
- [x] Create the monorepo layout: `apps/web`, `services/api`, `services/connector-runner`, `services/llm-gateway`, `workers`, `packages/query-spec`, `packages/chart-spec`, `packages/tela-common`, `migrations`, `packs/pmo`, `deploy/{compose,helm,keycloak}`, `tests`. *(query-spec, chart-spec, packs/pmo and helm hold README placeholders for P1/P3.)*
- [x] Set up the Python `uv` workspace (Python 3.13), ruff, mypy (strict on `packages/*`), pytest + hypothesis.
- [~] Set up the pnpm workspace with React + TypeScript (strict), Vite, eslint, prettier, Vitest + RTL, Playwright. *(Done except prettier.)*
- [x] Add `tela-common`: structured logging (structlog JSON with fields `ts, level, service, request_id, trace_id, tenant_id, user_id(hashed), event, duration_ms, code`), error codes catalogue, UUID v7 helper.
- [~] Add pre-commit hooks, CODEOWNERS, PR template with the Definition of Done. *(CODEOWNERS waits for named owners.)*
- [ ] Add a naming rule check: never shadow stdlib/PyPI module names (lesson S16 RISK-009).

#### US-002 · Docker images for every service · M — `[x]` done
As a platform operator, I want a hardened, versioned image per service, so that the same images run everywhere (ADR-005, NFR-019).
Refs: technical_document §Docker images; architecture §Deployment.
- [x] Multi-stage Dockerfiles: `tela-web` (nginx unprivileged, CSP headers), `tela-api`, `tela-migrate`, `tela-connector-runner`, `tela-llm-gateway`, `tela-worker`, `tela-beat` (worker image, beat command). *(ODBC Driver 18 added to the runner in EXEC-024; Chromium to the worker with US-096.)*
- [x] Non-root UID, read-only root filesystem + tmpfs, `no-new-privileges`, dropped capabilities, slim bases.
- [x] `/healthz` (liveness, no dependencies) and `/readyz` (DB, Redis, secrets) on every service; fatal start-up errors exit non-zero. *(Worker health = `celery inspect ping`; beat has no health endpoint.)*
- [~] Single release version tag shared by all first-party images (`TELA_VERSION`); third-party images pinned by tag. *(Pin by digest when a registry is chosen.)*

#### US-003 · Docker Compose stack and profiles · M — `[x]` done
As a developer and customer-hosted operator, I want one-command start, so that the full stack runs locally and on a single host.
Refs: diagrams/deployment-docker.md; technical_document §Compose profiles.
- [~] `deploy/compose/compose.yaml` with profiles `core`, `ai-local`, `observability`. *(`dev` profile with sample sources comes with the P1 connectors.)*
- [x] Networks: `edge` (proxy, web, API, Keycloak), internal `app`, `sources` (runner only), `llm-egress` (gateway only); only the proxy publishes a port (127.0.0.1:8088 locally).
- [x] Health checks and `restart: unless-stopped` on every daemon; `tela-migrate` and a one-shot `bootstrap` (dev tenant) finish before `tela-api` starts.
- [x] Secrets injected as files (`*_FILE`); `deploy/compose/dev-setup.sh` generates them (git-ignored) instead of a `.env.example`.
- [~] `dev` profile: sample PostgreSQL TPG source seeded with **synthetic** PMO data (EXEC-003). *(MySQL, SQL Server, MongoDB samples and the Dataverse fake added in EXEC-024; Mailpit still open.)*
- [x] Smoke test: `compose up` → all containers healthy (TEST-096 baseline) + `tests/smoke` end-to-end sign-in.

#### US-004 · CI pipeline from day one · M — `[~]` authored, not yet run on a hosted runner
As the team, I want CI on every pull request, so that quality and security gates are enforced from the first commit (REC-012, NFR-024).
Refs: technical_document §CI.
- [~] Stages in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`: lint → types → tests with 80% coverage gate → migrations up/down/up → web typecheck/lint/test/build → image build, SBOM (Syft), Trivy (fail on critical). *(Missing: OpenAPI → `schema.d.ts` diff, Compose integration + Playwright stage, multi-arch, cosign.)*

- [x] Guard: build fails if `openai`, `anthropic`, `google.genai` or `ollama` are imported outside `services/llm-gateway`.
- [ ] Deploy jobs: dev automatic; UAT and prod manual approval with change record. *(Needs a registry and environments.)*

#### US-005 · Metadata database and migrations · L — `[x]` done for P0 schemas
As the platform, I want the PostgreSQL 16 + pgvector metadata store with versioned migrations, so that all Tela state lives in one transactional store (ADR-004).
Refs: db_scheme §2, §6–§12, §17. DATA-001 – DATA-051.
- [x] SQLAlchemy 2 async engine (asyncpg), unit of work per request (`tenant_tx`). *(SQLAlchemy Core with explicit SQL rather than ORM models.)*
- [~] Alembic revisions `0001_extensions_util`, `0002_iam`, `0003_conn`, `0004_iam_policies`, `0005_ops`, `0006_rls`. *(Schemas `model`, `defs`, `content`, `gov`, `chat`, `rag` are added by the epics that use them, each with its RLS policies.)*
- [x] Enumerations as `text` + CHECK constraints, connection status aligned with the state machine (PRE-06, I-3).
- [~] Immutability triggers and audit hash chain on `ops.audit_events`. *(Model versions, dashboard versions and decisions get theirs with their tables.)*
- [~] Monthly partitioning for `audit_events` and `query_executions` with `util.ensure_month_partitions` + daily worker job. *(`turns`, `turn_events` with US-046.)*
- [x] Seed roles and permissions (one permission catalogue, PRE-06 / I-2).
- [x] Migration test: upgrade on empty DB, downgrade to base, upgrade again (TEST-098 baseline).

#### US-006 · Tenant isolation with row-level security · M — `[x]` done
As a security architect, I want `tenant_id` + RLS on every tenant table, so that tenants can never read each other's metadata (NFR-023, REC-020).
Refs: technical_document §Tenant isolation; db_scheme §9.
- [x] Revision `0006_rls`: RLS policies on every tenant table; app role `tela_app` is not owner and has `NOBYPASSRLS`.
- [x] Per-transaction `set_config('app.tenant_id', …, true)`; tenant always from the token issuer, never from the body.
- [x] Global rows (`tenant_id IS NULL`) readable only via read policy; app role cannot modify them.
- [x] Tests: crafted cross-tenant IDs return zero rows; WITH CHECK rejects cross-tenant writes (TEST-091).

#### US-007 · Secrets store integration · S — `[x]` done (Azure Key Vault with US-122)
As a platform operator, I want credentials only in OpenBao/Vault, so that the database, logs and browser never hold secrets (NFR-013, RISK-009).
Refs: COMP-013; API-069.
- [x] Secrets adapter (`openbao`/`vault` via `hvac`, `file` for local only); 5 s timeout, 2 retries on transient errors; fail closed. Service identities: AppRole, Kubernetes auth or an agent-written token file (`TELA_SECRETS_AUTH`); signs in again before the lease ends or once when a token is refused (EXEC-027). *(`azure_keyvault` with US-122.)*
- [x] Write-only secret fields: API writes straight to the store and keeps only `secret_ref` (EXEC-003, US-028).
- [x] Policies so only the runner and gateway can read their secret paths: `deploy/openbao/policies` (API create/update/patch/delete, never read — credential changes are KV v2 merge patches; runner reads `connections/*`; gateway reads `llm/*`). Local profile: `openbao-init` sets up the policies and AppRoles, and only it holds the dev root token; `deploy/openbao/check-policies.sh` checks the allow/deny matrix (EXEC-027). *(Gateway wiring comes with the first keyed provider; Helm roles with US-121.)*
- [x] Start-up refuses empty or placeholder (all-zero / repeated) signing keys.
- [x] **BUG-001 fixed (EXEC-030):** editing a connection's password failed with "A required credential is not available." when the store had lost the secret (dev OpenBao is in-memory and loses data on restart; the edit was a merge patch only). A full credential edit now re-creates the secret (`SecretNotFound`, `_patch_secret`); an edit without a password says "The saved credential is missing. Enter the password again." Test: `test_credential_edit_recreates_lost_secret`.
- [x] **BUG-002 fixed (EXEC-033):** Home scrolled sideways and its shelves looked empty when a source had many all-rows entities: the scope badge listed every entity on one unbreakable line. The summary now counts all-rows entities ("All rows (36 tables)"), the badge wraps, and suggested questions rank business tables first and skip dimensions reached by fanning out. Tests: `test_home_unit.py`, `test_summary_counts_all_rows_entities_instead_of_listing_them`.
- [x] **SSH key files (ENH-003, EXEC-035):** uploaded .pem / .ppk / OpenSSH keys are stored only in the connection's secret (`tenants/<t>/connections/<id>`, fields `ssh_private_key` / `ssh_password`), normalised to OpenSSH, passphrase not kept; the existing policies already fit (API writes, runner reads). Checked live in dev OpenBao.
- [x] **SSH key library (ENH-004, EXEC-036):** `tenants/<t>/ssh_keys/<id>` — API create/update/patch/delete, runner read, gateway nothing; `check-policies.sh` covers it (all ok live).

#### US-008 · Object storage integration · S — `[x]` done (Azure Blob with US-122)
As the platform, I want S3-compatible storage, so that uploads, exports and knowledge documents are stored with encryption and lifecycle rules.
Refs: COMP-014; API-068.
- [~] S3 adapter (`boto3`); local profile uses **SeaweedFS** (MinIO community images are no longer pullable). *(Azure Blob adapter with US-122.)*
- [x] Tenant-prefixed keys and server-side encryption on put: `AES256` or `aws:kms` (+ key id); a put the store does not confirm as encrypted is deleted and fails; encryption off only in local development (EXEC-027).
- [x] Lifecycle: objects tagged `tela-class=exports|files|knowledge`; `tela-admin storage-lifecycle [--apply]` sets the bucket rules (exports expire after 2 days as a backstop to the 24 h sweep, unfinished uploads and old versions after 1 day), reads them back and removes them if the store changed them. SeaweedFS (local) keeps only a whole-bucket rule, so it is refused there and the API sweeps stay authoritative (EXEC-027).
- [x] Readiness reports `storage` without gating it (API-068: dashboards keep working without storage).
- [x] Short-lived pre-signed URLs only.

#### US-009 · Background worker and transactional outbox · M — `[x]` done
As the platform, I want durable background jobs, so that introspection, indexing and exports retry safely (ADR-011).
Refs: technical_document §Background jobs; DATA-050.
- [x] Celery app with Redis broker; queues `default`, `index`, `export`, `introspect`, `maintenance`; `acks_late`, `task_reject_on_worker_lost`.
- [x] `outbox_events` written in the same transaction as state changes; `outbox_relay` every 2 s, idempotent claim (SKIP LOCKED), dead-letter after 10 attempts.
- [x] `beat` single replica; `ensure_partitions` daily; `retention_sweep` stub (DEC-12).
- [x] Outbox lag: `ops.f_outbox_lag_seconds()` sampled by the relay into Redis, exposed by the API as `tela_outbox_lag_seconds` / `tela_outbox_relay_up`, scraped by Prometheus, alerts `TelaOutboxLagHigh` and `TelaOutboxRelayDown` (EXEC-028).
- [x] Every emitted topic has a handler (`workers/tests/test_outbox_topics.py`); `tela-admin outbox-requeue [--topic] [--tenant] [--dry-run]` replays dead letters (EXEC-029).

#### US-010 · Observability baseline · M — `[x]` done (Alertmanager routing and worker metrics pending)
As a platform operator, I want traces, metrics and logs end to end, so that every request is traceable by one correlation ID (NFR-017).
Refs: architecture §22; technical_document §Error handling, logging and observability.
- [x] OpenTelemetry (enabled when an OTLP endpoint is set): FastAPI on api, gateway and runner; httpx; Celery; SQLAlchemy per engine (statements only, no parameter values). Request id from the edge proxy is bound to logs and forwarded API → gateway/runner and worker → API; log lines carry `trace_id`. One trace spans api → runner (EXEC-028).
- [x] `observability` profile: collector, Prometheus (scrape + 10 alert rules), Tempo, Loki, Grafana with provisioned data sources and a *Tela overview* dashboard (`deploy/observability/`); config checks in `tests/observability` (EXEC-028). *(Alertmanager / notification routing: with the pilot's on-call choice. Worker process metrics: only its outbox lag is exposed, through the API.)*
- [x] Metrics: request duration, turn/query duration, validation rejections, scope denials, auth failures, cache hit ratio, export jobs, outbox lag; gateway calls/tokens/provider latency/policy refusals; runner executions; `/metrics` on each service (not exposed at the edge); API gunicorn workers aggregate through `PROMETHEUS_MULTIPROC_DIR`.
- [x] Log redaction test: no prompts, rows, SQL parameter values, tokens or passwords in logs.

#### US-011 · LLM gateway skeleton · M — `[x]` done (Ollama, Anthropic, OpenAI, Azure OpenAI, Gemini — EXEC-031)
As a security architect, I want one chokepoint to model providers, so that masking, leak scanning, budgets and audit apply to every call (ADR-008, RULE-019, RULE-021).
Refs: technical_document §LLM gateway implementation; API-060, API-061; DATA-046.
- [x] Internal `POST /generate` and `POST /embed` with signed short-lived service token. *(mTLS with Helm, US-121.)*
- [~] Policy check: allowed task types, provider allow-list (empty by default; compose lists the DEC-01 providers), aggregates switch for AG-08 narration (`TELA_LLM_ALLOW_AGGREGATES` and the tenant toggle), per-tenant monthly token budget, per-user rate limit. *(In-process counters; Redis-backed for multi-replica with US-121.)*
- [x] Leak scan on raw text (secrets, injection markers) **and** on masked text (PII) → `leak_blocked`, no outbound call (TEST-079). *(Found and fixed: masking had hidden a connection string's credentials.)*
- [x] Output safety and JSON-schema check → `invalid_output`.
- [x] Provider adapters (EXEC-031): Ollama; Anthropic (structured output, server-side refusal fallback); OpenAI and Azure OpenAI (tenant endpoint); Gemini. Official SDKs, imported only inside the gateway. Tenant keys are read from OpenBao with the gateway's read-only AppRole, one key per provider. Embeddings from the tenant's provider (OpenAI, Azure, Gemini at 1024 dimensions) or the deployment's Ollama; changing the embedding model clears vectors for the re-embed job.
- [x] Metadata-only ledger to `ops.llm_invocations` (no prompt or completion text — TEST-080).
- [x] Operations signals (EXEC-028): `/metrics` (calls by outcome, tokens, provider latency, policy refusals — labels never carry tenant, user or text), request-id echo and log binding, traces with the API's trace context. The open `[~]` item above waits on US-121 (Redis counters, mTLS).

### EPIC-01 Identity and access (part 1)

#### US-012 · Microsoft sign-in via Keycloak · M · FEAT-001 — `[x]` done (Entra broker pending PRE-01)
As any user, I want to sign in with my Microsoft account, so that Tela has no separate passwords.
Refs: REQ-001, REQ-002, NFR-004; API-001, API-002, API-058; DATA-002, DATA-003. Acceptance: **TEST-001**.
- [~] Keycloak realm template (public SPA client, PKCE S256, `tela-api` audience, `groups` claim, 5-minute tokens, refresh rotation) with local dev users. *(Entra ID identity provider + group/oid/tid mappers need the tenant app registration, PRE-01; local profile runs Keycloak in dev mode.)*
- [x] SPA: OIDC Authorization Code + PKCE (`oidc-client-ts`), tokens in memory only, silent renew. *(Verified in Chromium: no JWT in web storage, no CSP violations.)*
- [x] API `JwtVerifier`: JWKS from the configured issuer only, `aud = tela-api`, `exp/nbf`, access token type, RS256/ES256; JWKS cache 10 minutes, refetch on unknown `kid`.
- [x] Map issuer + subject (and Entra `oid`/`tid` when present) to `users` / `external_identities` (never e-mail only); create user on first login.
- [x] `GET /api/v1/me`; `POST /api/v1/auth/logout`; SPA sign-out ends the Keycloak session (bug found and fixed by E2E).
- [x] No-mapped-group path shows MSG-001; wrong issuer/audience/expired/ID tokens rejected; suspended users refused.
- [x] Audit `auth.login` (success and deny, committed even when access is refused) and `auth.logout`.

#### US-013 · Principal, permissions and authorisation dependencies · M · FEAT-001/002 — `[x]` done
As a developer, I want a `Principal` and permission checks on every route, so that features are protected consistently.
Refs: technical_document §Principal and permissions.
- [x] `PrincipalResolver`: user → groups → `group_role_mappings` → roles → `role_permissions`; cache ≤ 60 s, invalidated on mapping/policy change. *(In-process; Redis-backed with multi-replica.)*
- [x] FastAPI dependency chain: request id → `get_principal` → `require(permission)` → `tenant_tx`.
- [x] Permission key catalogue (single source, PRE-06 / I-2) seeded for Admin, Creator, Viewer.
- [x] Out-of-scope or other-tenant object IDs return 404 (RLS).
- [x] Every route except health requires a valid token (tests).

#### US-014 · Group to role mapping administration · M · FEAT-002 — `[x]` done
As an Admin, I want to map Entra groups to a permission level and audience roles, so that nobody's access is hand-built per user.
Refs: REQ-003, REQ-006; API-004; DATA-004, DATA-006, DATA-008. Acceptance: **TEST-002**. Depends: DEC-03, DEC-04.
- [x] `GET|PUT /api/v1/admin/group-mappings` (full replace, If-Match / ETag); unknown groups and roles rejected; group registration endpoint.
- [x] Admin-granting mappings stay pending until a *different* Admin approves; high-severity audit; changes to an Admin mapping need re-approval; the last active Admin mapping cannot be removed (409).
- [x] Multi-group resolution: highest level wins (E-01).
- [x] Cached principals invalidated on change; audit `access.mapping.changed`; outbox `access.mappings_changed`.
- [ ] Graph lookup for group search when SCIM is not yet live (API-059, optional).

#### US-015 · Scope and field policy administration · M · FEAT-002/003 — `[x]` done (model checks in P1)
As an Admin, I want to define row scope and hidden fields per group and connection, so that data access follows business rules.
Refs: REQ-004, REQ-005; API-005, API-006; DATA-009, DATA-010; RULE-004, RULE-005.
- [x] `scope-policies` CRUD with typed predicates (`=`, `in`, `not in`, `is null`, attribute references, explicit "all rows").
- [x] Validation: entity and fields must exist in the approved model when there is one (EXEC-003).
- [x] `field-policies` (hide/mask per role), full replace per connection.
- [x] Scope = union across policies; hidden fields = most restrictive; missing attribute or no policy = deny (DEC-03); stable `scope_hash` for cache keys.
- [x] `POST /scope-policies/preview` returns the resolved level, roles, scope, hidden fields and rows in scope per entity for a named person (EXEC-003).
- [x] **ENH-001 done (EXEC-034):** New scope policy picks Entity and Field from the selected connection's approved model instead of typing them. `GET /admin/scope-policies/entities?connection_id=` (`policies.manage`) returns the model's entities (key, label, source table) and fields, not limited by the admin's own scope. Entity shows "Label (schema.table)"; Field lists that entity's fields, stays disabled until an entity is chosen and clears when it changes. With no approved model both stay text inputs (any entity is accepted then). Tests: `test_policy_form_lists_the_approved_models_entities`, no-model and 404 paths in `test_scope_policy_validation_paths`, vitest "picks the entity and its fields from the connection's approved model".
- [ ] **ENH-002 (proposed):** "Shown on the scope chip" is required but never shown for All rows policies (the summary says "all rows" / "All rows (N tables)"). Make it optional when All rows is ticked, or fill a default.

#### US-016 · Admin access screens · M · FEAT-002 — `[x]` done
As an Admin, I want screens for mappings and policies, so that I can manage access without API calls.
Refs: SCR-12 Admin access.
- [x] Group mappings list/edit with approval state; register groups.
- [~] Scope policy editor per connection with preview of a named person (level, roles, scope). *(Row count with P1.)*

- [x] Admin menu visible to Admin only (also enforced by the API).

## Phase P1 — Governed read path

**Goal:** one source, trusted answers. **Exit:** zero out-of-scope rows in adversarial tests on every surface; certified figure parity across widget, chat and export paths; ground-truth suite meets the agreed threshold (DEC-11).

> **Status (EXEC-003, 2026-10-21): read path delivered end to end (backend).** A PostgreSQL TPG extract is
> connected read-only through the connector runner, introspected, the PMO pack is detected and installed,
> model v1 is approved, and every pack measure reconciles with an independent oracle under four personas.
> Scope isolation, injection and property suites run in CI. Delivered: US-017 – US-028, US-030, US-031,
> US-035 – US-041, US-043. Not in this run (agreed scope): US-029, US-032 – US-034 (screens and model
> assistant), US-042, and EPIC-04, 05, 10, 03, SP (Home, Ask, Reading, RAG, spikes).
>
> **Status (EXEC-004, 2026-10-21): rest of the agreed P1 delivered** — Connections screen, Model studio with the
> model assistant and plain-language corrections, AI settings and usage, Home, Ask (SSE, planner, clarifications,
> grounded narrative) and the dashboard viewer with definitions and drill, on starter dashboards built from the
> pack. Remaining in P1: EPIC-03 (gold examples, evaluation suite — needs PRE-05/DEC-11) and EPIC-SP (spikes,
> need PRE-03).
>
> **Deviations (EXEC-004):** starter dashboards are a minimal read-side content model (dashboards, tabs, widgets,
> publications) that P2 authoring extends; intent routing and planning are rules-first, with the LLM planner only
> when the rules find no measure (the local 3B model rarely returns valid specs or model patches, so the rules
> carry the assistant too); Ask turns run as in-process background tasks; the connection form creates and tests
> in one step.
>
> **Deviations (EXEC-003):** model versions are JSON snapshots materialised into the normalised tables on
> approval, and entities name source objects rather than FK them; schema drift returns `schema_changed` (409)
> per model column, not `signature_invalid`; introspection runs inline, not on the worker queue; compilation
> and filter options stay in the API (the adapter contract has no `compile`/`distinct_values`); calculated
> health is a pack calculator, not a SQL measure; the runner uses the OpenBao dev token locally.

### EPIC-QE Query engine and connector runner (shared path)

#### US-017 · Query Spec contract package · M — `[x]` done
As the agents and query engine, we want one typed Query Spec, so that the LLM proposes specs and code compiles them (ADR-001).
Refs: technical_document §Query Spec; `packages/query-spec`.
- [x] Pydantic models + JSON Schema: `spec_version`, `model_version_id`, `connection_id`, `measure`, `extra_measures`, `breakdowns`, `time` (grain, range), `filters`, `personal_fields`, `sort`, `limit`, `compare`, `visual`, `follow_page_filters`.
- [x] Generated TypeScript types for the web app (`apps/web/src/api/querySpec.ts`; CI diffs them).
- [x] Spec versioning and migration hook for stored specs (`migrate_spec`).

#### US-018 · Spec validator · M — `[x]` done
As the query engine, I want to validate every spec against the pinned model version, so that unknown or hidden references never reach a source.
Refs: RULE-003, RULE-006, RULE-008, RULE-014; REQ-049.
- [x] Every `ref` exists in the pinned model version; breakdowns reachable via approved links; `limit` ≤ cap; filters on fields visible to the principal.
- [x] Filter values validated against scoped distinct values for enum-like dimensions (S30 enum-fabrication lesson; ≤ 200 distinct values).
- [x] One connection per widget (`one_database_per_widget`, TEST-063).
- [x] User-readable rejection reasons; Admin-only detail (`admin_detail` stripped for non-Admins).

#### US-019 · SQL compiler (sqlglot) · L — `[x]` done (postgres; mysql, tsql, duckdb verified by the dialect parity suite in EXEC-024)
As the query engine, I want to compile specs to dialect SQL as expression trees, so that queries are correct and injection-proof.
Refs: technical_document §SQL compilation; RULE-012, 013, 014, 023. Acceptance: **TEST-081, TEST-082, TEST-083**.
- [x] `SqlCompiler(dialect)` for `postgres` first (then `mysql`, `tsql`, `duckdb` in P3); bound parameters only.
- [x] Root entity from the measure; joins only on approved (accepted) `model_links`.
- [x] Fan-out protection: distinct root keys in a `tela_base` CTE for every 1:N path (S27 example: 1,000 not 3,000).
- [~] Currency-safe aggregation: group by currency or filter to one *(FX conversion when a model defines an FX table)*.
- [x] Personal fields compile to `CASE` (groupings RULE-012, bands RULE-013), tested on PostgreSQL.
- [x] Time grain bucketing and relative ranges using the server as-of date.

#### US-020 · Scope injector and field removal · L · FEAT-003 — `[x]` done
As a security architect, I want every governed table wrapped in a scoped derived table, so that no query can escape the viewer's scope.
Refs: REQ-004, REQ-005, NFR-002, NFR-003; RULE-004, RULE-005; ADR-009. Acceptance: **TEST-003, TEST-004, TEST-073, TEST-074, TEST-076**.
- [x] `ScopeResolver.for_principal(p, connection_id)` from scope policies; unresolved attribute → `scope_unresolved` (MSG-008), audited.
- [x] `ScopeInjector`: `FROM t` → `FROM (SELECT <visible cols> FROM t WHERE <scope>) AS t`; filters ANDed after scope.
- [x] Re-parse and `assert_scoped` — any unscoped governed reference fails closed; CTE names may not shadow governed tables (TEST-072); security audit event.
- [x] Hidden-field handling: widgets using a hidden field return `hidden: true` with "Hidden for your role" and run no query; hidden columns dropped from scoped tables; `mask` policies show `••••`.
- [x] Client-supplied scope parameters ignored and stripped.
- [x] Locked scope summary text returned with every result.

#### US-021 · Limits, as-of and statement signing · M — `[x]` done
As the platform, I want every execution capped, timestamped and signed, so that sources are protected and the runner only executes approved statements.
Refs: RULE-006, RULE-018; technical_document §Execution envelope. Acceptance: **TEST-088, TEST-089, TEST-090**.
- [x] Inject `LIMIT cap+1` (`TOP` for T-SQL); `truncated` flag; 10,000 rows / 20 s defaults, configurable lower per connection; 10 MiB payload cap.
- [~] `asof.py`: live = query time; cached = fill time; source-declared time or freshness column override. *(Excel/CSV: the pinned file version's upload time, EXEC-024.)*
- [x] `statement_sha256`; Ed25519-signed envelope `{execution_id, tenant, connection, statement_sha256, scope_hash, limits, exp}` plus allowed objects and column checksums.
- [x] Record `query_executions` with SQL hash and row count, never row values (statement text off by default).

#### US-022 · Single scoped execution path · M — `[~]` path done; surfaces join as they are built
As a security architect, I want one `execute(spec, viewer)` entry point used by every surface, so that scope cannot be bypassed (REC-004, RISK-001).
Acceptance: **TEST-075, TEST-061** (completed as surfaces are added).
- [x] `QueryService.execute(spec, principal, context)` is the only caller of the runner's execute operation. *(Custom SQL with FEAT-031.)*
- [x] Static check in CI: runner client imported only by the query module (`test_single_path.py`).
- [~] Same path for filter-value lookups, previews, prechecks (approval dry runs) and scope-preview counts. *(Widgets, chat, drill, export, custom SQL join as they are built.)*

#### US-023 · Connector runner service · L — `[x]` done (signed token + envelope; mTLS with Helm)
As the platform, I want an isolated runner that executes signed read-only tasks, so that only one component can reach customer sources.
Refs: COMP-005; technical_document §Connector implementation; API-062.
- [x] Internal API (signed token + Ed25519 envelope): `/test`, `/introspect`, `/execute`, `/cancel`. *(mTLS with Helm, US-121.)*
- [x] Verify signature, hash and expiry; reject column-checksum drift (`schema_changed`, see deviations).
- [x] Credentials fetched from the secrets store at execution time, with the runner's own read-only identity (US-007, EXEC-027).
- [~] Per-connection pools (1–5), idle timeout 5 minutes, pre-ping on checkout. *(Warm-up at start needs a persisted recent-use list.)*
- [x] Per-connection concurrency cap (4), cancel on client abort (NFR-026).
- [x] Network: only the runner joins the `sources` network (plus `app` for the API and OpenBao).

#### US-024 · Adapter contract and PostgreSQL adapter · L · FEAT-005 — `[x]` done
As the platform, I want a pluggable adapter contract with a first PostgreSQL implementation, so that new engines are added without changing the engine (ADR-002, NFR-025).
Refs: RULE-001; REQ-010, REQ-011, REQ-012. Acceptance: **TEST-007, TEST-077**.
- [x] `ConnectorAdapter` protocol: `test_connection`, `verify_read_only`, `introspect`, `capabilities`, `object_columns`, `execute`, `cancel`. *(Compile and distinct values live in the API engine so scope is always injected there.)*
- [x] PostgreSQL (psycopg 3): no superuser/createrole/createdb/replication/bypassrls in any member role; no INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE/TRIGGER/REFERENCES or CREATE; `default_transaction_read_only = on`; read-only transactions; `statement_timeout`.
- [x] Read-only verdict cached ≤ 60 s plus session read-only flag on every checkout; forced re-check on test.
- [x] Connector contract test suite (reused by every later adapter) running against live PostgreSQL.

#### US-025 · Result post-processing and masking · S — `[x]` done
As a security architect, I want results masked before they leave the runner, so that secrets and personal data are not exposed.
Refs: RULE-020.
- [x] Secret-named columns → `[REDACTED]`; e-mails → `n***@domain`; phones → last 4 digits; Luhn cards → `[REDACTED_PAYMENT_DATA]`; model field tags override heuristics.
- [x] Typed columns, rows, truncation flag, as-of stamp in the result envelope.

#### US-026 · Scope-keyed result cache · M · FEAT-050 — `[x]` done
As a viewer, I want fast repeated loads that are never shared across scopes, so that dashboards are quick and safe (ADR-010, RISK-015).
Acceptance: **TEST-059, TEST-073**.
- [x] Key = `sha256(tenant | connection | model_version | statement_hash | scope_hash | page_filter_hash | as_of_bucket)`; value in Redis with TTL (default 900 s).
- [x] `result_cache_index` metadata only; invalidation on model approval, scope or field policy change, connection edit, manual refresh.

#### US-027 · Security test suites (validator and scope) · L — `[x]` done (matrix grows with each surface)
As the team, I want adversarial suites in CI, so that the core guarantees are proven continuously.
Acceptance: **TEST-071 – TEST-076, TEST-061**.
- [x] Injection corpus (561 statements: stacked queries, comments, UNION to system tables, `SELECT INTO`, `pg_sleep`, `WAITFOR`, homoglyphs) × 4 dialects; hostile filter values through the API.
- [~] Scope isolation matrix: 4 personas × run, filter values, catalogue, scope preview. *(Chat, widgets, drill, export join with their surfaces.)*
- [x] Hypothesis property tests for the scope injector and compiler.

### EPIC-02 Connections and data model (part 1)

#### US-028 · Connection management API · M · FEAT-005 — `[x]` done
As an Admin, I want to create, test, edit, disable and delete connections, so that sources are linked read-only.
Refs: REQ-009, REQ-012 – REQ-014; API-007, API-008, API-009; DATA-011. Acceptance: **TEST-006, TEST-007**.
- [x] `POST/GET /connections` (secrets write-only, `secret_ref` presence only); status `draft` until a test passes.
- [x] `POST /connections/{id}/test` → reachability, server version, latency, read-only verdict with reasons (MSG-002/003/004); rate limit 10/min.
- [x] `PATCH` (If-Match) with mandatory re-test on credential change; `disable`/`enable` (re-test); delete refused while an approved model depends on it (A6).
- [~] Connection state machine aligned with DB enumeration (PRE-06). *(Periodic background check → `error` is a worker follow-up; failed re-tests already move to `error`.)*
- [x] Audit `connection.created/tested/updated/disabled/enabled/deleted/introspected`; cache invalidation on change.
- [x] **ENH-003 done (EXEC-035):** PostgreSQL, MySQL and SQL Server through an SSH tunnel (bastion). Key file (.pem, .ppk v2/v3, OpenSSH; optional passphrase) or password; the key is normalised to OpenSSH and kept only in the connection's OpenBao secret; the descriptor shows key type and fingerprint. The SSH host key is pinned by the first passing test and checked before any password is sent; "trust the new host key" re-pins. Runner `tunnel.py` (asyncssh); TLS still checked against the real database host. Tests: `test_sshkeys.py`, `test_ssh_tunnel.py`, `test_connections_ssh.py`, vitest "SSH tunnel (ENH-003)". Verified live through an OpenSSH bastion on the dev stack. Commit `30c1054`.
- [x] **ENH-004 done (EXEC-036):** SSH key library (Admin → Data → SSH keys; `conn.ssh_keys`, vault `tenants/<t>/ssh_keys/<id>`, migration 0020; rotation sends users to draft, delete refused while used); up to 3 jump hosts (library key or password, own host check); OpenSSH user certificates on keys and host-CA checking per hop; MongoDB (one host) through SSH. Verified live through two OpenSSH servers. Tests: `test_ssh_key_library.py`, `test_ssh_tunnel.py`, `test_sshkeys.py`, vitest "SSH key library, jump hosts, certificates and MongoDB (ENH-004)".
- [ ] **ENH-005 (follow-up to ENH-004, proposed):** uploaded keys on jump hosts; reminders before a key's certificate expires; MongoDB replica sets through SSH.
- [x] **BUG-003 fixed (EXEC-036):** Read schema on a connection with no model said "Columns the model uses changed": the API returns `model_broken` as a list and the web treated an empty list as true. Now only a non-empty list warns, and it names the entities. Tests: vitest "Read schema warns only when the model broke (BUG-003)" and the add-connection flow.

#### US-057 · Ground-truth evaluation suite · L · FEAT-015 — `[~]` done; gold set is a draft for STK-008 and multi-turn cases wait
As the product owner, I want an evaluation suite, so that answer accuracy is objective before UAT (REC-010).
Depends: PRE-05, DEC-11.
- [~] Curate the PMO gold set with STK-008, including look-alike pairs, multi-turn, date-field, enum and fan-out cases. *(Draft of 30 in `packs/pmo/gold_examples.yaml` — look-alikes, synonyms, date fields, enums/value labels, finance, demand gates, fan-out, top-N, trend — loaded as suggestions with "Load PMO draft set". STK-008 review outstanding; multi-turn cases need conversation fixtures.)*
- [x] `POST /gold-examples/evaluate` and `eval_gold_set` nightly job; pass rate by tag; spec diff for failures. *(Runs are queued and run by the worker (`index` queue) through the same planner as Ask; pass rate, recall@8 (TEST-092) and per-tag rates stored in `rag.eval_runs`; diffs per result. Nightly at 02:45 UTC as the Admin who last ran it by hand.)*
- [x] Run on every prompt, model or provider change; warn on model approval if below threshold. *(Model approval queues a run as the approver after indexing; below the tenant threshold (DEC-11 working position 0.80, editable) Admins are notified — warn only. A provider or model change in AI settings queues a run too; prompt changes ship with deploys and are covered nightly.)*
- First baseline on the draft set (rules planner, synthetic TPG): **18 / 31 (58 %)**, recall@8 100 %; weakest tags look_alike 33 %, synonym 0 %, finance 25 %. Follow-up: planner accuracy work against these failures.
- [x] Planner accuracy against the suite (EXEC-023, branch `p2-advisor`): **30 / 30 live** through the worker (was 18 / 30); held-out paraphrases 23 / 24 (was 17 / 24). Generic labels match weakly, synonyms and keys scored on their own, the measure's own words are not filters or ranges, top-N subjects become breakdowns, filters keep stored values, values load first for dimensions the question names. Guarded by `test_planner_gold_unit.py`. Open: "late" → overdue is a pack vocabulary gap for STK-008.

#### US-150 · Colour palettes generated from the uploaded logo · M · FEAT-058 — `[x]` done (EXEC-038)
As an Admin, after uploading a logo I want a few colour palettes made from it to choose from, so that the app matches our brand without designing tokens by hand.
- [x] Extract the logo's dominant colours (ignore transparent/near-white/near-black pixels) and build 3–5 candidate palettes: accent, accent-hover, focus, brand-* tokens, chart categorical order, sequential and diverging ramps — for light and dark themes. *(The browser reads the logo (`features/admin/branding/extract.ts`: canvas at ≤ 96 px, so PNG, JPEG and SVG alike; similar shades merged, < 1 % dropped) and sends up to 12 colours as seeds to `POST /api/v1/admin/branding/palettes`; `platform/palettes.py` builds accent, accent-strong, accent-soft, on-accent, focus, chart-1…8, seq-1…5 and div-* for both themes; one lead per distinct logo hue plus deep / soft / vivid versions, up to 5. The logo colours themselves are kept as `brand` (up to 6) for brand moments. With no logo the Tela logo's colours are used; a greys-only logo gets none and says so.)*
- [x] Keep the US-132 rules: every text pair ≥ 4.5:1, control edges/focus/axes ≥ 3:1 in both themes; chart slots CVD-safe (adjacent ΔE ≥ 15); red and green never used as accent or series (they mean RAG status); adjust lightness to pass, and drop candidates that cannot. *(28 checks per palette in `validate()`: 7 text pairs and 2 focus pairs per theme, chart marks ≥ 3:1, adjacent ΔE ≥ 15 for normal vision and for protanopia/deuteranopia, diverging poles ≥ 15, no red/green. ΔE is OKLab distance × 100 with Machado 2009 CVD simulation, which reproduces the recorded US-132 figures (16.6 / 16.2, 19.3 / 19.1) exactly. Red logo colours become orange, green become teal, with a note; chart slots are found by a search over hues outside red and green, logo colours first. Lightness walks until text passes. Any accent hue/lightness/chroma tested gives a passing palette.)*
- [x] Palette picker on the Branding screen: swatches, a preview (buttons, badges, a KPI card, a chart) in light and dark, contrast check shown per palette; option to keep the default Tela palette or tweak the accent with a colour input (re-validated). *(`PaletteCard.tsx`: "From the logo" swatches, radio list (Tela default + candidates + your colour) with swatches and "n of 28 checks pass", preview with the palette as `--tl-*` overrides on a light and a dark scope, notes and a checks table; colour input → "Try this colour" (a red or green accent is refused with the reason). Saving sends only the spec (`PUT /api/v1/admin/branding/palette`); the server rebuilds and re-checks it, stores it in `ops.tenant_branding.palette`, audits it and returns it in `GET /branding` (checks and notes for Admins only). Reset also clears it. The palette is stored but not yet applied to the app — that is US-151.)*
- [x] Tests: unit tests for extraction and the contrast/CVD validator; a logo with only green/red yields safe palettes; vitest for picking and previewing. *(`test_palettes_unit.py` 113 (WCAG reference values, OKLCH round trip, US-132 figures reproduced, Tela palette passes, each rule caught, 96 accents across the hue wheel, Tela logo, four red/green-only logos, greys, distinct candidates); `test_branding_api.py` +7 (candidates, save rebuilt from spec, viewer/public see it, 409, reset, audit, custom accent adjusted, red/green refused, input checks, 403); vitest `branding.test.tsx` +7 (extraction ×3, pick/preview/save, own accent and red refused, greys only, back to Tela); live `e2e/branding.spec.ts` palette test.)*

#### ENH-007 · PMO Starter Pack 2.0 and dashboard-building knowledge · FEAT-059 *(new, requested 2026-10-27; planned in EXEC-040)*
Three asks from the user: (1) the Starter pack can only be applied while adding a connection — once a model is built the option is gone; (2) build the initial starter pack (dashboard design and the metrics on each) from the PMO report catalogue; (3) teach the agents to build dashboards and widgets the way the PMO reference does.
Sources, copied into the repo: [reference/pmo_dashboard/PMO_REPORT_SCREENS_CATALOG.md](reference/pmo_dashboard/PMO_REPORT_SCREENS_CATALOG.md) (39 screens: Overview, Executive, Governance, Delivery, Finance, Flow, Pipeline; KPIs, visuals and populations per screen) and [reference/pmo_dashboard/DASHBOARD_AND_WIDGET_BUILDER_SKILL.md](reference/pmo_dashboard/DASHBOARD_AND_WIDGET_BUILDER_SKILL.md) (10 rules, visual catalogue, report recipe, acceptance checklist).
**Translation rule:** both files describe the older PMO-UpdatedLatest React/FastAPI/MySQL app (`ReportFrame`, `KpiCard`, `useReport`, `/api/executive` …). Tela takes their *content* — metrics, populations, rules, layouts, wording — and builds it as pack measures, starter reports and dashboards, chart specs and knowledge. Their component names, file paths and endpoints are not copied.

| Reference concept | Tela equivalent |
|---|---|
| Metric (certified rule) → Widget → Report → Dashboard → Publication | certified measure → widget → report (canvas) → dashboard (tabs) → publish to roles — same 5 layers |
| `KpiCard`, `Donut`, `BarList`, `PairedBarList`, `Columns`, `HealthBars`, `TrendChart`, `DataTable` | visuals `figure`, `donut`, `ranked_bar`, `paired_bars`, `columns`, `stacked_status`, `trend`, `table` |
| `HeatGrid`, `Meter` | visuals `heat_grid`, `meter` (US-157, done) |
| `Callout`, `Footnote`, `AsOf` | report text blocks and reporting date → US-158 |
| Global 6 filters (cio, region, portfolio, project manager, priority, platform) | starter dashboard shared filters (RULE-005) |
| `asOf` from `/api/meta` | the pack's reporting date (install question "Set a fixed reporting date") |

- [x] **BUG-004 (2026-10-27) fixed by US-153 (EXEC-041); real-TPG matching follows in US-154/155:** "Starter pack" disappears once a connection has a model. Cause: Connections shows the action only when `!c.model.id` (`ConnectionsPage.tsx`) and the Add-connection wizard says "This connection already has a model" (`AddConnectionPage.tsx`); the API `POST /starter-packs/{id}/install` on an existing model *overwrites* the whole draft definition (Admin's own entities, fields and edits lost), and `GET /starter-packs/match` scores only physical column names, so a model built from real Dataverse names (TPG v2) never matches. Fixed by US-153 and US-154.

- [x] **BUG-010 (2026-10-29) fixed in EXEC-093:** the starter pack dialog grew past the screen with a large pack (pmo_tpg 2.0.0 on Dataverse), so its title and "Add to the model draft" were out of reach; its `max-w-3xl` never applied (the base `sm:max-w-lg` won). `ApplyPackDialog` now fits the viewport (up to 96rem wide), header and footer stay in view, and on large screens the mapping (left) and "What applying does" with clashes and unavailable items (right) scroll side by side. "Match the pack to your fields" rows line up in columns: Pack field · Type · How found · Source column (`PackMappingStep`).

- [x] **BUG-011 (2026-10-29) fixed in EXEC-094:** approving the PMO model on a Dataverse / SQL Server source failed with "declared date but the source column is datetime" for ten Projects date fields. The pack declares them `date`, the source keeps them as `datetime`, and only date → datetime was allowed. Approval now records each field's source type (`source_type`); a date field over a date-time column is read as its day (SQL: `CAST(col AS DATE)`; Dataverse Web API: [day, day + 1)), so the approval check accepts the pair. Time zones of "user local" date-times are not handled yet.

- [x] **BUG-012 (2026-10-29) fixed in EXEC-096:** model approval on a real Dataverse (TDS) failed its dry run. The TDS endpoint drops empty-string parameters, so the four budget-code measures (`<> ''`) "could not run"; and it can drop a new session on its first statement (08S01), so the first queries after a runner restart were "not reachable". The runner now writes empty strings as `N''` for Dataverse and retries a dropped session once on a new connection.

- [x] **BUG-013 (2026-10-30) fixed in EXEC-097:** a Dataverse table the Tela application user cannot read (missing `prvRead…` privilege, e.g. Project Life Cycle `new_projectlifecycle`) failed approval as "The data source could not run this query". The runner now names the table and says the role needs Read on it, or the table should be left out of the model.
  - **Prevented (EXEC-098):** reading the schema now checks each Dataverse table's Read access (migration 0040 `connection_objects.readable`). Unreadable tables are listed on the Read schema step with what to grant, never become entities or count measures (Build model, starter packs, model assistant), and approval names them before its dry run.

- [x] **BUG-014 (2026-10-30) fixed in EXEC-100:** a reopened Ask conversation showed an old "Which did you mean?" question whose turn had already ended, and choosing a reading did nothing (the API answered 409 "This question is not waiting for an answer", which the page swallowed). The turn stream stopped at its first `clarify` event, so what followed it (the answer, or an error such as `scope_unresolved`) was never replayed. The stream now goes past a clarify the person has answered; the page drops the buttons once later events arrive and says when a choice is refused ("… Ask it again to get an answer.").

- [x] **BUG-015 (2026-10-30) fixed in EXEC-101:** Ask answered "What is the overall health of the project portfolio?" with "I couldn't find a certified definition with that name." Every question starting "What is/are …" was routed as a definition request, so data questions such as "What is the approved budget for open projects?" (a gold example) never reached the planner and either failed or returned a measure's rule instead of its figure. A bare "what is X" is now a definition only when X has no determiner or scope ("the", "total", "overall", "for", "by", "this year" …); "what does X mean", "how is X calculated", "define X" and "what is the definition of X" still are. A bare "what is X" that names no certified measure is answered with data instead of failing.

- [x] **BUG-016 (2026-11-03) fixed in EXEC-125:** model approval on the local TPG MySQL model (`tpg_syn`) was blocked by US-199's "Each source column stands for one field" for 26 pairs (Status and Record status on `record_status`, Issue and Issue name on `issue_name`, Project name and Project on `project` in seven tables …). The model was discovered from the source first (fields named after their columns), then the pack was applied; the merge (`packs/apply.py`, US-153) matched fields by key only, so each pack field was added beside the discovered one on the same column. A model field under another key on a pack field's column is now folded into the pack's field (its description kept, display fields re-pointed) unless the model's own measures, dimensions, links or keys use it; then both stay and the preview names the pair (`same_column`, approval still asks for a fix). The preview lists folded fields (`folded`); re-applying the pack cleans a draft that already holds both. The local draft was cleaned the same way (26 folded, none in use; draft revision 5 → 6). *(Pack updates, `packs/updates.py`, still merge fields by key.)*
- [x] **BUG-024 (2026-11-06) fixed in EXEC-167:** the Dataverse pack (0.1.0, built from the Dataverse model) could not be installed on the Dataverse connection: ~270 blocking "both read one column" messages. BUG-021's label-column rule moved each lookup field (`createdby`, `ownerid`, `tpg_project` …) onto its `<column>name` column, which the pack also has as a field of its own. `label_column` now leaves a column that another field of the pack's table names as its own; the PMO pack's lookups still read their labels. *`packs/mapping.py`, `packs/manifest.py`; `test_mapping_guards_unit.py` +1.*
- [x] **BUG-025 (2026-11-08) fixed in EXEC-174:** Ask added filters nobody asked for, so answers looked right but counted the wrong rows: "Calculated health of open projects … schedule 35% … cost 25% (estimate vs approved budget) … % complete … Green, Amber, Red" answered "Open projects: 0" with Risk category Schedule, PCR change type Cost, Closure approval Approved, Current state Completed and the three colours as filters; "Approved budget value and cost total …" filtered PCR change type Cost. Of 30 dev Ask turns, the extra-filter cases were the largest group of wrong answers. A value of a child table or a yes/no value now filters only when the question names its field or table; a value inside another measure's label, a filter on every value of a field and a loose link on a field the question lists are left out, each said in "Read as"; partial value links skip number words and short synonym words. Live: the health question has no filters (Open projects 103), the budget question keeps CAPEX only. *`ask/planner.py`, `ask/orchestrator.py`, `knowledge/values.py`; new `test_extra_filters_unit.py` (13); paraphrase floors raised 33→34 / 35→36, confident-wrong-measure ceiling 7→8 (one wrong measure no longer hidden by a guessed filter).*
- [x] **BUG-026 (2026-11-08) fixed in EXEC-175:** Ask ignored "not", "no", "without", "excluding" and answered the opposite: "Count the projects with no budget code by CIO" → "… has the most with a budget code (45 of 86)"; "not in KSA", "excluding CAPEX" filtered on KSA and CAPEX; "not on hold" counted projects on hold; "other than Active" filtered Active and Platform "Legacy / Other"; "non-CAPEX" was refused. Negated values now filter with `!=` / `not_in` (and the values listed after them); a negated measure is its base count without the rule's values when the rule is listed values on one field, and is otherwise refused with the reason instead of answered the opposite way. *`ask/planner.py`; new `test_negation_unit.py` (13); `test_extra_filters_unit.py` −1 case (now refused).*
- [x] **BUG-027 (2026-11-09) fixed in EXEC-176:** with Gemini (`gemini-3.5-flash-lite`) as planner every AG-03 plan failed "The answer was not valid JSON" and Ask said "Say which records to count …" (run 01a1befd, "How many projects (any state) are past the Plan stage …"). Gemini answered valid `PlannerOut` JSON inside a ```` ```json … ``` ```` fence; hosted providers are not held to the schema (`llm_constrained_providers` = `ollama`, their strict modes refuse the recursive `PlannerOut`), and the gateway passed the raw text to `json.loads`; the repair turn could not show Gemini what was wrong, so all three tries failed the same way. The gateway now reads JSON inside one surrounding code fence before parsing and the schema check. *`llm-gateway/main.py` `unfence`; `test_gateway.py` +9. Same run: "at least one" linked to Platform Network One, and the measure should be Past planning (`baselining_in_scope`) — not fixed here.*
- [x] **ENH-026 follow-up (2026-11-11) fixed in EXEC-180:** with LLM review on and qwen3:8b on Ollama, AG-03's review of the rules' plan mostly fell back to the rules (13 of 30 dev reviews ended "the review took too long" at the 60 s review timeout), so the review rarely changed an answer. The review was sent as task `plan`, the one task that runs with qwen3 thinking; its ~4k-token prompt took 21–76 s with thinking and 5–16 s without, same verdicts on the replayed prompts (12.6–14.2 s against 23.8–57.8 s through the rebuilt gateway). The review is now its own gateway task `plan_review` (planner model, no thinking); AG-03 planning keeps thinking. *`ask/orchestrator.py` `_review_plan`; gateway `settings.py` `llm_allowed_tasks`, `tenant_settings.py` `ROLE_BY_TASK`; `test_gateway.py`, `test_ask_review_api.py`, `test_gateway_tasks_unit.py`. Live (review switch turned back on): 7 AG-03 reviews 3.6–45 s, no time-out; it confirmed `reported_amber_pct` for "How many projects are reported Amber?", a rules miss it used to correct with thinking.*

- [x] **BUG-017 (2026-11-03) fixed in EXEC-126:** asking Ask for a chart or a table gave its default visual (one figure for "how many …"). The rules planner read a visual only as "as a / in a <visual>", so "show me a table of …", "bar chart of …", "pie chart of …", "… in table format", "… by status table", "tabular" and plain "chart" / "graph" were ignored; "chart", "graph", "tabular" also counted as unknown words; the model, knowledge and recipe planners never read visual words. `ask/planner.py` `requested_visual` now reads them all (a named visual, a table, or any chart; "in line with" is not a line chart); "a chart" is one that suits the answer (`chart_for`; one figure stays a figure). Applied in the rules plan, follow-ups (`refine.py`, "now as a table", "make it a pie chart") and, for every other planner, in the orchestrator when the plan has no visual.

- [x] **BUG-018 (2026-11-04) fixed in EXEC-132:** after updating a connection (e.g. adding Dataverse tables) and reading the schema again, the new tables did not show up. The wizard's "Read schema" and the Connections list's "Read schema" refreshed only the connection list, so the schema step kept showing the old table list and starter pack match; and a connection that already has a model had no way to bring a new table into it (`POST /models` answers "already has a model"; approval only allows the model's own tables). Reading the schema now refreshes the objects, the pack match and preview, and the model draft. The draft lists `unmodelled_objects` (readable tables no entity uses); Model studio shows them under "Tables not in the model" and `POST /models/{id}/draft/objects` (If-Match) adds the chosen ones as the baseline would (one entity each, a count measure, foreign-key links to tables already in the model, questions), leaving the Admin's entities untouched. The schema step says how many new tables are not in the model yet.
- [x] **BUG-028 (2026-11-11) fixed in EXEC-179:** after columns were added to Dataverse tables and the connection was updated, tested and its schema read again, the new columns never reached the model or Ask. The schema read did see them (audit 06:46: 35 tables, 33 changed, 0 added; the catalogue held 48 columns on 13 tables that no field read, e.g. 14 in `tpg_projectrequests`, 7 in `tpg_project`), but BUG-018 only gave new *tables* a way into an existing model; new columns on tables already in the model had none. The draft now lists `unmodelled_columns` (per entity: catalogue columns no field reads, matched without case); Model studio shows them under "Columns not in the model" (grouped by table, select a table or single columns) and `POST /models/{id}/draft/columns` (If-Match, `{"columns": {entity: [column, …]}}`) adds the chosen ones as baseline fields (type, personal-data and currency questions, numbered key when taken), leaving the Admin's fields, measures and links untouched; approval then records them. The schema step says how many tables changed and where to add their new columns. New Dataverse *tables* still have to be added to the connection's table list first (by design: Dataverse reads only the listed tables). *`model/catalog.py` `_field`/`unmodelled_columns`/`extend_columns`; `model/router.py`; `ModelStudioPage.tsx` `NewColumns`; `AddConnectionPage.tsx`; new `test_bug028_new_columns_unit.py` (3); `test_connections_model_api.py` +1.*
- [x] **BUG-029 (2026-11-11) fixed in EXEC-182:** after adding the new columns (BUG-028), approving the Dataverse model failed with "measure:new_projectlifecycle_count: The source structure changed since the model was approved …". The draft keeps each entity's `source_checksum` from the approved version, which covers the columns the entity read then; the approval dry run sent that old checksum with the entity's new column list, so the runner saw drift on every entity whose fields changed (added, removed or remapped), not only after BUG-028. Approval now checksums the columns the draft reads against the last schema read before the dry run (the same checksum the freeze stores), so real drift since that read is still caught. *`model/router.py` `approve_draft`; `test_connections_model_api.py` BUG-028 test now approves (fails on the old code with this message on every projects measure).*
- [x] **BUG-033 (2026-11-12) fixed in EXEC-209:** In the Aurora layout the Branding screen's bar colours did nothing: the shell always drew Aurora's sidebar and top bar as Standard (ENH-027 let the canvas design win over US-209). Aurora's sidebar now takes the company's sidebar colour (Standard, Tinted, Brand or Dark; the Aurora CSS already draws the icon chips for Brand and Dark). The top bar stays on the Aurora look, as asked, and the Bar colours card says so. *`AppShell.tsx`; `NavColorsCard.tsx` note; Vitest `branding` +1.*
- [x] **BUG-031 (2026-11-12) fixed in EXEC-205:** Direct SQL "How many unique business units? and show there list" (agent run `01a1ce49`) failed with "The SQL passed the check but could not be run". AG-10 wrote `SELECT DISTINCT dbo.tpg_assignment.owningbusinessunit … FROM dbo.tpg_assignment`. Every check passed, but the scope injector replaces the table with `(SELECT … ) AS tpg_assignment`, so `dbo.tpg_assignment.owningbusinessunit` named a table no longer in the FROM, and Dataverse (SQL Server) refused it (SQLSTATE 42000, "multi-part identifier could not be bound"). The agreed-term hints AG-10 gets are themselves written as `schema.table.column`. Custom SQL now points a schema-qualified column at its table's alias before the checks (a table used under two aliases is left alone), so Custom SQL, Direct SQL and the drafter all run it. *`query/custom_sql.py` `_columns_to_aliases`; `test_authoring_unit.py` +2.*
- [x] **BUG-032 (2026-11-12) fixed in EXEC-205:** Direct SQL "For open projects … count projects for every region and colour pair, as a heatmap" (agent run `01a1ce59`) answered "No rows match". AG-10 wrote `… WHERE tpg_totalactual_state IN (1, 2, 3) GROUP BY tpgmea_region, tpg_totalactual_state`: `tpg_totalactual_state` is the Dataverse roll-up calculation status of `tpg_totalactual`, not a colour, and the values were invented. The right columns (`tpg_overallkpiname`, `tpg_projectstatusname`, `tpgmea_regionname`) were among the 256 of 296 columns the prompt left out: "kpi" did not match the run-together name, "state" did not match "status", and code columns took places their text twins should have had. Dev's TPG connection also had no pack applied (model v1: 35 automatic "Number of …" measures, 0 breakdowns, no agreed terms), so nothing told AG-10 what "open" or "KPI colour" means. Two fixes: (1) AG-10's column list (`content/sql_context.py`) lists a Dataverse code column with a text twin once, as its text ("text of tpg_overallkpi"; the yomi twin goes too), ranks question words found inside run-together names and plain English variants (state/status, colour/color), and puts roll-up helpers (`x_state`, `x_date`) last with a note. On the real dev model the question now lists all three right columns, and `_state` helpers drop out. (2) A Direct SQL answer with no rows looks up the values its WHERE clause filters on, through the same scoped path (only values the person may see; at most 3 columns, two small queries each). Values the data doesn't hold go back to AG-10 once with the column's real values; the new SQL is checked and run like the first. If that repair fails, the card keeps "No rows match" and says which values matched nothing (`value_notes`). The badge now reads "SQL repaired after a failed check". *`ask/direct_sql.py` `filters`/`missing_values`/`_value_repair`; `DirectSqlAnswer.tsx`; `test_direct_sql_unit.py` +11, `test_direct_sql_api.py` +3, `test_sql_context_unit.py` +1, Vitest `us257b-direct-sql` +1.* *(Still open: applying the PMO or Dataverse pack to the dev TPG connection. That is what gives AG-10 the business fields and agreed terms such as "open".)*
- [x] **BUG-030 (2026-11-11) fixed in EXEC-187:** Model studio's assistant timed out on "Add more dimensions" (agent run `01a1cbb5`, 110 s, `llm_timeout`, fallback). The rules didn't read the phrase, so AG-04 on qwen3:8b got the whole Dataverse draft: every field of every entity (35 entities, 2,569 fields, ≈ 121k characters plus a 14k-character schema). The AG-04 prompts now leave out platform bookkeeping columns (audit, ownership ids, `*yominame`, `*_base`, `address*_`, `versionnumber` …) unless the draft uses them or the Admin names them, list the named tables first and stop at a 16k-character budget (the rest by name and field count): 18.5k characters on the real draft. The rules now read "add/propose/suggest (more) dimensions|measures [to <table>]" (the rules' proposals of that kind, status and groupings before dates) and "add dimension <field>" / "add <entity.field> as a dimension" (a question when the field is on several tables), and skip Dataverse lookup ids when their `…name` column exists. *`model/assistant.py` `_draft_text`/`_catalog_text`/`rule_proposals`/`parse_correction`; new `test_model_assist_prompt_unit.py` (6).*
- [x] **BUG-019 (2026-11-04) fixed in EXEC-132:** changing a widget's chart type on the report canvas showed the old chart until the page was reloaded. The canvas saved the change but the widget's run was cached by widget id (`["widget", id, …]`), and the chart is drawn from the run. After every canvas change, widgets whose chart type, query, title or filters changed are refetched (`changedWidgets`, also for Top N, breakdowns, tone, suggestions and Undo).
- [x] **BUG-020 (2026-11-04) fixed in EXEC-132:** a widget placed on two reports (the library adds the same widget again) changed on both when it was edited on one. Edits from a report (title, chart type, filters, Top N, breakdown, tone, suggestion) now go to a copy that only that report item uses when the widget is placed anywhere else (`reports.item_widget`); Undo puts the item back on the shared widget. Editing from the widget library still changes it everywhere. The copy keeps no definition hash when another of the owner's widgets has it (the library keeps one widget per definition). The rename dialog says other reports keep their title.
- [x] **BUG-021 (2026-11-04) fixed in EXEC-132:** mapping the PMO pack, a yes/no, number or long-text column could not be chosen for a text pack field (e.g. Dataverse `tpgmea_keypriorityproject`, a yes/no, for "Key priority project"): the column list showed only columns of a fitting type and the API refused other types. The column list now shows every column; any column can stand for a text field (`packs/mapping.py` `can_map`), while number, date and yes/no fields still need a fitting column (listed, but not selectable, with the reason). A Dataverse yes/no or choice picked by hand reads its label column (`<column>name`) when there is one; otherwise queries read the column as text (`ModelField.reads_text`): yes/no as "Yes" / "No" (what the pack's rules expect), numbers and dates cast to text. *(Dataverse Web API mode without a label column is not covered: it reads the raw value.)*
- [x] **BUG-022 (2026-11-05) fixed in EXEC-137:** on the report canvas, after changing a widget's chart type once, opening "Chart type" again showed only greyed-out types. The menu lists all 27 chart types with nothing capping its height; when it opened upwards the top of the list (the types that fit) ran off the screen and could not be scrolled to, so only the unsuitable types at the bottom showed. The API's list was right every time (traced on the Dataverse model). Every dropdown menu now fits the room on screen and scrolls (`max-h-(--radix-dropdown-menu-content-available-height) overflow-y-auto`, 8 px collision padding), as Select already did.
- [x] **BUG-023 (2026-11-05) fixed in EXEC-137:** dates and date-times on dashboards showed as stored ("2025-01-13T00:00:00"). `formatDate` (`components/format.ts`) shows an ISO date in words, "Jan 13 2025", with the time only when it is not midnight ("Jan 13 2025, 14:30"), read as written (no time-zone shift). `formatValue` uses it for text, so tables (DataTable default cells, ResultTable, DetailTable), chart axes and labels (ECharts, Recharts, Plot) and the heat grid all show dates in words; highlights match on the same words and clicks still find their row by index. Sorting still uses the ISO value. *(Exports keep their own formats.)*

#### US-153 · Apply a starter pack to a connection that already has a model · M · FEAT-059 / FEAT-046 — `[x]` done (EXEC-041; fixes BUG-004)
As an Admin, I want to add the starter pack after I have built the model myself, so that I get the certified PMO measures, reports and dashboards without starting over.
- [x] Entry points: Connections row action "Starter pack" for every connection that is not a draft (not only without a model); Add-connection "Read schema" step offers "Add starter pack" when the connection has a model; Model studio header "Apply starter pack"; Admin → Starter content "Apply a starter pack to a connection" (connection picker, marks "has a model"). All open one shared dialog (`features/admin/starter/ApplyPackDialog.tsx`).
- [x] Preview first: `POST /api/v1/starter-packs/{pack_id}/preview {connection_id}` (`model.approve`; writes nothing) → counts added (tables, fields, measures, dimensions, links), the model's own items kept, clashes with both definitions, "same table" notes, starter reports available / unavailable and the unavailable items with their gaps; "already applied here" when it is the installed pack.
- [x] Apply *merges* into the model draft (`packs/apply.py`, pure, used by preview and install): pack items the model lacks are added; the Admin's own entities, fields, measures, dimensions and labels stay; same-column fields take the pack's governance attributes with the Admin's wording (also under another key, BUG-016: folded into the pack's field unless the model uses it); a clash (same key, different definition — field source/type, measure, dimension, entity on another table) takes the pack's certified form unless the Admin picks "Keep mine" (`keep_mine` refs); pack items that need a field or entity the Admin kept are left out and become Unavailable "(kept as yours)". Open model questions are kept (no longer dismissed), the reporting-date question is not asked twice, the merged draft is validated like a draft edit (`model_invalid` with issues), `installed_pack_id` set; approval builds the starter reports and dashboards as before. Blocked (409) while a pack update waits for approval. *(Not done: "keep both with a new key" — keeping both would need the pack's reports re-pointed; the Admin keeps theirs or takes the pack's.)*
- [x] Re-apply allowed through the same preview (adds only what is missing); audit `starter_pack.installed` with `mode: new|merge` and each clash's choice.
- [x] Tests: `test_pack_apply_unit.py` (5: whole pack on an empty model, own items and wording kept, clash pack vs keep mine, kept field drops dependent measures and reports, entity on another table / same table noted); `test_pmo_pack_api.py::test_pack_applied_to_a_model_the_admin_built` (preview writes nothing, 403 for creators, merge keeps the Admin's measure and questions, audit mode, re-apply adds nothing, approval builds 11 starter reports); vitest "offers the starter pack on a connection that already has a model and merges it (BUG-004, US-153)"; live `e2e/starter-pack-apply.spec.ts` (read-only).
- [x] **Choose the pack (user, 2026-11-06; EXEC-166):** "In the starter pack, admin need to select option for pmo pack for the connection." The apply dialog (Starter content › Preview and apply, and Connections › Starter pack) took the best-scoring released pack on its own, so on Dataverse the Admin could not pick the PMO pack over `dataverse_pack`. It now has a **Starter pack** select listing every released pack with its title, version and fields found, best match first; changing pack starts that pack's preview and saved mapping afresh. A pack with 0% found can still be chosen and mapped. `GET /starter-packs/match` adds each pack's `title`. *`ApplyPackDialog.tsx`; Vitest "lets the Admin choose the PMO pack when another pack matches the connection better".*
- Finding: on the real TPG source (connection "TPG", schema `dataverse`) pack 1.2.0 matches 18 % of its fields (`tpg_projectrequests` and the `tpgmea_*` column names are unknown to it), so the dialog says "No starter pack matches this source" there. Fixed by US-154 (mapping) and US-155 (pack 2.0 bound to the real names, D1).

#### US-154 · Map the pack to the model when names differ · M · FEAT-059 — `[x]` done (EXEC-042)
As an Admin, I want to tell Tela which of my fields a pack field means, so that a source with different column names (e.g. the real TPG Dataverse) can still use the starter pack.
- [x] Matching also uses the model (entity/field labels and synonyms of the Admin's model), Dataverse names — the label column of a choice or lookup (`tpgmea_region` integer → `tpgmea_regionname`, also when only `…name` is exported), a key named `<table>id`, plural tables, publisher prefixes (`tpg_`, `tpgmea_`, `new_`; set aside only on a Dataverse-like catalogue with `statecode`/`versionnumber`, so MySQL's `variant_status` is never "status") — and pack synonyms (`synonyms`/`values` on manifest fields), not only physical column names. Name-based guesses must fit the pack field's type. Confidence and "found by" shown per pack table and field (`packs/mapping.py`, `manifest.match(model=, mapping=)`).
- [x] Mapping step in the preview (`mapping_step` in `POST /starter-packs/{id}/preview`, which now also previews a pack that does not match yet, `matched: false`): for each pack table a source table or "Not in this source"; for each pack field a searchable column picker that lists only columns of a fitting type, or "Not in this source" (items needing it become Unavailable with that gap). Value mapping for status-like fields (fields the pack's rules or dimensions name values for): source "Yellow" → pack "Amber", empty → "Not set"; measure rules are rewritten to the source values (`=` → `in`, blank → `is_null`) and dimensions show the pack's words (`value_labels`). Suggestions for source values come from the approved model's field on the same column. A mapping that does not fit (unknown column, wrong type) → 400 with the issues; install of a pack whose required fields are still unmapped → 409 `pack_not_matched`. Web: `features/admin/starter/PackMappingStep.tsx` in `ApplyPackDialog` (offers the closest pack when none matches), "Map the starter pack" on the Add-connection Read-schema step when a pack is close (≥ 10 % by name).
- [x] Mapping saved per connection and pack key on install (`model.pack_field_mappings`, migration 0023: `mapping` = the Admin's choices, `resolved` = every column the install bound) and reused by `/match`, preview/re-apply and pack updates (US-120 `plan_update` / `switch_pack` bind with resolved + choices).
- [x] Tests: `test_pack_mapping_unit.py` (8: Dataverse names without a mapping, model labels, Admin mapping and not-in-source gaps, mapping check, value rewrite and dimension labels, `=`/`!=`/blank rewrite, prefixes only on Dataverse sources, exact names still win); `test_pmo_pack_api.py::test_pack_mapped_to_a_source_with_other_names` (renamed Dataverse-style copy of the TPG fixture: found-by, preview unmatched, 409, 400 on a wrong type, manual mapping makes `overdue_go_lives`/`schedule_risk` available, "not in this source" gap, saved and reused, `Y → Amber` value map, approval, `schedule_risk` returns the source's count); vitest "maps a pack to a source with other names" (type-filtered picker, value map, install body); live `e2e/starter-pack-apply.spec.ts` on the MySQL "TPG" connection (Status → `record_status`, then matches; read-only).
- Finding (offline, real TPG PostgreSQL `dataverse` schema, Homebrew PG15): pack 1.2.0 now finds 78 % of its fields by itself (was 18 %); still unmatched only because the required planned deployment date has another name — map it (e.g. `tpgmea_productdeploydate`) or wait for pack 2.0 bound to the real names (US-155). Real TPG reports "Yellow" (11 projects), which the pack already shows as Amber.

#### US-155 · PMO Starter Pack 2.0: dashboards and reports from the report catalogue · L · FEAT-059 / FEAT-046 — `[x]` done (EXEC-052)
As a PMO lead, I want the starter dashboards to look and read like the PMO report catalogue, so that every role gets a complete, trustworthy set of reports on day one.
- [x] Pack `pmo_tpg` 2.0.0 (`packs/pmo/manifest.yaml`); existing installs move to it through US-120 (pack updates). *(The starter reports are the report blueprints: `reports_from: report_blueprints.yaml` (blueprints 0.2.0) turns every available blueprint into a starter report and every data-gap blueprint into an unavailable report at load time (`packs/manifest.py::blueprint_reports`), so the knowledge the agents read (US-159) and the starter screens are one source. Bound first to the real TPG names (D1): current state `tpg_projectstatusname`, request state `tpg_requeststatename`, planned go-live `tpgmea_productdeploydate`, project number `tpgmea_pprojectnumber`, key priority, PCR counter, table `tpg_projectrequests`. `min_confidence` 0.05 (the pack has 151 fields now). Pack updates from 1.3.1 are an ordinary US-120 update: 1.x reports are "removed" (D2).)*
- [x] Two starter dashboards with the catalogue's sections as tabs, shared filters CIO, Region, Portfolio, Project manager, Priority, Platform (requests: no Portfolio — callout when it is set):
  - **PMO — Projects** (26 tabs): Overview · CIO & Portfolio · Portfolio status (calculated health) · Lifecycle · Strategic initiatives · **ExCo priorities** · Delivery heatmap · Regional portfolio · Portfolio health · Baselining · Budget code · PM assignment · **PCR compliance** · On-hold aging · **Closure** · **Status reporting** · Upcoming go-lives · Delayed projects · **Stage completion** · Schedule variance · Update compliance · **Critical tasks** · **RAID summary** · **Resource utilisation** · CAPEX budget vs forecast · Flow metrics.

  *(Requests' filters: CIO, region, delivery manager, priority, platform. The Requests Overview carries a standing callout "No portfolio on requests" instead of a conditional one, since the dashboard has no Portfolio filter to set.)*
- [x] Every report follows the catalogue layout: KPI strip (4–5 figures, tone + definition tip) → visuals on the 12-column grid (6+6 or 12) → detail table (sortable, searchable, export) → footnote with population and data limits. KPIs, visuals and populations per report exactly as listed in the catalogue §3 and the summary matrix §5. *(`content/starters.py::catalogue_layout`: the figures split the 12 columns, visuals go 6 + 6 with a lone last one 12 wide, the table spans the width. Tones are blueprint `tones` by measure (DRAFT thresholds: overdue, attention, compliance % with `higher_is_worse: false` …), passed with meter notes into the widget's `visual`. A query takes at most 3 extra measures, so CAPEX cost components are two tables.)*

#### US-160 · Dashboard quality checks from the acceptance checklist · S · FEAT-059 / FEAT-039 — `[ ]`
As an Admin, I want publish to catch the mistakes the checklist lists, so that no dashboard goes out with wrong wording or missing caveats.

Warnings, not blockers, except the finance wording (blocker for certified widgets).

- [ ] Tests: one per check (API), composer shows them (vitest).

**Order:** BUG-004 (US-153 → US-154, both done) first — it unblocks applying any pack to TPG v2. Then US-156 → US-157/158 → US-155 (pack 2.0 needs the measures, visuals and text blocks), with US-159/160 alongside US-155.

**Decisions (user, 2026-10-27):**
- **D1 — Source:** build the screens on the current TPG PostgreSQL source (Homebrew PG15, db/schema `dataverse`, the "TPG v2" connection) and its real Dataverse names; a screen is Unavailable only when that source really lacks the data. Pack 2.0 binds to these names first (US-154 mapping stays for other sources).
- **D2 — Replace:** Starter Pack 2.0 *replaces* 1.x. Installs move through the US-120 pack update; 1.x starter reports that 2.0 drops are retired as "removed" (RULE-017: live dashboards keep working until republished, owners notified, users' copies untouched).
- **D3 — US-155 scope (2026-10-27):** build every "now build" screen (new activity entities) in US-155; a tab shows the widgets the source supports and names the rest (unavailable only when none is left); TPG's actual deployment date stays unbound until STK-008 confirms it (the Admin can map it, US-154); activity rows follow their project's scope through the link (`inherits_scope`).
