#### ENH-008 · Free-hand questions in Ask, by permission, certified on approval · FEAT-060 *(new, requested 2026-10-27; planned in EXEC-045)*
**Problem (2026-10-27).** "How many projects (any state) are past the Plan stage, meaning projects that have at least one stage planned end date and whose S2 plan planned end date is blank or before today?" got *"I couldn't match that to a certified measure"* with the suggestions "Number of action items, availability, business units, cios".
**Cause.** Ask answers only through certified measures (RULE-022). The rules planner (`ask/planner.py::plan_question`) found no measure ("projects" is a stop word). The LLM fallback (AG-03, `orchestrator.py::_llm_plan`) can only return one measure, up to two dimensions and `= / in / != / not_in` filters (`planner.py::spec_from_planner`). It cannot say "any of six date fields is set", "blank or before today", or compare a raw date field to today, so it had no valid plan. The suggestions are `src.measures[:5]`, the first five measures alphabetically, not the closest ones. The measure expression language can already express most of this rule (`all` / `any`, `is_null` / `not_null`, `<`, `other_field`; see the "Past planning" measure in `packs/pmo/manifest.yaml`), but it has no `today`, and Ask cannot build such a rule on the fly. Note: a fix put into the pack must use the expression format, never SQL.

**What the user asked for.**
1. An Admin screen to turn on free-hand questions **per user**. Only an Admin can give access.
2. A user with access to the database (connection) can ask free-hand questions there. Adding a free-hand answer as a widget sends it to an Admin to approve and certify.

**Decisions (user, 2026-10-27):**
- **D1 — Meaning of "past Plan":** the certified **Past planning** measure (lifecycle phase past Planning) stays the official meaning. A free-hand rule with another meaning (e.g. stage dates) is shown next to the certified number, never under the same name. On approval it is certified under a different name or rejected in favour of the existing measure.
- **D2 — What approval creates:** both a **certified widget** and a **reusable certified measure** in the model (the user's question kept as a synonym), so everyone's Ask can use it afterwards, with or without free-hand access.

**Rules for the free-hand path.**
- Certified first: when a certified measure answers the question, Ask uses it; free-hand runs only when nothing certified fits.
- Never SQL: the planner returns a structured rule (the measure expression format), checked by the validator (allow-list, scope, hidden fields, limits) and compiled by the same engine as certified measures.
- Never more data than the person already has: their connection access, scope policies (rows) and field policies (hidden columns) always apply.
- Always labelled **Not certified · built from your question**, with the rule in plain words. Free-hand answers and pending widgets are kept off dashboards, reports, publishing and exports until certified.
- Every grant, removal, free-hand run, request and decision is audited.

**Today.** Permissions come only from roles (Admin / Creator / Viewer, `migrations/versions/0002_iam.py`) through IdP group mappings; there is no per-user grant. Every Creator has `customsql.use`. Custom SQL (FEAT-031, US-079/080) and certification (EPIC-08, `certification.request` / `certification.decide`) exist and are reused here.

#### US-161 · Per-user permission grants · M · FEAT-060 — `[x]`
As an Admin, I want to give one permission to one person on one connection, so that free-hand questions are not tied to a role.

- [x] New table for per-user grants: tenant, user, permission, connection, reason, granted by, granted at, expires at, removed at / by. RLS by tenant like the other `iam` tables. Migration.
- [x] Effective permissions = role permissions + live grants (not expired, not removed). The check for `ask.freehand` is always per connection. Effective Access (`features/admin/access/EffectiveAccess.tsx`) shows grants and where they come from.
- [x] Expiry: a grant ends at its expiry date with no job needed (checked at use); a reminder notification 7 days before.
- [x] A user removed by SCIM / the directory loses their grants.
- [x] Audit: `permission.granted`, `permission.removed`, `permission.expired`.
- [x] API: `GET/POST/DELETE /api/v1/admin/permission-grants` (`mappings.manage`); a grant to a connection the user cannot see is refused with a clear message.
- [x] Tests: grant, expiry, removal, wrong tenant, connection the user cannot see, SCIM removal, Effective Access.

The Ask button itself is US-165.)*

#### US-163 · Ad-hoc rules in the query spec · L · FEAT-060 — `[x]` *(EXEC-053, merged into master `3343b70`)*
As the engine, I want a question's own rule (count / sum / average over one entity with a condition) to be a checked query spec, so that free-hand answers use the same safe path as certified ones.
- [x] Query spec gains an ad-hoc measure: `{"adhoc": {"entity", "agg", "field"?, "where": Condition, "label", "plain_rule"}}` in place of `measure.ref`, using the existing expression types (`all` / `any`, compare, `in` / `not_in`, `is_null` / `not_null`, `other_field`). *(`tela_query_spec.spec.AdhocMeasure`; the ref is always `measure:_adhoc`, so every reader of `measure.ref` keeps working; main measure only; a model can't store one (`Measure.status` `adhoc` refused in a snapshot).)*
- [x] New value **today** in expressions (`{"today": true}`, optional day offset), fixed once per run in the tenant's time zone, also usable by certified measures. The result cache key includes the date. *(`expr.Today`; `query/asof.py::pin_dates` turns it into the reporting date shifted by the gap, so every compiler reads only `asOf`; literal dates in an ad-hoc rule become shifts the same way; compilers refuse an unpinned today. Time zone = the person's, as for the reporting date (no tenant time zone exists).)*
- [x] Validator: allowed only with a live `ask.freehand` grant on that connection; fields must be in the model and not hidden for the person; scope policies applied; limits on condition depth, number of fields, number of OR branches; no joins beyond the model's links. *(`SpecValidator(freehand=…)`; 403 `forbidden` without a grant (Admins too) or with a hidden field, never naming it; limits 8 fields, depth 4, 12 alternatives; plain comparisons only (no calculators); value types checked; scope is injected exactly as for certified measures.)*
- [x] Compiler: reuses the certified measure compilation for PostgreSQL, MySQL, SQL Server, DuckDB, Dataverse and MongoDB; parity tests for the new parts (`today`, nested `any` inside `all`). *(4 ad-hoc shapes added to `test_dialect_parity.py::cases`: match PostgreSQL on every engine in the conformance run.)*
- [x] The run carries `certified: false` and is audited as `query.freehand`. *(`badge: "not_certified"`; refusals audited `deny`; each run calls `grants.mark_used()` (Last used on the Free-hand screen).)*
- [x] Tests: unit (validator refusals, today), dialect parity, scope / hidden field suites with an ad-hoc measure. *(`test_adhoc_rules_unit.py` 12, no DB, includes the problem question vs Python; `test_freehand_query_api.py` 5: no grant, scope per CIO, removed/expired grant, hidden field, values never SQL.)*

#### US-164 · Free-hand planner (AG-03 v2) · L · FEAT-060 / FEAT-018 — `[x]` *(EXEC-054, branch `enh008-us164-166`)*
As a user with free-hand access, I want Ask to turn my words into a rule over the model's fields, so that I get an answer when no certified measure fits.
- [x] When the person has `ask.freehand` on the connection, AG-03 gets the model's fields (entity, label, type, synonyms, a few in-scope sample values; hidden fields left out) and approved glossary knowledge, and may return either a certified measure (preferred) or an ad-hoc rule with a plain-words reading. Output checked against the schema; anything invalid → refused, never repaired by guessing. *(`ask/freehand.py`: `fields_context`, `AG03_FREEHAND`, `PlannerOut.adhoc` → `rule_from_planner` (schema, visible fields, model fields); the plain-words reading is always worked out from the rule itself, never the model's own text.)*
- [x] Rules planner: a generic measure plus unknown words hands over to AG-03 instead of ending in a refusal; everyday phrases mapped: "blank / empty / not set / missing" → is blank, "before today / overdue / in the past" → earlier than today, "after today / upcoming" → later than today, "at least one of" → any. *(A deterministic free-hand reader runs first — `build_rule`: the table, aggregate (count / total / average / highest / lowest), conditions joined by and, "or" between predicates, "at least one / all / none of" over a field group, dates (today, ±N days, literal dates), numbers, in-scope text values, "by X" breakdowns; a field phrase must name one field with all its words, a tie is refused with both names; AG-03 v2 only when the words don't make a rule.)*
- [x] Glossary: business terms as approved knowledge (e.g. "S2" = Plan stage, the stage end-date fields), reviewed by the Admin (FEAT-016 flow). *(`packs/pmo/glossary.yaml` (draft: S2, EAC, TCV, PCR, PM, DM; kept only when the model has the fields) indexed with each model version as a `glossary` document **pending review**, keeping an unchanged glossary's review; Ask reads approved glossary chunks of any source — "TERM means: …" / "TERM: …" lines.)*
- [x] Same-meaning check (D1): when a certified measure has a similar name or synonym ("Past planning"), Ask answers with it and, if the user gave another rule ("meaning …"), shows the free-hand version beside it. *(`choose`: certified first unless rule words remain once the certified name's words are taken out; "meaning / i.e. / defined as" + a same-meaning measure → certified answer with the rule beside it; a free-hand answer shows the same-meaning certified measure beside it.)*
- [x] Tests: offline planner tests for the problem question and 10 more; the gold set keeps its current score (30/30 on the old set). *(`test_freehand_planner_unit.py` 22, no DB: the problem question (D1, rule, DuckDB number vs Python), 11 more questions run on DuckDB vs Python, gold set + paraphrases unchanged with free-hand on, refusals (unknown/hidden/ambiguous field, S2 without the glossary), AG-03 v2 output checks, chip edit.)*

#### ENH-009 · Dashboard groups in the sidebar · FEAT-061 *(new, requested 2026-10-28; planned in EXEC-055; US-169 built in EXEC-056; US-170, US-171 in EXEC-057)*
**Fix done first (2026-10-28, EXEC-055, commit `845fe80`).** On "PMO — Projects" the dashboard ran off the right edge (fourth KPI card and the charts cut off), the tab labels wrapped onto two lines and "On hold" was cut off. **Cause:** the viewer's page grid had an `auto` column, so the 18-tab strip's full width set the width of the whole page and `overflow-x-auto` on the strip never took effect; tab triggers have a fixed height but could wrap. **Fix:** `DashboardPage` / `DashboardBody` grids are `grid-cols-1 min-w-0` (the strip now scrolls sideways inside the 1280 px column); `TabsTrigger` (`components/ui/tabs.tsx`) is `shrink-0 whitespace-nowrap` for every tab strip. Web type check clean, vitest 219/219.

**What the user asked for.**
1. Admins and Creators can put dashboards into **groups** (e.g. "Delivery", "Finance", "ExCo").
2. Each group shows in the sidebar as a menu that opens to list its dashboards.
3. Viewers see the same groups in their sidebar.

**Working positions (confirm or replace):**
- **W1 — Groups are shared across the tenant**, not personal: one set of groups that everyone sees.
- **W2 — Who manages:** a new permission `dashboards.group` on the Admin and Creator system roles. A Creator can rename, reorder and delete the groups they created and add any dashboard they can read; an Admin can manage every group.
- **W3 — Groups never widen access.** A person sees a dashboard in a group only if they could already open it (published to their role, shared with them, their own draft for Creators, starter content). A group with nothing visible to that person is hidden from their sidebar. Group names are not secret.
- **W4 — A dashboard can be in more than one group.** Order of groups and of dashboards inside a group is set by hand (drag or up/down buttons).
- **W5 — Sidebar placement:** a new **Dashboards** section between Explore and Build, one collapsible item per group (open/closed remembered per person). Dashboards not in any group stay where they are today (Home, Shared with me, Build → Dashboards). The collapsed rail and the mobile drawer show the groups too.

**Today.** The sidebar is fixed (`app/shell/nav.ts` `NAV`, `GROUPS`, `Sidebar.tsx`). What a reader can open is `LIVE_VERSIONS` in `content/access.py` (published to their role) plus `content.shares` and ownership; `GET /api/v1/dashboards` lists only own/shared user dashboards. Latest migration is `0028`.

#### US-169 · Dashboard groups: data and API · M · FEAT-061 — `[x]` *(EXEC-056, branch `enh009-dashboard-groups`)*
As an Admin or Creator, I want to create groups and put dashboards in them, so that people can find related dashboards together.
- [x] Permission `dashboards.group` in `identity/permissions.py`; added to the Admin and Creator system roles (migration). *(Effective Access labels it "group dashboards".)*
- [x] Tables `content.dashboard_groups` (tenant, name unique per tenant ignoring case and deleted groups, icon, position, created by/at, updated at, deleted at) and `content.dashboard_group_items` (group, dashboard, position, added by/at; primary key on the pair). RLS by tenant; deleting a dashboard row removes its items, a soft-deleted dashboard is left out of every list. Migration `0029_dashboard_groups.py` (up/down/up checked).
- [x] API (`content/groups.py`): `GET/POST /api/v1/dashboard-groups`, `PATCH/DELETE …/{id}` (rename, icon or `clear_icon`; delete is soft and frees the name), `PUT …/{id}/items` (ordered dashboard ids), `PUT /api/v1/dashboard-groups/order` (every group once). Writes need `dashboards.group`; a Creator changes only their own groups (403 otherwise), an Admin (`dashboards.publish`) any group, and **only an Admin reorders groups** (the order is everyone's). Adding a dashboard the caller cannot open is a 404 that names nothing. Items the editor cannot open are reported only as `hidden_count` and kept, after the editor's items, when they save.
- [x] Also `PUT /api/v1/dashboards/{id}/groups` for "Add to group…" (US-170): the dashboard ends up in exactly the listed groups among those the caller may change; other groups untouched.
- [x] `GET /api/v1/me/dashboard-groups` (`dashboards.view`): each group with only the dashboards the caller can open — published to one of their roles or to all (starter dashboards an Admin hid from them excepted), their own drafts, drafts shared with them, any draft for an Admin — with `link` (published view first, else `/draft`); empty groups left out.
- [x] Audit: `dashboard_group.created`, `.updated`, `.deleted`, `.items_changed`, `.reordered`.
- [x] OpenAPI snapshot regenerated (additions only); web types `DashboardGroup`, `MyDashboardGroup`, `DashboardGroupLink`, `DashboardGroupsResponse` in `api/authoring.ts` (hand-written, as for the other content types).
- [x] Tests: `tests/test_dashboard_groups_api.py` (6): create/rename/reorder/delete and name clashes; Creator cannot edit another's group or reorder, Admin can, Viewer cannot manage; a Viewer sees only dashboards published to them or shared with them, empty groups hidden, unpublish/delete drops out; hidden items kept with `hidden_count`; "Add to group…"; other tenant through RLS; audit rows.

#### ENH-010 · Scope policies for many entities and roles at once · FEAT-002 *(new, requested 2026-10-28; built in EXEC-059, merged into master)*
**What the user asked for.** The New scope policy form on Admin → Access → Scope policies saved one entity for one role at a time; Admins need to give access to **many tables at once** and **for many users at once**.

**Working positions:**
- **W1 — "Users" means roles.** Scope policies attach to roles (people get rows through their roles), so the form takes several roles. Per-person row policies are not added.
- **W2 — Same rule for every pair.** The chosen condition (or All rows) and the scope-chip text are saved as one policy per entity and role pair, so each stays separately editable, deactivatable and deletable in the table.
- **W3 — All or nothing; no copies.** Every entity and the condition's field on each must be in the approved model, or nothing is saved (one error names every problem). A pair that already has an identical policy (same rows, active or not) is skipped and reported.

As an Admin, I want to give several roles access to several entities in one step, so that setting up a connection does not take one form per table.

The single `POST /scope-policies` is unchanged (shares `_insert_policy`).

Without an approved model, entities are typed comma separated.

#### Tela Ask improvement plan · ENH-011 – ENH-014 · FEAT-062 – FEAT-065 *(new, 2026-10-28; from "Tela Ask — Improvement Plan: Retrieval, Query Skills, Accuracy and Insights", @Bright)*
**Renumbered.** The plan proposed ENH-010 and US-172 – US-187; ENH-010 and US-172 were already taken (bulk scope policies), so the plan's items are ENH-011 – ENH-014 and US-173 – US-188 here (plan US-n = US-n+1). FEAT-062 – FEAT-065 are proposals.

**Aim.** Keep Tela's certified-first, never-SQL design, and close the gap with the reference (an LLM that writes raw SQL) by making the Query Spec more expressive, retrieval more precise and the answer card richer. AG-03 does not get raw SQL: the spec learns the analytic shapes people ask for, and one governed SQL escape hatch (AG-10) covers the rest.

**Limits found today** (`reference/retrived.md`, this file):
- AG-03 returns one measure and up to two dimensions with `=`, `in`, `!=`, `not_in` filters. Ratios, top N per group, running totals, period comparisons and share of total are out of reach.
- Retrieval has no reranker; `ts_rank_cd` has no word rarity (US-159: "Heatmap of region by reported status" ranks second); RRF takes the top 8 with no relevance floor.
- Dimension values are not retrieved: "KSA", "CAPEX" or a PM's name must match stored spellings; no fuzzy value index.
- Few-shots are the top 3 gold examples; the AG-03 prompt has no conversation history, no column profiles and no confidence field.
- AG-08 writes one headline sentence: no drivers, outliers, concentration, trend breaks or data-quality notes.
- The chart catalogue is narrow for PMO work: no waterfall, dumbbell, bullet, Pareto, Gantt, small multiples, funnel or distribution views.

**Taken from the reference:** conversation-aware retrieval query (rewrite the follow-up into a standalone question); column profile stats in the prompt (fill rate, top in-scope values); self-correction loop, applied to specs (typed repair prompt); `confidence` / `reasoning_summary` / clarification in `PlannerOut` (low confidence → AG-02); per-user verbosity (assume-first, balanced, ask-freely) moving the clarification threshold. **Kept out of Ask:** LLM-written SQL, except through AG-10 + `CustomSqlValidator`, labelled Not certified (US-180). **Already better:** hybrid search + RRF stays, with a reranker on top.

**Decision (2026-10-28): D3 joins ECharts** for the gallery visuals ECharts draws poorly (ggplot2 extensions gallery and D3 gallery as the source; ggplot2 is R, so its ideas are ported to D3 in the browser, not run). Amends ADR-007 and DEC-22 ("no second chart engine"): ECharts stays the default; every visual in either engine comes from `tela_chart_spec`, uses the same tokens, has a table view and print/Excel export, and gets a RULE-008 suitability row before AG-07 may suggest it. Engine per visual: **D3** for UpSet, beeswarm (`d3-force`), ridgeline / raincloud, time-to-event, bump and dumbbell; **ECharts** for waterfall, bullet, Pareto, XmR, Gantt, calendar heatmap, sankey, funnel, small multiples and treemap; **HTML** for waffle.

**Order of work (measure first, then teach the spec new shapes, then extend):**
- **Wave 1 · Measure and link:** US-184 gold set 2.0, US-173 value index, US-174 reranker and weighting, US-175 query rewriting, US-185 insight engine (needs no planner change; visible value early).
- **Gate A:** baseline measured on the 150+ question gold set.
- **Wave 2 · Express and check:** US-176 multi-measure and ratios, US-177 window operations, US-178 period compare and cohort, US-181 linking and spec repair, US-182 voting and confidence, US-186 narrator and card v2.
- **Gate B:** parity green on six engines; accuracy at the DEC-11 threshold.
  - **Parity: green (EXEC-090, 2026-10-29, merged into master)** — 15/15 on PostgreSQL, MySQL, SQL Server, Excel (DuckDB), MongoDB and Dataverse (TDS, OData, FetchXML). Fixed: T-SQL `AVG` cast to float (integer day counts were truncated: `slip_vs_baseline_avg`, `avg_health_score`); Dataverse Web API `filled` field profile; MongoDB and the Web API refuse US-178's grained `compare` as Unavailable instead of answering the current window alone. Explained, not a code fix: on Dataverse the pack binds no column for three optional health inputs (percent complete, actual start, issues KPI), so the health score there uses fewer factors by design; the parity reference now leaves the same inputs out.
  - **Accuracy at the DEC-11 threshold: not measured yet** (needs a live model run on gold set 2.0).
  - **Fixed (EXEC-092):** US-178's grained `compare` now matches each earlier period to its counterpart (BUG-006), and a figure compare with an empty earlier period compares with 0 or says why (BUG-005); `% change` keeps one decimal on MySQL / SQL Server.
- **Wave 3 · Extend:** US-179 multi-step plans and recipes, US-180 governed SQL hand-off, US-183 sanity checks and feedback, US-187 visuals wave 1, US-188 visuals wave 2.

**Success targets (proposed; confirm with STK-008 alongside DEC-11):**

| Metric | Today | Target |
|---|---|---|
| Execution accuracy on gold set 2.0 | not measured; spec match 54/54 on the draft set | ≥ 90 %, or the DEC-11 threshold |
| Held-out paraphrase accuracy | 23/23 | ≥ 85 % on a new held-out set of 50 |
| Value-linking accuracy (alias set) | not measured | ≥ 95 % |
| Knowledge retrieval quality | recall@8 100 % on the draft set | MRR ≥ 0.80, nDCG@8 ≥ 0.85 |
| "Couldn't match" rate on real questions | not measured | half the wave-1 rate after wave 2 |
| Answers flagged wrong by users | no feedback yet | < 3 % |
| Breakdown and trend answers with a verified insight | 0 % | ≥ 70 % |

#### ENH-011 · Retrieval 2.0 · FEAT-062
Most planner misses in the gold runs (look-alike, synonym, finance tags) are linking failures, not reasoning failures; a value index plus a reranker is the biggest single gain.

#### US-173 · Dimension value index · M · FEAT-062 / FEAT-014 — `[x]` *(EXEC-065, branch `enh011-us173-value-index`)*
As a user, I want my words for values ("KSA", "capex") matched to stored values, so that filters are right first time.
- [x] Table `rag.dimension_values` (tenant, connection, model version, dimension ref, stored value, normalised value, synonyms, embedding); migration and RLS; refreshed on `model.version_approved` and nightly; scope applied at query time (only values the person can see). *(Migration **0033**: `rag.dimension_values` (+ display `label`, trigram, synonym and HNSW indexes), `rag.value_synonyms`, `pg_trgm`, `rag.f_value_tenants()`, surface `value_index`. `knowledge/values.py::refresh_values` reads each text dimension's distinct values through `QueryService.index_values` (every row, recorded as the approver; at most `TELA_VALUE_INDEX_MAX_VALUES` 1000, more = free text, skipped); computed, temporal, `sensitive`, identifier, e-mail/phone fields are not indexed; person names are indexed but never embedded; unchanged values keep their vectors; a dimension the source can't read now keeps its old rows. Outbox `model.version_approved` also queues `tela_worker.tasks.knowledge.refresh_values` → `POST /internal/knowledge/model-versions/{id}/values`; beat `knowledge-values` 02:15 → `/internal/knowledge/values/nightly`. Scope at query time (`Orchestrator._linked`): only dimensions the person sees (no hidden or masked field, entity in scope), and a value only if it is in their scoped value list unless their scope on that entity is every row.)*
- [x] Search with `pg_trgm` similarity plus vector match: "KSA", "Saudi" and "Kingdom of Saudi Arabia" resolve to the stored `Saudi Arabia` before planning. Synonyms from the pack and from clarification choices (Admin-reviewed, FEAT-014 review gate). *(TPG stores `KSA`, so the pack maps "Saudi", "Saudi Arabia", "Kingdom of Saudi Arabia" to `KSA`. Order: exact value or label → approved synonym → a word that is part of only one value ("Lina" → Lina Farouk) → spelling slip (`pg_trgm` finds candidates, an edit distance of 1, or 2 from 8 letters, decides: "Jordon", "Magnatti"; "deployed" is not "Deployment") → meaning for words the model doesn't know (cosine ≥ `TELA_VALUE_LINK_VECTOR_MIN` 0.85). Two candidates in one dimension ask one clarifying question ("By “network” do you mean Platform Network Flex or Network One?"); the conversation remembers the choice and it is proposed as a synonym. `packs/pmo/value_synonyms.yaml` (draft, 20 values) is added as pending review; Admin → Knowledge → **Value synonyms** (`/admin/knowledge/values`) lists, approves/rejects (bulk), adds (approved at once) and has "Try a phrase". API: `GET/POST /knowledge/value-synonyms`, `POST /knowledge/value-synonyms/review`, `GET /knowledge/values`, `POST /knowledge/values/link` (knowledge.manage).)*
- [x] Linked values passed to the rules planner and AG-03 as quoted data, with the stored spelling. *(Rules planner: `plan_question(linked=, choices=)` filters on the stored value, one dimension per phrase (one the question names first: "Omar is the project manager"), a multi-word phrase is not also read word by word ("medium priority" ≠ issues' "Medium"), and says "Interpreted “saudi arabia” as Region: KSA". AG-03: `linked_block` (quoted `<value … asked= stored=/>` lines) and `spec_from_planner` puts copied words back to the stored spelling.)*
- [x] Tests: 50 alias questions ("KSA", "capex", nicknames); value-linking accuracy reported in the eval run. *(Gold set 0.3.0: +50 `alias` examples, held out of few-shots; the eval run reports them as `by_tag.alias` and in `value_linking`. Offline: 50 / 50 (100 %) with the pack synonyms approved, also executed on DuckDB (`test_gold_scoring_unit`). New `test_value_linking_unit.py` (11), `test_value_index_api.py` (5: refresh and review gate, scope, clarification → synonym, Admin synonyms, meaning + nightly), worker `test_value_index_tasks_call_the_api`, vitest `admin-value-synonyms.test.tsx` (2). Live eval on the dev stack not run (stack not migrated to 0033; TPG source unreachable, EXEC-062).)*

#### US-174 · Reranker and lexical weighting · M · FEAT-062 / FEAT-014 — `[x]` *(EXEC-063, branch `enh011-us174-reranker`)*
As the agents, we want the best 8 chunks, not the first 8, so that context is relevant.
- [x] Gateway task `rerank` (local cross-encoder, e.g. `bge-reranker-v2-m3`): RRF top 30 → rerank → top 8 above a score floor; below the floor send fewer chunks rather than noise. Fall back to plain RRF with the existing "limited context" note when the reranker is down. *(Gateway `POST /rerank` → `providers/rerank.py::TeiReranker`, a Hugging Face text-embeddings-inference `/rerank` endpoint (user's choice: TEI container, not torch in the gateway); `TELA_RERANK_URL` unset = off and `scores: null`. Compose service `reranker` (`text-embeddings-inference:cpu-1.8`, `--model-id BAAI/bge-reranker-v2-m3`, profile `ai-local`, amd64 only). API `knowledge/retrieval.py`: fused top `TELA_KNOWLEDGE_RERANK_POOL` (30) → reranker over header + text → top k with score ≥ `TELA_KNOWLEDGE_RERANK_FLOOR` (0.02, sigmoid); no reranker → fused order, no note; reranker down → fused order, `limited_context` and "Limited context: knowledge ranking is simplified right now."; search output gains `reranked`. Not run live: the TEI image and the 2.3 GB model are not pulled on the dev stack.)*
- [x] Lexical ranking: `setweight` on title/term (A), synonyms (B), body (C); optional BM25 (ParadeDB `pg_search`) behind a setting. Fixes the "no word rarity" issue noted in US-159. *(Migration **0032** rebuilds `rag.knowledge_chunks.tsv` from `metadata.term` (A), `metadata.synonyms` (B) and content (C). Retrieval takes the top 100 by weighted `ts_rank_cd` and re-scores them with BM25F (field weights A 3, B 2, C 1; k1 1.2, b 0.75; document frequency of each query lexeme over the chunks the search could return). `TELA_KNOWLEDGE_LEXICAL=pg_search` uses `paradedb.score` over a `bm25 (id, content)` index that migration 0032 creates only when `pg_search` is available. That path is untested because our pgvector image has no `pg_search`; without the extension it logs `knowledge.pg_search_missing` and keeps the weighted path.)*
- [x] Contextual header line on every chunk before embedding (kind, entity, measure, document title, e.g. "Certified measure · Projects · Forecast cost (EAC) — …"); re-embed job on the next model approval. *(`knowledge/index.py`: `contextualise` sets `term` (own, passage heading or document title), `context` and `ctx` = 1 in chunk metadata; `embed_text` = header + text is what is embedded and reranked; stored content and citations unchanged. Chunk builders now set `term`/`entity_label`/`synonyms` (entity: dimension labels and synonyms; measure: synonyms; blueprint: triggers). `recontextualise` runs in the model-approval index call (`recontextualised` in its output): older chunks get the header and new vectors (cleared for the re-embed sweep when embeddings are down).)*
- [x] Tests: MRR and nDCG@8 and per-kind recall added to `rag.eval_runs` (US-057); "Heatmap of region by reported status" ranks first. *(The `rag.eval_runs` columns and their computation are US-184's (`knowledge/scoring.py`, migration 0031, built in parallel by another session), so they are not duplicated here. Live retrieval is reranked, so those eval runs measure it. New: `test_retrieval_rank_unit.py` (6: tsvector parsing, BM25 rarity and title weight, floor, header, reranker off/down/answering, notes); `test_knowledge_api.py::test_dashboard_rules_and_blueprints_retrieved` now needs all 10 first, hybrid and keywords-only (was ≥ 8/10, heatmap second), MRR 1.0 and nDCG@8 1.0; `test_reranker_orders_floors_and_falls_back`; `test_chunks_carry_term_synonyms_and_header`; gateway `test_rerank_scores_with_the_local_cross_encoder_or_says_it_is_off`.)*

- **Live measurement on TPG (EXEC-068):** with the reranker on (arm64 TEI 1.9, bge-reranker-v2-m3, CPU) and a pool of **10** — 30 timed out on CPU (EXEC-066) — retrieval got **worse** than the EXEC-064 baseline (same API image, lexical weighting on, reranker off): recall@8 94.4 % → 90.7 %, MRR 0.80 → 0.61, nDCG@8 0.73 → 0.61, dimension recall 50 % → 44 %; answers unchanged (54 / 54, rules planner). The reranker lowered the expected measure in 24 questions and raised it in 5. Rerank 16 s median per question on CPU. Before turning it on anywhere: check why (score floor dropping relevant chunks, the header/text sent, the pool size) and measure again with pool 30 on a faster machine. **Found (EXEC-069):** the dev stack's knowledge was indexed before US-174 — none of the 301 approved chunks has the contextual header (`context`), `term` or `synonyms`, so the reranker scored bare chunk text and the A/B lexical weights had nothing to weight. Both runs therefore measured US-174 without its indexing half. Re-index (`tela-admin reindex-knowledge`, or the next model approval) before measuring again. Reranker turned off on the dev stack (too slow on CPU for everyday use).

#### US-175 · Query rewriting and typed retrieval · M · FEAT-062 / FEAT-018 — `[x]` *(EXEC-070, branch `enh011-us175-query-rewrite`)*
As a user, I want follow-ups and compound questions understood, so that context carries over.
- [x] Standalone-question rewrite from the last two turns' specs; glossary expansion ("S2" → "Plan stage"); sub-query split for long or compound questions, fused with RRF. Deterministic where possible; a small LLM call only when rules find nothing. *(`ask/rewrite.py`. `Orchestrator.history` reads the conversation's last two turns; the base is the latest with a spec. A message is a follow-up when it starts like one ("and", "what about", "only", "by", "top 3", "excluding"), points back ("same", "those") without naming a measure, or is ≤ 3 content words naming no measure and not asking anew ("how/what/where …", money words). It is applied to the previous spec with Refine's ops (US-060): lead words dropped and a filter cue added ("what about Jordan" → "only Jordan"), linked values (US-173: "and Saudi?" → Region: KSA), one dimension per value (the measure's own table first), a newly named measure keeps the breakdowns, filters and time it can reach ("same for forecast cost"; the words of its name are not values). The answer says "Follow-up on “…”" and each change. Otherwise the message is written out with the previous answer in plain words (`describe`) and planned; only when that is unsupported does one AG-03 call (task `plan`, `REWRITE_SYSTEM` + the conversation) rewrite it, and the rewrite is planned by rules first ("Read as: …"). Glossary terms (approved FEAT-016 glossary, `freehand.expand`) are read as their meaning for retrieval, and for the rules planner when the question as asked can't be planned. A question with "; ", sentence ends, "and also", "as well as", "and what/which/how", or "and" between two named measures (≥ 12 words) is also searched as up to 3 parts, fused by RRF (`retrieval.fuse`); it is still planned as one question. The retrieval event carries `rewrite` (question, how, parts, glossary). Gold multi-turn examples are planned the same way (`gold.plan_messages`).)*
- [x] Typed, budgeted retrieval: per-kind quotas (measures and dimensions 6, glossary 3, values 5, gold examples 5, dashboard rules and chart patterns 2) with MMR inside each quota so near-identical chunks of one document do not crowd out the rest. *(`retrieval.QUOTAS`: model (certified measures, entities, model definitions) 6, glossary 3, reference (uploaded documents, answered questions, blueprints) 3 — a quota the story did not name — and pattern (dashboard rules, chart patterns) 2; from the fused pool of 30 (or the reranked pool), `budget` keeps each group's quota by MMR (λ 0.5; relevance by rank, redundancy by word overlap, two chunks of one document at least 0.5 alike), in overall order. Values 5 = linked values in AG-03's prompt; gold 5 = few-shots (next item). Ask planning uses it (`Orchestrator.retrieve_typed`); definitions, chart suggestions and the Admin search keep the plain top 8.)*
- [x] Gold few-shots 3 → 5, chosen by embedding similarity and spec shape (a "top N per group" question sees a top-N example). *(`gold.pick_shots`: 20 most similar approved examples; shapes `top_n`, `breakdown`, `two_breakdowns`, `trend`, `range`, `filter`, `compare`, `total` read from the question's words and from each example's spec; rank lowered by up to 6 places for the share of the question's shape an example has, and a strong shape the question needs (top N, trend, compare, two breakdowns) gets at least one example when a candidate has it.)*
- [x] AG-03 context gains four blocks, quoted as data like the knowledge block: conversation state (previous spec in plain words), linked values (US-173), field profiles (type, fill rate, top 5 in-scope values, date range) and time context (reporting date, fiscal year start). *(`conversation_block` (`<turn asked= answered=/>`), `linked_block` capped at 5, `profile_block` and `time_block`, all attribute-escaped. Profiles: `QueryService.field_profile` under the person's scope through the one query path (surface `field_profile`, migration **0034**): row count, fill rate (new field-query kinds `filled`, `min`, `top` on SQL and MongoDB; `min` and `top` on Dataverse Web API, which has no `filled`), the 5 most frequent values with counts, earliest–latest for dates; up to 5 dimensions — those of linked values, then those the question names. Values of personal, sensitive or person-name fields are left out (type, fill rate and dates only). Fiscal year: model setting `fiscal_year_start` ("4", "04-01", "April"), January when unset (working position).)*
- [x] Knowledge grows from use: approved "Save as gold" answers, certified free-hand questions (US-166 synonyms) and clarification choices feed new synonyms and gold examples through the FEAT-014 Admin review gate. *(`gold.suggest_example`: a question answered after a clarification (origin `clarification`, not for follow-ups or dashboard context) and a certified free-hand question on its new measure (origin `freehand`) become **suggested** gold examples, Admins notified; duplicates and ad-hoc specs skipped. `gold.answer_synonyms`: when an Admin approves an answer's gold example (or saves one from their own answer), the question's words that linked to a filter value by part, spelling or meaning ("UAE" → GCC - UAE) are proposed as value synonyms (source `answer`, pending review). Clarification value choices already proposed synonyms (US-173). Migration 0034 widens `gold_examples.origin` and `value_synonyms.source`; the gold and synonym screens say where a suggestion came from.)*
- [x] Tests: multi-turn fixtures; follow-up rewrite cases; quota and MMR unit tests. *(New `test_query_rewrite_unit.py` (41: follow-up detection, 6 rewrite cases, reach after a measure swap, words rewrite, base turn, describe, glossary, parts, quotas, MMR, fusion, shapes, few-shot choice, context blocks, fiscal year, profile queries on DuckDB); `test_gold_scoring_unit` multi-turn fixtures 3 → 9 planned the Ask way (execution 100 %); new `test_query_rewrite_api.py` (5: follow-ups in a conversation, one rewrite call, AG-03 context with scope and no person names, clarification → suggested gold, approved answer → synonym); `test_freehand_certification_api` (gold suggestion); MongoDB and Web API profile queries in `test_document_compilers_unit`, parity cases in `test_dialect_parity`; vitest origin label.)*

#### ENH-012 · Query Spec analytics ("SQL skills") · FEAT-063
Raise SQL skill in the compiler, not the LLM: analytic operations on the Query Spec, AG-03 chaining up to three specs, raw SQL only behind AG-10. The LLM picks a shape and its parameters; sqlglot writes the SQL for all six engines, with scope and hidden fields injected as today. Each operation is a typed spec field, validated against the model and compiled per dialect (window functions where the engine has them, a DuckDB post-step for MongoDB and Dataverse Web API). **Safety rules:** every operation is allow-listed in `SpecValidator`; each gets a dialect parity case in `test_dialect_parity.py`; RULE-008 learns which visuals each new shape may use.

#### US-176 · Multi-measure, derived and share-of-total · L · FEAT-063 — `[x]` *(EXEC-074, branch `enh012-us176-multi-measure`)*
As a user, I want several measures, ratios and shares in one answer ("Forecast over budget % by CIO"), so that I don't have to ask twice and divide myself.
- [x] `measures[]` (up to 4, several aggregates in one `GROUP BY`); `derived` (ratio, difference, % of) over two certified measures, divide-by-zero guarded; `share_of_total` (`x / SUM(x) OVER ()`). *(`tela_query_spec.spec`: `measures: [a, b, …]` is read as `measure` + `extra_measures` (max 4; giving both is refused; a measure listed twice is refused). `derived` (max 3) `{key, op, of, to, label?}` with op `ratio`, `difference`, `percent_of` and `percent_change` ((a − b) ÷ b × 100: the "over budget %" the story names), both refs among the answer's measures, never the ad-hoc rule, keys unique and not a measure key. `share_of_total`: measure refs of the answer, added as `<key>__share` (percent, 0–100). Sort by `derived:<key>` / `share:<measure>`. Zero or blank divisor → blank.)*
- [x] Validator, compiler and parity cases on six engines. *(`SpecValidator._extras` → `ValidatedSpec.extras` (`Extra`: label and plain rule from both measure labels, format: ratio decimal, difference the unit, percentages percent); refused: difference or percentage across units, share without a breakdown, share of a non-additive measure (average, percentage). SQL (PostgreSQL, MySQL, SQL Server, DuckDB, Dataverse TDS): the grouped query is wrapped, `SELECT d.*, <derived>, CAST(x AS DOUBLE) / NULLIF(SUM(x) OVER (), 0) * 100 FROM (…) AS tela_d`, so a share's total covers every group before a top N; a CTE moves out; derived keys are quoted ("over" is reserved in T-SQL). MongoDB: `$addFields`, a share's total by `$group`/`$unwind`/`$replaceRoot` (the guard has no `$setWindowFields`). Dataverse Web API: Unavailable (one measure per query already). Result columns of extras carry `of`; `definitions` list them with their plain rule. Parity cases: three derived figures over a money pair by region, four counts + ratio without a breakdown, top 3 by share, shares + ratio by priority and month, fan-out share — equal to PostgreSQL on MySQL, SQL Server, DuckDB (Excel), MongoDB and Dataverse TDS; Unavailable in Dataverse OData/FetchXML (`scripts/conformance.sh`). Four parity tests fail on master too and are not US-176's: SQL Server/TDS averages (`slip_vs_baseline_avg`, `avg_health_score`) and the Web API `filled` field query (US-175).)*
- [x] RULE-008 rows for paired measures and percentages. *(`tela_chart_spec.suitability`: a derived figure or share is a **companion** (`ResultShape.companions`, `ChartSpec.companions`) of the measure it comes from — it labels that series (bars, line points, donut slices; "–" for a blank) and is in the table, never a series; rows count series only. Trend now needs one unit for several lines (reason text updated); paired bars keep "same unit". business_rules.md §4.1 has the new rows; the web chart draws the labels (`Chart.tsx`).)*
- [x] Ask. *(Not in the story's bullets; added so the shapes are reachable.)* Rules planner: `measure_pair` reads "A as % of B", "A over B %", "ratio of A to B", "A to B ratio", "difference between A and B", "A minus B", "A vs/versus/compared to B" and "A and B" (exact names only), both sides certified measures of one entity, same unit for difference and percentages; "share of total", "% of total", "as a share" → `share_of_total` when there is a breakdown and the measure adds up; chips for the extra measure, figure and share. AG-03: `PlannerOut.extra_measures`, `derived`, `share_of_total` and prompt lines; `spec_from_planner` keeps only what the spec rules allow (same entity, certified, units). Gold set **0.4.0**: +9 examples tagged `derived` / `share`; gold compares a derived figure by op, of and to; shapes `derived` and `share` for few-shot choice.
- [x] Tests: new `test_multi_measure_unit.py` (38: spec rules, validator labels/formats/refusals, DuckDB derived vs measure columns, four measures, share over every group before top 3, fan-out share, zero/blank divisor, RULE-008 companions and units, rules planner and AG-03 mapping); `test_dialect_parity` cases (above); `test_pmo_pack_api::test_several_measures_derived_and_shares_reconcile` (finance oracle, shares, `of`, definitions, 422 across units); `test_gold_scoring_unit` oracle check reads the measure column; vitest `visuals.test.tsx` (2: paired bars labelled with the percentage, donut labelled with the share).

#### US-177 · Window operations · L · FEAT-063 — `[x]` *(EXEC-080, branch `enh012-us177-window-ops`)*
As a user, I want rankings, top N per group, running totals and percentiles, so that common analytic questions are answered.
- [x] `top_n_per_group` (`ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY … ORDER BY …)`), `running_total`, `moving_avg`, `rank` / `bucket` (`RANK()` / `NTILE()`), `percentile` (P50, P85, P90; `PERCENTILE_CONT` or an approximate equivalent per engine), `having` (filter on the aggregate). *(`tela_query_spec.spec`: `TopNPerGroup` (by, n 1..50, order), `RunningTotal` (measure?, per?), `MovingAvg` (window 2..24, measure?, per?), `Rank` (measure?, per?, order), `Bucket` (k 2..10, measure?, per?, order), `PercentileOp` (p: p50|p85|p90, measure?, per?), `Having` (measure?, op, value) — all optional fields on `QuerySpec`; validated by `SpecValidator._window_ops`; rejects running_total/moving_avg without a time grain or on a non-additive measure, having without a breakdown. Running total and moving average go along the grain column and, without `per`, restart in each group of the other breakdowns. Percentile is exact everywhere: `quantile_cont` on DuckDB, positions + interpolation (`ROW_NUMBER() - 1`, `COUNT`, `FLOOR`/`CEIL`) on PostgreSQL, MySQL and SQL Server, which have no windowed `PERCENTILE_CONT`. Window orders put empty values last and break ties by the other breakdowns on every engine. Rules planner: "top 3 projects per programme" adds the project name breakdown and drops the answer-wide limit; "programmes with more than 10 …" adds the programme breakdown; a median of a summed figure is taken over the projects in each group.)*
- [x] DuckDB post-step for MongoDB and Dataverse Web API. *(`query/duckdb_post.py`: `build_post_sql` builds DuckDB SQL from the validated spec's window operations; `apply_post` runs it over the fetched rows in an in-memory DuckDB; `MongoCompiled.duckdb_post` and `WebApiCompiled.duckdb_post` carry the post-step; `service.py._run` applies it after row fetch, keeping the source's row order and value types (MongoDB periods stay ISO text).)*
- [x] Parity tests; limits on window size and result rows. *(Limits at parse time by the spec model: n ≤ 50, window ≤ 24, k ≤ 10; result rows stay under the connection row cap (the outer query is limited). Seven parity cases (top 3 projects per programme, running total by month per region, 3-month moving average, rank and quartiles by CIO, P85 forecast per programme, having) equal PostgreSQL on MySQL, SQL Server, DuckDB (Excel), MongoDB and Dataverse TDS (Dataverse OData and FetchXML: Unavailable or equal; 94 shapes ran in each); MongoDB and the Web API through the post-step in the parity harness too. 57 DuckDB unit tests in `test_window_ops_unit.py` (values checked against Python); live `test_window_ops_api.py` (top 3 projects per programme by forecast cost; running total of open projects by month) checked against `pmo_oracle`. Gold 0.6.0 +7 held-out. Refine chip ops `set_running_total`, `set_moving_avg`, `set_rank`, `set_having`, `remove_window`. RULE-008: window columns are companions. The insight engine skips window columns.)*

#### US-179 · Multi-step plans and analytic recipes · M · FEAT-063 / FEAT-014 — `[x]` *(EXEC-091, merged into master, migration **0038**)*
As a user, I want questions that need two queries ("portfolios with above-average slip") and named analyses, so that I get them in one ask.
- [x] `plan` of up to 3 specs, where a later step may reference an earlier step's single value or value list (`{"from_step": 1}`). The orchestrator runs them in order through `QueryService`, each scoped; every step is shown in "How this was worked out". No step carries rows to the LLM. *(`ask/steps.py`: a plan carries its earlier steps in `spec["steps"]` and, on each step that uses one, bindings in `spec["uses"]` (`{"step", "at": "having"|"filter", "ref", "op", "take": "value"|"values"|"mean"|"median"}`); AG-03 writes `{"from_step": n}` inline in a filter's `values` or a `having` `value` (`PlannerOut.steps`, ≤ 2 earlier plans in the same shape; AG-03 prompt says so and that it never sees a step's rows) and `lift` moves it into `uses`, so each step without its bindings is a valid Query Spec: checked step by step (`Orchestrator._plan_errors`, "step 1: …" typed errors), repaired (`repair.precheck` also names a reference to a later or missing step, a list where one value goes, an unused step), described (`rewrite.describe`: "step 1: …; then …, above step 1's average across its rows") and voted on (`gold.comparable` compares steps and bindings). `Orchestrator._steps` runs the earlier steps in order with `QueryService.execute` as the person (surface `ask`, the turn's id), takes from each only what it returned to them (`take`: one row's figure, the first breakdown's members (≤ 500, refused when truncated or hidden), or the mean / median of its figures) and binds it before the next step; a step that can't run ends the turn with `step_failed` / the step's error, naming the step. The final step is answered as any spec (narrated alone; step rows never reach a model); the turn stores the bound final spec. `plan` and `complete` carry `steps` (`text`, `value`, `values_count`, `row_count`); the card lists them in "How this was worked out". Rules: "… above / below (the) average …" with a breakdown named (or a plural field: "portfolios with …") is planned in two steps — the mean of the members for a count or sum, the overall figure for an average or rate — and says "Two steps: …". A reading question (US-182) offers multi-step readings by their plain words and runs the chosen one's steps.)*
- [x] Knowledge kind `analysis_recipe`: named, parameterised spec templates in the pack (variance bridge, Pareto 80/20, aging buckets, funnel by gate, overdue trend, compliance rate) that know their operations, chart and insight checks; AG-03 picks a recipe and fills its slots. Indexed and reviewed like `report_blueprint`. *(`packs/pmo/analysis_recipes.yaml` 0.1.0 (draft for STK-008): six recipes, each with triggers, slots (`measure` / `dimension` / `grain`: options and a default), a spec template (`"{slot}"`), operations (share of total, sort, limit, moving average), chart (waterfall, Pareto, columns, funnel, trend, bullet — US-187) and insight checks (US-185 fact types). `knowledge/guidance.py`: `Recipe` model (template, slots and operations checked), `load_recipes`, `lint_recipes` (CI pack lint), `recipe_for_model`, `recipe_chunks` (one per recipe, labels only, the cut recipe in `metadata.definition`, `measures` for visibility); `index_guidance` builds an `analysis_recipe` document per model version approved with it and retired with older versions, exactly as the blueprints. Migration **0038** adds the source kind. `ask/recipes.py`: approved recipes of the model version only (`Orchestrator._recipes`), cut to what the person can see; the rules pick one by trigger and fill its slots from their plan and the question's words (a field label; else the option sharing a word with the measure, "Time on hold" for Projects on hold; else the default), keeping the question's filters, time range and comparison — never when the question asks for a measure or breakdown the recipe doesn't offer ("PM assignment compliance by lifecycle phase" stays as asked); AG-03 sees `recipes_block` and may answer `recipe: {"id", "slots"}` (`from_planner`; a slot option not listed or not reachable falls back). The recipe's insight checks lead the card (`lead_insights`); `plan` / `complete` carry `recipe`; the card says "Used the analysis “…”". Admin search and the knowledge screen label the kind "Analysis recipe".)*
- [x] Tests: multi-step plans with scope per step; recipe slot filling. *(`tests/test_multistep_recipes_unit.py` (30): lifting and refused references, takes and binding, the above-average rule (count vs rate), AG-03 steps and typed step errors, votes telling multi-step plans apart, pack lint and chunks, slot filling for all six recipes from the rules, operations and the question's filters, slots limited to visible options, recipes never overriding the ask, AG-03 picks, insight order. `tests/test_multistep_recipes_api.py` (6, FakeGateway): above-average runs two scoped steps (the Creator's average is over their own rows, not the Admin's); a Creator limited to the Gulf regions gets only KSA and GCC - UAE from a step-1 value list (the Admin gets all six; each step logged in `ops.query_executions` under one scope hash; no AG-03 call carries the list) with the first round three samples at 0.6; a bad step reference repaired by one single call (0.6 ×3, then none) and a step whose figure can't be used named in the error; disagreeing multi-step samples become a reading question and the chosen reading runs its steps; recipes approved with the model are used and not once back in review; AG-03 picks a recipe from its context. `test_knowledge_api` expects the new kind (6 chunks). Web: `ask-card-v2.test.tsx` lists the recipe and the steps. Full API suite on the branch: 1,064 passed, 14 skipped (parity), 1 xfailed.)*

#### US-181 · Schema linking and spec repair · M · FEAT-064 — `[x]` *(EXEC-078, branch `claude/enh013-us181-linking-repair-913250`, migration 0036 — renumbered at merge after US-183's 0035)*
As the planner, I want a scored candidate list and typed errors, so that I choose among few options and can fix my own mistakes.
- [x] Schema linking as its own step before planning: a deterministic pass (AG-03 only on a miss) picks candidate measures, dimensions, values and time words with scores; AG-03 plans from 5 – 10 candidates instead of the full catalogue (targets the 33 % look_alike tag in the first baseline). *(`ask/linking.py::link_schema`, run in `Orchestrator.plan_for` after the rules: measures scored by the rules' `_score` (label, key, synonyms) or by word forms (`_near_share`, as `refusal_help`), a measure pair (US-176) or a bare money word counted as named, the rules' `measures_considered` first among equals; dimensions the same way among those reachable from the candidate measures, plus those the value index (US-173) linked a value of and the measure's date field when the question has a time word; values = the scoped links and choices; time words = `RANGES` / `GRAINS`. At most 5 measures + 5 dimensions, at least 5 together. AG-03's prompt has `candidates_block` (references, rules, scores, time words) in place of the full catalogue (`planner_context` removed). A miss (no measure scores 0.5) makes one AG-03 call (`LINK_SYSTEM` / `LinkOut` over the compact catalogue: keys and labels) whose picks are kept only where the person may see them; without a model or on failure the word-form ranking stands. The `retrieval` event carries `candidates`. On the draft gold set and its paraphrases (136 questions) the candidates hold every expected measure, extra measure, breakdown, filter and time dimension and filter value, without the rules' reading; the 10 `look_alike` questions rank the right measure first.)*
- [x] Repair loop on specs: validator and compiler refusals go back to AG-03 as typed errors ("dimension:region is not reachable from measure:forecast_cost", "value 'KSA' not found; closest: 'Saudi Arabia'"); up to 2 repairs; each repair reason logged (top reasons show what context the planner lacks). *(`ask/repair.py`: `precheck` names references the spec would have dropped silently (a measure or dimension not in the model or hidden from the person, with the closest by key/label; an extra measure of another table; a derived figure of a measure not asked for); `QueryService.check` (new: prepare + compile, no run) gives the validator's and compiler's refusals, turned into `SpecError`s by `from_refusal`: `not_reachable` ("… is not reachable from measure:…; reachable: …"), `value_not_found` (closest from the value index — a synonym — else by spelling among the person's scoped values, so a value outside their scope is never offered), `unknown_measure`, `invalid`, `compile`. `Orchestrator._llm_plan` sends the question again with `repair_block` (previous plan JSON + typed errors), at most `MAX_REPAIRS` = 2; after that the person gets the refusal's own message. A repaired plan says "Plan corrected: …" in `interpreted`. Free-hand rules are still not repaired (US-164). Migration **0035** `rag.plan_repairs` (attempt 1–3 = the plan refused, kind, ref, typed text without filter values, repaired, turn; RLS); `GET /api/v1/knowledge/plan-repairs?connection_id=&days=` (knowledge.manage) lists the top reasons and counts by kind.)*
- [x] Tests: linking candidates per gold question; repair cases. *(`tests/test_schema_linking_unit.py` (9): every draft gold question and paraphrase among 5–10 candidates, look-alikes first, values and time words, the miss path and model picks, the prompt block, typed errors from real `SpecValidator` refusals (reachability on a model without links, enum values, share without breakdown) and the repair request. `tests/test_plan_repair_api.py` (4, FakeGateway with a reply per call): AG-03 plans from candidates and picks only on a miss; a wrong value repaired from the person's own values; two repairs then the refusal, nothing outside the Creator's scope offered; unknown measure + dimension repaired, top reasons listed, Creator refused.)*

#### US-182 · Voting, back-translation and confidence · M · FEAT-064 / FEAT-019 — `[x]` *(EXEC-087, merged into master after US-187; no migration)*
As a user, I want Ask to ask me one question when it is unsure, so that I don't get a confident wrong number.
- [x] Self-consistency: for LLM plans, 3 samples at a moderate temperature; canonicalise each spec (sorted keys, the comparable core used by gold examples); take the majority; disagreement is an uncertainty signal. *(`ask/confidence.py::tally`: `Orchestrator._vote` runs `TELA_ASK_VOTE_SAMPLES` (3; 1 = off) AG-03 calls at once at `TELA_ASK_VOTE_TEMPERATURE` (0.6); failed samples don't vote. Each is reduced by `gold.comparable` (the gold diff's core with filters, breakdowns and derived figures sorted, plus limit/sort and the window and link keys); "can't answer" and free-hand are readings too, so two refusals outvote one plan. Only the first round is voted; US-181 repairs stay single deterministic calls. Gateway: `temperature` (0–1, optional) on `/generate`, passed to Ollama, Gemini and OpenAI (0 when absent, as before); Anthropic ignores it (thinking is always on).)*
- [x] Back-translation: the chosen spec in plain words through the existing plain-rule renderer; a small model or rules check it answers the question; a mismatch routes to clarification. *(`back_translate`: `rewrite.describe(spec)` ("Open projects by Region, Region: KSA") checked by rules — the question names a measure outright that the plan doesn't use; names a reachable field the plan leaves out ("by region and CIO" planned by region only); names a linked value the plan doesn't filter on; the plan filters on a value the question never said (stored, label or linked phrase). A question naming nothing the model knows passes. `TELA_ASK_BACKCHECK_MODEL=true` adds an AG-02 check (task `clarify`, `BackCheckOut {answers, missing}`) when the rules pass; off by default (one more call per LLM plan). A mismatch caps the score at 0.5.)*
- [x] `confidence` and `reasoning_summary` in `PlannerOut`; one combined score (model confidence, vote agreement, linking scores, back-translation). Below the threshold AG-02 asks one question with the top 2 – 4 readings as options. *(Score = 0.2 model (0.75 when not given) + 0.4 agreement + 0.15 weakest value link filtered on (US-173 scores) + 0.25 back-check, capped at 0.5 on a mismatch or when no reading has a majority: all agree ≈ 0.95, 2 of 3 ≈ 0.82. Rules plans: 1.0, or the weakest spelling/meaning link, or 0.7 for a trap reading. Below the threshold `confidence.decide` asks "I can read this more than one way. Which did you mean?" (`kind: reading`, `why`): the plan first, then what the samples disagreed on, then the plan asked of each alternative measure (a trap's misreadings, or the next linked candidates after a back-check mismatch), distinct in meaning and in words, each checked by `QueryService.check` first, at most 4. The specs stay on the turn (never in the event); the answer picks one by option id and the turn runs it as stored ("You chose: …"), and it becomes a suggested gold example like any clarified answer. One reading only: answered with "Not fully sure this is what you meant". The `complete` event carries `confidence {score, threshold, verbosity, signals, reasoning, why}`; the answer card shows "Confidence 70%" (· low) with the reasons. Gold evaluation runs at "balanced".)*
- [x] Per-user verbosity setting (assume-first, balanced, ask-freely) moves the threshold. *(Thresholds 0.35 / 0.6 / 0.85. `iam.users.preferences.ask_verbosity` through `PUT /api/v1/me/preferences`, which now saves only the keys sent (it used to reset the others to defaults). Ask header: "Clarifying questions" picker.)*
- [x] Ambiguity library: known traps as pack rules ("late" → overdue (the open STK-008 gap), "cost" → forecast cost not spend, "active" → open projects), feeding the rules planner and the glossary. *(PMO glossary 0.2.0: terms with `reading` (measure key) and `instead_of` — "late projects" → Overdue go-lives (was Total projects), "cost" → Forecast cost (EAC) (was a which-money question), "active projects" → Open projects, "active requests" → Open demand requests (was all Requests), "pending change requests" → PCRs awaiting approval (was demand Change requests, EXEC-082's note). Indexed with the glossary, pending Admin review; written "“cost” is read as …, not …" so free-hand never replaces the word. Rules planner: `find_trap` takes the longest trap phrase said, unless a measure is named outright in more words ("Forecast cost: internal", "Late critical tasks") or the question names another entity; it says "Read “late projects” as Overdue go-lives". Each still needs STK-008 sign-off.)*
- [x] Tests: vote and threshold unit tests; clarification routing. *(`test_confidence_unit.py` (24): voting (same meaning, majority, ties, refusals, window ops), back-translation (pass, missing field, other measure, unsaid filter, synonym, left-out value), score and caps, verbosity thresholds, readings question and resume, one reading, at most 4, each trap and outright names, trap asks only when asking freely, spelling link, glossary chunks. `test_confidence_api.py` (4, FakeGateway): agreeing samples answered with confidence and 3 sampled calls; disagreeing samples ask and the chosen reading runs as stored; an unsaid filter is checked with the person, answered with 0.5 under assume first; approved traps answered at balanced and asked under ask freely. Updated: `test_ask_api` (4 AG-03 calls with temperatures), `test_plan_repair_api` (rounds; the KSA question says KSA), `test_query_rewrite_api` (the canned plan answers "by region and CIO"), `test_identity_api` (preferences), `test_knowledge_api` (11 glossary chunks).)*

#### US-183 · Result sanity checks and feedback · M · FEAT-064 / FEAT-018 — `[x]` *(EXEC-079, branch `claude/enh013-us183-sanity-feedback-e3de15`, migration 0035)*
As a user, I want to be told when a result looks odd and to flag wrong answers, so that mistakes are caught and fixed.
- [x] Sanity checks in code after execution: empty result (offer the filter that removed everything); a single row when a breakdown was asked; totals that differ from the unfiltered measure in a surprising way; fan-out (distinct root count vs row count). Show a short note, never silently rerun. *(`ask/sanity.py::check` runs in `Orchestrator.answer` after the insight engine, on the answer's result plus at most a few one-figure totals through the same scoped path (surface `ask`, cached); a failing probe, a hidden or a truncated result skips the checks. Notes join the card's data notes (`narrative.notes`, ids `s1`…, `kind`, `numbers`, optional `action {label, ops}` — refine chip ops the person clicks; nothing is rerun in the answer's place). **empty**: each filter, then the time range, dropped in turn (≤ 4 probes); the first whose removal brings data back is named ("Nothing matches with the Region: KSA filter…") and offered as `remove_filter` / `clear_time`; when only all together empty it, "Remove all filters" (and dates); no filters → "There is no data for this in your scope". **single_row**: a breakdown or time grain asked and one row back; a filter on the same dimension is offered for removal. **filters_no_effect / filtered_above_total** (additive measures with filters): the filtered total against the same measure without filters — equal ("Your filters (…) don't change the total") or, for counts, higher. **fan_out / excluded** (additive breakdowns): the groups' sum against the total of the same spec without breakdowns, where each root record counts once — a larger sum means records count under several groups; a smaller one (no top N) is the "excluded rows beyond Not set" US-185's data note waited for ("3 of 15 (20%) isn't under any region shown"). Not additive (averages, ratios): no totals compared.)*
- [x] Thumbs up/down and "This isn't what I asked" on each answer; down-votes with the question and spec (never rows) go to an Admin queue; fixing one creates a gold example. *(Table `chat.answer_feedback` (migration 0035, RLS tenant isolation): one vote per person and turn, `reason` `wrong` / `not_what_i_asked`, comment ≤ 500, copies of the question and spec only, status `none` (up) / `open` / `fixed` (with `gold_example_id`) / `dismissed`. API (`ask/feedback.py`): `GET/PUT/DELETE /ask/turns/{id}/feedback` (`ask.use`, own turns only, finished answers; a resolved vote is final), `GET /ask/feedback` (`knowledge.manage`; open first, counts incl. helpful votes), `PATCH /ask/feedback/{id}` (`fixed` needs an existing, non-rejected gold example; `dismissed` with a note; the voter is notified of a fix). Audit `answer.feedback`, `answer.feedback_resolved`. Web: `AnswerFeedback` under the card's actions (Helpful / Not helpful with `aria-pressed`, "This isn't what I asked" opens the reason + comment form; the same thumb again takes the vote back); sanity notes show their action as a link in Data notes. Admin → Knowledge → **Flagged answers** (`/admin/knowledge/feedback`): table with reason, comment, how Ask read it, voter; **Fix** edits the question and spec and saves them through `POST /knowledge/gold-examples` with the turn id (approved, tag `from_feedback`, the comment as notes), then marks the feedback fixed — a duplicate question links the existing gold example; **Dismiss** with a note.)*
- [x] Tests: each sanity check; feedback queue API and vitest. *(`tests/test_sanity_unit.py` 8 tests (each check with a fake runner, probe specs, skips); `tests/test_answer_feedback_api.py` 2 live tests (sanity notes on live answers and the offered refinement accepted; votes, permissions, queue contents without rows, fix through a gold example, final states, dismiss, take back, audit); `src/test/answer-feedback.test.tsx` 4 tests (note action, thumbs, "This isn't what I asked" form, fixed state, queue fix, duplicate link, dismiss). Axe (WCAG 2.2 AA tags, contrast left to `contrast.test.ts`) on the card with the form open, the queue and the fix dialog in jsdom: no violations (one-off, not committed).)*

Per run: `mrr` (expected measure's rank), `ndcg_at_8` (a hit counts only for the measure or dimensions it adds), `recall_by_kind` (measure, dimension), `value_linking` (filter values right, for questions with filters; a right answer that needed no filter on that field is not counted). First live figures on the test fixture (57 examples): execution 98.2 %, spec match 96.5 %, recall@8 91 %, MRR 0.75, nDCG@8 0.69, **dimension recall 39 %**, value linking 4 / 4 — the baseline for US-173 – US-175. Dev stack at 0031 with the 54 draft examples approved on TPG (EXEC-062). **TPG baseline (EXEC-064, with US-174's lexical weighting, reranker not active):** execution 54 / 54, spec match 100 %, recall@8 94.4 %, MRR 0.80, nDCG@8 0.73, dimension recall 50 %, value linking 4 / 4 — the draft set is too easy to separate changes; the 150 questions are what Gate A needs. **Wave-1 rerun (EXEC-072, dev stack at 0034 with US-173 – US-175 and US-185, knowledge re-indexed with US-174 headers/terms/synonyms, value index 551 values, reranker off):** execution 54 / 54, spec match 100 %, recall@8 92.6 %, MRR 0.78, nDCG@8 0.75, **dimension recall 70.6 %** (was 50 %), value linking 4 / 4. **Wave-2 rerun (EXEC-088, dev stack at 0037 on master f9094f0 with US-176 – US-178, US-181 – US-183, US-186, US-187, glossary 0.2.0 approved, reranker off; run `01a18775…`):** execution 54 / 54, spec match 100 %, recall@8 90.7 %, MRR 0.77, nDCG@8 0.73, dimension recall 67.6 %, value linking 4 / 4, **over-clarification failures 0** (EXEC-072: 0); all 54 by the rules planner. Before the glossary approval the same build scored recall@8 92.6 %, MRR 0.79, nDCG@8 0.75: the approved “active projects” trap passage outranks the measure for three open/active-projects questions. The DB still holds the 54 draft examples (pack gold 0.6.0 has 124), so the wave-2 shapes are not yet measured. Long runs: the worker's call claims the run and returns 202; the API runs it in the background, marks it interrupted on shutdown and fails one a crash left running after 4 hours (EXEC-064).)*

#### ENH-014 · Insights and visuals · FEAT-065
Turn the answer card from "one headline and one chart" into "headline, two to four verified insights, the right chart, and next questions". All numbers stay computed in code; AG-08 only phrases them, and every number is checked as today.

#### US-185 · Insight engine · M · FEAT-065 — `[x]` (EXEC-071)
As a user, I want the answer to point out what matters in the numbers, so that I don't have to find it myself.
- [x] `ask/insights.py` runs after execution on the cached result; each detector returns typed facts with an id, a priority and the numbers it used; only detectors whose data shape fits run; no row-level values leave the API. *(`insights.detect(result, additive, agg, tone, comparison, previous)` is called in `Orchestrator.answer` after `execute` (cache hit or not). A fact is `{id: "f1"…, type, priority 0–1, numbers, members, text}`; `members` are aggregated group labels (a programme, a month), never record values. Shapes: single value → gap, change (with compare); one period → change, trend/break, data note; period + one dimension (additive) → change, driver, trend, data note; one dimension → concentration (additive), outlier, distribution (avg/median/percentile/min/max), threshold, change + driver (with compare: the previous-period result is kept from `_compare`), data note. Facts go out in the `narrative` event as `insights` (stored on the turn) and, until the card v2 (US-186), as extra sentences `{text, fact}` after the existing ones. `insights.numbers_of(fact)` gives the numbers a sentence may use, for US-186's grounding check.)*
- [x] Detectors: **concentration** (breakdown with 4+ items; top-N share, Pareto 80 % point), **outlier** (6+ items; robust z-score on median and MAD, |z| > 3), **change vs previous period** (time grain or `period_compare`; delta and % with the period named), **trend and break** (6+ periods; slope sign; XmR limits for a shift), **driver** (change plus a second dimension; contribution of each member to the delta), **distribution** (percentile or record-level measure; P50, P85, spread, skew), **gap vs target** (measure with a threshold in the pack), **data note** (Not set or excluded rows: count and share). *(Concentration names the items when three or fewer hold everything ("Only 2 of 7 have any overdue go-lives: …") and stays quiet for an even spread. Outlier: MAD scaled by 1/0.6745, mean absolute deviation when MAD is 0; at most two. Change: latest vs previous period of a time grain, or FEAT-020 `compare` until US-178's `period_compare`. Trend: least squares, reported when r² ≥ 0.3; break: XmR limits (± 2.66 × average moving range) around the mean, or around the fitted line when the series trends, so a steady rise isn't a break; a run of 8 on one side of the earlier mean is a shift and replaces a weak trend (r² < 0.8). Driver: top three members by |delta| with share of the total change. Distribution: P15/P50/P85 (PostgreSQL `percentile_cont` interpolation) and Bowley skew, 8+ items. Gap: the pack's figure `tones` (report_blueprints.yaml, new `guidance.load_tones`) — on the figure, or on the total of an additive breakdown; per member for ratios and shares. Data note: the Not set group's figure and share, and a truncated read. "Excluded rows" beyond Not set need the record counts of US-183's sanity checks — added there as the `excluded` sanity note (EXEC-079).)*
- [x] Rank facts by priority (size of effect × how unusual), keep the top four, drop any fact that restates another. *(Each fact carries the subjects it covers (`("lead", member)`, `("period", p)`, `("break",)`…); a fact whose subject a higher-priority fact already covers is dropped — e.g. the concentration lead that is also the outlier.)*
- [x] Unit tests per detector. *(`tests/test_insights_unit.py`, 26 tests: each detector, shape gate, ranking, threshold on total vs per member, and every template sentence grounded in its own numbers; `test_ask_api::test_certified_answer_with_grounded_headline` checks a live answer's insights: ids, members ⊆ result labels, sentences cite their fact, grounded.)*

#### US-186 · Narrator v2 and answer card v2 · M · FEAT-065 / FEAT-054 — `[x]` *(EXEC-075, branch `enh014-us186-narrator`)*
As a user, I want a headline with cited insights, drivers and next questions, so that one answer tells the whole story.
- [x] AG-08 v2 prompt: one headline and up to three insight sentences from the supplied facts only, numbers copied exactly with units, each sentence ending with its fact ids (`[fact:3]`); no causes, advice or forecasts unless a fact states them; JSON `{"headline": {"text", "facts"}, "insights": [{"text", "facts"}]}`. *(`narrator.AG08_V2_SYSTEM`, `NarratorV2Out`, `v2_prompt`: the headline facts go in as `f0` (template headline + `facts()` numbers) beside the engine's facts (id, type, text, numbers, members; data notes are not narrated). Ids may come as `facts`, `[fact:3]` / `[fact:f3]` markers or both; markers are stripped from the text. v1 `NarratorOut` removed.)*
- [x] Checks: cited ids ⊆ supplied ids; every number matches a fact; wording check from `content/wording.py`; a template sentence per fact when the LLM is off or a check fails. *(`narrator.check` per sentence: at least one id, all ids supplied (an insight may not cite `f0`), every number in the text is a number of a fact it cites (`insights.numbers_of` / `fact_numbers`), `wording.ok` with the model's certified labels. `narrator.compose`: a failing headline falls back to the template; each insight fact not covered by a kept model sentence gets its own template sentence; at most three, in fact order; `source` llm / template / mixed.)*

"Why?" opens the fact's kind, members and numbers. Driver: a breakdown answer's own driver fact (US-185) names its dimension; a single figure compared with the previous period is broken down by up to three candidate dimensions of the measure's entity in model order (`nextsteps.driver_candidates`; both periods, ≤ 50 members) and the one whose lead member explains most of the change is kept (`Orchestrator._driver`) — "Break down by …" applies `add_breakdown`. Ask next (`ask/nextsteps.py`): driver dimension (when not already a breakdown), `compare_previous` (when the spec has a time range and no compare), drill = `add_filter` to the outlier or concentration lead on the first breakdown (stored value from `value_labels`); all go through the refine path. How this was worked out: the steps list (moved from the top of the card), what was read, and "Show query" (`/query/explain`) for `widgets.create`, not for free-hand rules. Multi-step plan steps wait for US-179.)*
- [x] Highlight-and-grey: the item an insight mentions is coloured and the rest muted. *(`Chart` `highlight` prop → `toOption(..., {labels, muted})`: one bar series keeps the colour on named items, others take `chart-other`; on a trend the named points are larger; labels that match nothing change nothing. The card highlights the first cited insight with members, and the one hovered or opened with "Why?".)*
- [x] Tests: narrator checks and fallback; vitest card sections; axe clean in both themes. *(`tests/test_narrator_v2_unit.py` 9 tests (checks, markers, per-fact fallback, wording, cap, prompt, follow-ups, candidates, strongest driver); `test_ask_api`: the live answer's v2 story and every Ask-next op accepted by `/refine`, and a FakeGateway AG-08 v2 run (bad headline number → template, cited sentence kept). `src/test/ask-card-v2.test.tsx` 3 tests (section order, why, driver apply, Ask next ops, Show query for Creators only, no empty sections, highlight in `toOption`). Axe (WCAG 2.2 AA tags) on the card sections in the `/dev/ui` catalogue ("Answer card v2 (US-186)", light and dark side by side) from the Vite dev server: no violations of any impact; the stack-based `a11y.spec.ts` covers the empty Ask page only.)*

#### ENH-015 · Ask on dashboard screens · FEAT-022 *(new, requested 2026-10-30; built in EXEC-099, branch `enh015-us189-dashboard-ask`)*
**What the user asked for.**
1. Add Ask to the dashboard screens, like a bot.
2. Clicking it opens a right side bar with a chat window on the dashboard screen.
3. Whatever Ask does, the side bar does the same.

**Working positions:**
- **W1 — Same Ask, not a copy.** The Ask page's conversation (streams, stages, clarifying questions, errors with their ways forward, answer card v2, refine, undo, pin, drill, feedback, gold) moves into `features/ask/AskChat.tsx` (`useAskSession`, `TurnList`); the Ask page and the side bar both use it, so they cannot drift apart.
- **W2 — The dashboard is the context.** Questions carry the dashboard's id, version, connection and the page filters the reader has set. Filters apply only where they reach the answer's measure; the others are named on the answer ("Dashboard filters not applied …"), never a failed query.
- **W3 — Beside, not over.** The side bar is not modal: the dashboard stays usable (tabs, filters, drill) while it is open. Its conversation lasts while the reader is on the dashboard; "Open in Ask" continues it full screen.
- **W4 — "Ask about this" on a widget opens the side bar** about that widget (replaces the Ask-about dialog that navigated to the Ask page).

#### ENH-016 · Ask accuracy on real wording · FEAT-066 *(new, 2026-11-01; from the EXEC-102 diagnostics and paraphrase probe)*
**Why.** The pack gold questions plan 36/36, but paraphrases of the same questions plan 1/38 on the Dataverse model (EXEC-102): 10 answered with the wrong measure at confidence 1.0, 20 refused on the LLM path (AG-03 on llama3.2:3b produced "no measure chosen" every time), 3 missed the breakdown, 3 asked (one without the right option). In 5 of the 10 wrong answers, and 12 of the 20 refusals, retrieval had the right measure in its top 8; the rules planner and schema linking never read it.

**Working positions:**
- **W1 — No LLM-written SQL.** The planner keeps producing a Query Spec that code compiles; free-hand keeps the expression format. The reference `reference/production_grade_hybrid_retrieval_nl2sql_architecture.md` is reference only.
- **W2 — Measure first, on wording nobody tuned on.** Every story here is accepted on the US-190 paraphrase set (held out, never a few-shot, never used to write synonyms) and must not lower the original gold set. EXEC-102 is the baseline.
- **W3 — Certified first stays.** Changes add evidence to linking and confidence; they don't let a model pick outside the certified measures.
- **W4 — Better to ask than to be wrong.** A confident wrong number is the worst outcome; turning it into one clarifying question is a win, over-asking on the original gold set is not.

Order: **Wave 0** US-190, US-191 · **Wave 1** US-192, US-193, US-195 · **Wave 2** US-194, US-196, US-197 · **Wave 3** US-198.
