- [x] At least 100 held-out paraphrases of pack gold questions (`held_out: true`, tag `paraphrase`), starting from the 38 of EXEC-102; colloquial, look-alike, breakdown-word ("why", "how long", "how late"), money and percentage wording; no sample-only values, so the set runs on TPG and on a live Dataverse model. *(Gold set 0.7.0: 125 paraphrases of 50 originals, each with `paraphrase_of` and the original's spec, no filters; styles `reworded`, `colloquial`, `look_alike`, `breakdown_word`, `money`, `percentage`; the 38 of EXEC-102 tagged `exec102`.)*
- [x] Scores per run and per path (rules, free-hand, LLM): spec match, execution accuracy, refusal rate, **confident-wrong rate** (wrong answer at or above the person's threshold), clarification with the right option among the readings, over-clarification on the original set. *(`scoring.group_scores` / `by_path` / `by_set`; run columns `by_path`, `by_set`; result columns `status`, `confidence`, `confident_wrong` (threshold = `confidence.threshold`, balanced 0.6), `clarify_right` (expected measure among the options or readings); a clarification on the original set is over-clarification. Migration **0041**.)*

- [x] A plan-only mode (no result rows) for live sources, as the EXEC-102 probe ran; `tela-admin` command or gold-run option; results in `rag.eval_results` with the planner path and latency. *(`POST /gold-examples/evaluate {plan_only}`; `tela-admin gold-run --tenant … [--tag paraphrase] [--plan-only] [--run-as …]` and `gold-report`; `eval_results.latency_ms`. Review now takes up to 500 ids: the draft set is 249.)*

#### US-191 · Planner model fit for AG-03 · S · FEAT-066 / FEAT-049 — `[~]` built EXEC-109; hosted run pending a hosted key
Depends: US-190, DEC-23 (decided: Local and Hosted both offered).
As an Admin, I want to know whether the configured model can plan, so that the LLM path answers rather than refuses.
- [ ] Run US-190's LLM-path questions on llama3.2:3b, qwen3:8b and a hosted model (the tenant's configured DEC-01 provider); record schema-valid first replies, accuracy, refusals, latency, timeouts and calls per question (EXEC-102: 128 calls, 8 timeouts, ≈ 44 s each, 81–240 s per question on llama3.2:3b).

#### ENH-017 · Trustworthy pack mapping and knowledge-led Ask · FEAT-067 *(new, 2026-11-03; from the EXEC-115 diagnosis)*
**Why.** On the TPG MySQL connection, "How many open projects (current state Project Created, Active or On Hold) are there?" answered **279**; the data has **431** (Active 279 + On Hold 79 + Project Created 73). Cause, in order:
1. At pack install (07:21, `pmo_tpg` 2.1.0, merge) the pack's **Status** field (Active/Inactive record state; Dataverse `statecodename`) found no column by name (`status`, `statecodename`), so it was mapped by hand to **`current_state`** (lifecycle values); the right column is `record_status` (exactly Active, Inactive). Current state was also matched to `current_state` by exact name, so **two pack fields read one column**.
2. The mapping step checks only that the column exists and its type fits; model approval checks nothing more. Model v2 was certified with every Status measure changed: Open projects = current state Active (279), Closed projects, Projects on hold and Projects not yet started = 0.
3. Ask matched "open projects" by name, the rules planner used the certified formula as-is and stacked the question's own criteria on it as a filter (dropping "On Hold", read as the "Projects on hold" measure). The answer note "your filters don't change the total" was recorded, yet the answer went out certified at confidence 1.0.
4. Uploaded knowledge played no part: the rules planner doesn't read documents, and a data question gives documents ≈ 400 characters of AG-03's context.

**Working positions:**
- **W1 — The LLM suggests; checks and the Admin decide.** No mapping is applied without the Admin's confirmation, and every mapping (LLM, name match or hand-picked) passes the same guards at install and at model approval.
- **W2 — Numbers come from queries only.** Knowledge shapes the plan and the explanation; an answer's figures always come from a compiled, validated query (ENH-016 W1 stands: no LLM-written SQL).
- **W3 — Approved definitions outrank a measure's formula, visibly.** When an approved business definition or the question's own criteria disagree with a certified measure, Ask uses the stated definition and says so, or asks; it never silently picks the measure.
- **W4 — The rules shortcut stays for plain questions.** The LLM planner with knowledge is used when the question states its own criteria, knowledge defines a term in it, or a sanity signal fires — not for every question (EXEC-113: local AG-03 3/38 on paraphrases).
- **W5 — Measured.** Accepted on the US-190 sets (no loss on originals) plus a new set of questions that state their own criteria; EXEC-115's case is the first.

Order: **Wave 1** US-199, US-200, US-202 · **Wave 2** US-201, US-205 · **Wave 3** US-203, US-204.

#### US-199 · Mapping guards at install and approval · M · FEAT-067 / FEAT-059 — `[x]`
As an Admin, I want a wrong field mapping stopped before the model is certified, so that every measure built on it is not silently wrong.
- [x] **Same column:** two pack fields of one entity mapped to the same source column → blocking issue in the mapping step (`packs/mapping.check`) and at install; the exact-name match also respects columns already taken by the Admin's mapping (`packs/manifest.match` skips `used` only for guesses today). *(`packs/manifest.match` seeds `used` with the Admin's mapped columns; an exact name already taken leaves the field unmapped with `how = column_taken` ("Its column is mapped to another field") and `EntityMatch.taken`. `packs/mapping.conflicts` names both cases: "Status and Current state both read current_state", "current_state is Current state's own column, but it is mapped to Status". The preview returns them as `checks.blocking` (the step stays visible to fix them); install and the measure check refuse with `validation_error` and the issues.)*
- [x] **Values:** for a pack field whose measures filter on known values (`pack_values`), profile the mapped column's distinct values (top values with counts, through the connector runner, scoped and limited); warn when the pack's values are missing (Status expects Active, Inactive; `current_state` has Completed, Active, On Hold …), and show the column's values beside the pack's in the step. *(Preview reads the 50 most frequent values of each status-like text field (`QueryService.trial`, `field_query` "top", `index_scope` as the value index, recorded as `precheck`) over a definition of the mapped fields only (`mapping.profile_definition`, so it works while a required field is still missing). Each step row gets `source_values`; `checks.warnings` lists fields whose rule values (`pack_values(rules_only=True)`) are neither in the column nor value-mapped; blanks and free-text columns (50+ values) are not checked. UI: "Pack: Active, Inactive · current_state: Active (279), … · not found: Inactive" on the row, a warning panel above the step.)*
- [x] **Measures after mapping:** a trial count of each bound pack measure on the real data; warn on measures that return 0, or the same figure as another measure with a different rule (Open projects = In delivery), and list them before install. *(`POST /starter-packs/{id}/check`: each bound measure once over every row, `guards.measure_warnings` — 0, or the same figure as another measure of its table and format with a different rule. Audit `starter_pack.checked`. Dialog: "Check the measures" in "Measures on your data", reset when the mapping changes; disabled while a blocking issue stands.)*
- [x] **At model approval:** the same checks run on the draft (fields sharing a column, measure filter values not found in the column, zero measures); a blocking issue needs an explicit Admin override with a note (audit `model.approval_override`). *(`model/guards.py`. Approve refuses fields sharing a column (`model_invalid`, `details.can_override`) unless `override_note` (3–1,000 characters) is given, then audits `model.approval_override` with the note and issues. The data checks — each measure's figure and the values of fields measure rules filter on; warnings for a filter none of whose values the column holds, zero, same figure — run on request through `GET /models/{id}/draft/checks` (`?data=false` for the same-column check only), not inside approve (one read per measure made each approval ≈ 17.6 s). UI: Checks step "Each source column stands for one field" and "Measures look right on the data" (Check the data); Approve step "Why approve anyway?" + Approve anyway.)*
- [x] Tests: the EXEC-115 mapping (`projects.status → current_state`) is blocked with the reason; `record_status` passes; zero and duplicate measures listed; approval refuses the broken draft without an override. *(New `test_mapping_guards_unit.py` (7) and `test_mapping_guards_api.py` (1, live: a TPG copy with `record_status`; preview blocks and warns, install and check refused; `record_status` passes, Open projects = the source's Active count; the draft broken as model v2 is listed by draft checks, refused at approval, approved with an override note and audited). Vitest `admin-data.test.tsx` +2.)*

Reply held to a schema: column or none, confidence, reason. *(`packs/ai_suggest.py`, `POST /starter-packs/{id}/ai-suggestions` (Admins), AG-04 on a new gateway task **`map_suggest`** (planner model). Asked: fields with no column (not "not in this source") and fields whose column's values don't fit (US-200), unless a value suggestion of confidence ≥ 0.8 settles them; at most 30 per table, required first. Shown: the pack fields (key, label, type, description, the values its rules use) and the table's columns no other pack field reads (≤ 150), with up to 6 of the most frequent values of text columns (≤ 40 read, one `precheck` trial read; none for personal columns by flag or `model/catalog.PERSONAL`); no counts, no rows. Reply `AiMapReply` {suggestions: [{field, column or null, confidence 0–1, reason}]}; a field not asked about, a column the table lacks or the field's own column is dropped (`dropped`). Audit `starter_pack.ai_suggested` with counts only.)*
- [x] Suggestions are shown with the reason and marked "Suggested by AI"; nothing is applied until the Admin confirms; every confirmed mapping passes US-199's guards. *(Mapping step: "Suggest with AI" (shown when `checks.ai_available`), then on each row a "Suggested by AI" block — column, confidence, reason, "Use <column>"; "No column found" with the reason. Using one sets the field's column and the preview reruns with US-199's guards; the others stay. Each suggestion carries `flags`: a column another pack field reads, a type that doesn't fit, values that don't fit the pack's, one column suggested for two fields.)*
- [x] Works offline: with no model configured the step works as today plus US-199/US-200. *(No gateway or no provider (tenant's, else the deployment's default): `ai_available: false`, no button, the endpoint answers `available: false` with a note. A failing or invalid reply leaves that table `failed`, the others stand. The preview never calls the model.)*
- [x] Tests: FakeGateway suggestions shown and not applied; schema-invalid reply ignored; a suggested column that fails US-199's guards is flagged; invocation logged (`ops.llm_invocations`). *(New `test_ai_mapping_suggestions_unit.py` (4) and `test_ai_mapping_suggestions_api.py` (1, live on the TPG copy with `record_status` and `go_live_target`: offline; Status settled by values and not asked; the go-live date suggested and not applied; `current_state` flagged as Current state's column and of the wrong type; an answer invalid after its repair ignored; the confirmed column passes; audit; Creators refused). Gateway `test_mapping_suggestions_are_allowed_and_recorded`: the ledger row (`ops.llm_invocations`) names AG-04 / `map_suggest` and no column names. Vitest `admin-data.test.tsx` +2.)*

#### US-203 · Business definitions from knowledge · L · FEAT-067 / FEAT-014 / FEAT-062 — `[x]` *(EXEC-119, branch `enh017-us203-knowledge-definitions`, migration 0043)*
As an Admin, I want the business definitions in an uploaded document turned into approved, structured definitions, so that Ask applies them rather than reading them as background text.
- [x] On indexing an uploaded document or glossary, propose definitions: term, meaning, and where it can be expressed on the model, a rule in the measure expression format (entity, field, operator, values), e.g. "Open project = current state in Project Created, Active, On Hold". Proposals go to the review gate (FEAT-014); nothing applies until approved. *(`knowledge/definitions.py`, called when the worker's chunks are stored (`put_chunks`): a heading with "An X is …" (or its first sentence) under it, `Term: meaning` / `Term = meaning` / "Term means …" lines, two-column table rows. Read deterministically, no model call. Rule: the term's entity by its noun ("project" → Projects; else the meaning's subject), the longest field label the meaning names, the operator ("is", "in", "one of"; "not", "other than", "except" → `not_in`; values straight after "Current state" or "with status") and the listed values, `{entity, field, op: in | not_in, values}`. A term that names nothing on the model ("Go-live means the deployment …") is not proposed. One proposal per connection the document is for (its own, or every connection with an approved model), status `pending`. Re-indexing keeps unchanged proposals and their review; a changed one waits again. The uploader's "indexed" notice says how many terms were proposed.)*
- [x] An approved definition is stored per connection and model version, linked to the passage it came from; a definition that names fields or values the model lacks is kept as text only, with the gap shown. *(`rag.knowledge_definitions` (migration **0043**, RLS): `chunk_id`, `chunk_no`, `heading` and the passage text; `gaps` and `expressible`. Gaps: a field phrase the model lacks ("The model has no field “stage gate” on Projects."), a value not in the value index (US-173; values take their stored spelling), a non-text field, and at approval a listed value no row has (a count per value: "No rows have Current state “Parked”."). Approval needs the document approved first; the Admin may edit the term, the meaning or the rule (checked on the model), or keep it as text only. A newly approved model version carries the connection's pending and approved definitions (re-read on its model, review kept) and retires the older version's rows (`carry_forward`, from `index_model_version`). Audit `knowledge.definition_approved` / `_rejected` / `_retired`.)*
- [x] **Conflicts:** an approved definition that disagrees with a certified measure of the same term (by synonym or linking) is listed for the Admin with both figures (trial count), not resolved silently. *(The measure of the same term by its label, key or a synonym, plural or not (a looser name match is not used: "Stuck request" is not Projects). At approval (and `POST /knowledge/definitions/{id}/check`) both are counted under the Admin's scope on the `certification_preview` surface: the measure's figure and the definition's (the measure's aggregate with the definition's condition in place of its rule, W3). `checks` holds the figures, the per-value counts, `same_rule` and `conflict`; `GET /knowledge/definitions?conflicts=true` lists them. The measure is never changed.)*
- [x] Approved definitions are always included in AG-03's context for questions that use the term (not cut by section budgets, US-196). *(`definitions.for_question`: approved definitions of an approved document on the connection's current model version whose term, or the matched measure's label or synonyms, the question says (plural or not), and whose field the person can see. `context_block(definitions=…)` writes them first as "Business definitions (approved; …)", outside the section budgets, each cited by its passage's chunk: the meaning, "Rule: count Projects where Current state is one of …" or "Text only: …", and "Differs from the certified measure Open projects (Status is Active)" when it conflicts. Applying them to the plan and the answer is US-204; the certified-answer check is US-205.)*
- [x] Tests: definition extracted from a Markdown section; review gate; text-only when a field is missing; conflict with Open projects listed with 279/420 vs 431. *(New `test_knowledge_definitions_unit.py` (10): reading headings, lines and table rows; stored spellings and missing values; measure by name only; same rule; the definition's figure; terms in questions; context first and never cut; re-read on a new model. New `test_knowledge_definitions_api.py` (4, live): 4 proposals from an uploaded Markdown document, linked to their chunks, "stage gate" text only, refused before the document is approved, Admins only, reject; **conflict**: with 3 Active projects marked record-Inactive (as the 11 on TPG MySQL), Open projects (status Active) counts 3 fewer than the definition (current state in Project Created, Active, On Hold), both figures and the per-value counts listed, `conflicts=true`, the measure unchanged; edited rule checked; "Parked" → text only; AG-03's system prompt has the approved definition first and only when the question uses the term; a new model version carries the definitions. Vitest `admin-definitions.test.tsx` (3): new tab **Definitions** (`DefinitionsPage.tsx`).)*

#### US-204 · Knowledge-led planning route · L · FEAT-067 / FEAT-066 / FEAT-062 — `[x]` *(EXEC-123, merged into master, no migration)*
Depends: US-203 (definitions); works without it on the question's own criteria.
As a person asking, I want a question that states what it means answered by that meaning, so that I get the number I described, not a measure's built-in rule.
- [x] **Route:** the rules planner keeps plain questions; the plan goes to AG-03 with knowledge when the question states its own criteria on a field the matched measure's rule uses, when an approved definition covers a term in the question, or when a US-205 signal fires. *(`ask/stated.py`, run in `Orchestrator._rules` after the rules' checks. Deterministic first (W2, W4): stated criteria or an expressible approved definition become a certified base measure plus a filter, planner **`knowledge`**, no model call. AG-03 with the definitions first in its context plans what that can't express: a text-only definition the question uses ("Planned with the approved definition “…”"), a definition with no measure (the rules refuse it, as in US-203's test), and the US-205 no-effect re-plan as before. "Same field" = the same field or another field of the table on the same source column (EXEC-115: Status mapped to `current_state`); criteria on another field (the right mapping: Status is the record state) stay a filter on the measure, and US-205 asks when a listed value is left out.)*
- [x] **Stated criteria win:** criteria on the same field as the measure's rule replace that part of the rule (count of the measure's entity with the stated filter) rather than being stacked on it; the answer says "Counted by your definition: Current state in Project Created, Active, On Hold" and shows the certified measure's figure beside it when they differ. Every value of a listed set stays in the filter (no value of the list read as another measure). *(`stated.from_question`: a `=`/`in` filter on a field one of the rule's conditions reads, allowing a value the rule doesn't (criteria that only narrow the rule — "open projects with status Active" — keep the measure). The replaced conditions are dropped and the rest of the rule must be a certified measure of the table with the same aggregate (`base_measure`: Projects for Open projects); none → the rules' plan stays. Listed values the filter left out are added from the field's scoped values (US-205 `listed_values`/`with_listed`), so On Hold stays a state. The plan's other filters are kept on both figures. Scored by `stated.check` as a rules plan, with the replaced measure and measures named only by a stated value (Projects on hold) out of the running. Answer: interpreted line first, sentence on the card, badge `your_definition`, `certified: false` (export allowed: a certified measure plus a filter), `counted_by`; the certified measure with the plan's other filters runs beside (`own_filters`) and is shown only when its figure differs (`when_differs`).)*
- [x] Approved definitions (US-203) are applied the same way, cited by name in the answer; the badge says the answer used a knowledge definition rather than a certified measure (certified-first, W3). *(`stated.from_definition`: an approved, expressible definition the question uses (`definitions.uses_term`) whose certified measure the rules planned and whose rule is not the definition's (`same_rule`): the measure's base count with the definition's condition in place of its rule, as US-203's approval check counts it. Stated criteria win over a definition. "Counted by the approved definition “Open project”: Current state in Project Created, Active, On Hold", badge `knowledge_definition` ("Approved definition"), the card shows the meaning and the document.)*
- [x] The narrator writes from the query result and cites the definitions and passages it used; it never states a figure that is not in the result. *(The counted-by sentence leads the story with `cites: [chunk:<passage>]`; `story.definitions_used` holds the definition, its document and passage. Figures stay the template's and AG-08 v2's, both checked against the result's facts; the beside figure comes from its own query.)*
- [x] Accepted on a new set of stated-criteria questions (EXEC-115's first) and the US-190 sets with no loss on originals; latency recorded per path. *(Offline: 6 stated-criteria questions on the EXEC-115 model plan as expected; no original or paraphrase is routed (unchanged). Per path: histogram `tela_ask_plan_duration_seconds{path=rules|knowledge|llm|freehand}` and log `ask.plan_path` (path, ms); gold runs group by planner, so `knowledge` shows there too. Live latency on TPG MySQL not yet measured (dev stack not rebuilt).)*
- [x] Tests: EXEC-115 question → 431 with the note; the same question without criteria → the certified measure; definition applied and cited; list values kept. *(New `test_knowledge_route_unit.py` (21) and `test_knowledge_route_api.py` (4, live, TPG fixture: the EXEC-115 mapping counts the three states — the fixture's figure, 431 on TPG MySQL — with no AG-03 call and Open projects beside; without criteria the certified measure; the right mapping keeps the rules and US-205 asks; an approved "Open project" definition applied, cited by its chunk, Open projects beside (3 fewer), and the US-205 conflict on a two-measure question). `test_answer_sanity_api.py`: the two EXEC-115 tests now run with the stated route off (`no_route`), so US-205's ask and re-plan stay covered. Vitest `ask-knowledge-route.test.tsx` (1). Affected suites 391 passed; full API suite 1,286 passed, 14 skipped, 1 xfailed.)*

#### US-205 · Answer sanity checks · S · FEAT-067 / FEAT-064 — `[x]` *(EXEC-121, merged into master; definitions check EXEC-123 with US-204)*
As a person asking, I want Ask to notice when its answer doesn't fit my question, so that a misread question asks or re-plans instead of answering certified.
- [x] **Filters with no effect:** when the question's filters don't change the total (today's `filters_no_effect` note), confidence drops below the threshold and the turn re-plans once through US-204's route or asks with the readings. *(Before answering, `Orchestrator._no_effect` → `sanity.filters_no_effect` probes four totals through the scoped path (the card's own sanity probes then hit the cache): the measure with and without the filters, and its table's own count with and without them. It fires only when the measure is unchanged **and** the filters narrow the table to another figure — the measure's rule already implies them. Filters that narrow nothing (every portfolio named), agree with the rule (same figure on the table count), sit on a field the rule reads ("open projects that are active") or a field the person's scope restricts say nothing. Fired → `confidence.no_effect`: score capped at 0.55 (below "balanced"; "assume first" answers with the note), the note in `why`, signal `filters_no_effect`, the table's count with the same filters as a reading. A rules plan is then planned once more by AG-03 (until US-204's route exists) — kept when it differs, with "Planned again: …" in the interpreted line; otherwise `_decide` asks. An AG-03 plan gets the cap, no re-plan.)*
- [x] **Named values left out:** a value the question names that the plan neither filters on nor uses → mismatch (the "On Hold" of EXEC-115), as US-193's back-check does for other words. *(`confidence.listed_values`: the back-check now also reads the scoped values the rules planner looked up (`Orchestrator._values`), so it works without a value index — the live EXEC-115 turn had none and scored 1.0. A value of a field the plan filters on (`=`/`in`), said in the question and not in the filter → "the question names On Hold"; not a value whose words name the plan's measure, nor one the question excludes ("not on hold"). Readings added (`with_listed`): the filter with every listed value, and the same on the table's own count ("Projects, Current state: Active, Project Created, On Hold" — 431 on TPG MySQL).)*
- [x] **Against definitions:** an answer whose measure conflicts with an approved definition (US-203) of a term in the question is not marked certified; the note names the definition. *(EXEC-123: `stated.conflicting` in `Orchestrator.plan_for` — an approved definition with `checks.conflict` on the plan's measure, the question using its term, and the plan not counted by it (US-204 couldn't express it: two measures side by side, no base measure). Signal `definition_conflict`, interpreted "Not certified here: the approved definition “Open project” differs from Open projects", `certified: false`, badge `definition_conflict`.)*
- [x] Tests: EXEC-115 turn no longer certified at 1.0; originals unchanged on the US-190 offline harness. *(New `test_answer_sanity_unit.py` (11): EXEC-115 without a value index 1.0 → clarify with the 431 reading; with the index caught once; listed-value exclusions; the 63 originals and 249 paraphrases plan, score and decide exactly as without the scoped values; the no-effect cap and readings; the four-probe rule with each exemption. New `test_answer_sanity_api.py` (4) on the TPG fixture with EXEC-115's mapping (Status bound to `current_state` before approval; `setup_world(before_approve=…)`): the right mapping still answers by rules at 1.0 with no AG-03 call; the EXEC-115 mapping asks, the stated-states reading answers 62, "assume first" answers 13 at < 0.6 with the note; with AG-03 it is planned once more (3 samples) — that reading counts the stated states instead of the named measure, so the person confirms it until US-204 lets stated criteria win. Affected suites 182 passed; full API suite 1,239 passed, 14 skipped; after merging master with US-203, 1,253 passed.)*

#### ENH-018 · Chart library choice · FEAT-068 *(new, 2026-11-04)*
**Why.** The user asked for the chart library to be configurable: ECharts (today) plus two alternatives suited to a modern dashboard — shadcn/ui Charts on Recharts (the chart set of our UI library) and Observable Plot (the library behind the observablehq.com D3 gallery). ggplot2 was considered and left out: it is R and gives static images only.
- **W1 — One spec, three drawers.** Every chart stays a `ChartSpec`; the library only changes how it is drawn. Clicks (cross-filter), highlight-and-grey, companion labels, the ordinal ramp, tenant palettes, dark mode and the table alternative work the same in all three.
- **W2 — Standard charts only.** The choice covers ranked bars, columns, trend, paired bars, stacked status bars and the donut. The visual catalogue (US-187/188), heat grid, meter, figure and sparkline keep their drawers, whatever the choice.
- **W3 — Loaded on demand.** Recharts and Observable Plot are separate chunks, downloaded only when the tenant chose them (US-148 bundle budget).

#### ENH-022 · Ask robust to everyday wording · FEAT-072 *(new, 2026-11-05; from the EXEC-139 diagnosis)*
**Why.** On the Dataverse connection, "How many number of project will be active in UAE region" answered **Open projects: 0**, certified, at confidence 1.0. The data has **35** open projects in GCC - UAE (26 of them in current state Active); "Open projects in UAE" and "How many active projects in UAE region" both answer 35. The failure is not this question's. Six gaps line up, and any question with everyday filler words can fall through them:
1. **Every word must mean something.** `STOP` (`ask/planner.py`) is a hand list of about 90 words; grammar words (will, would, shall, going, expected, should, being, been …) are missing, so each one is matched against data: partial value, spelling, then meaning.
2. **Free-text fields are linked like categories.** The value index (`knowledge/values.py`) skips a field only when it has more than 1,000 values or is typed `free_text`. `hold_reason` holds 8 values that are sentences ("Training material will be prepared once the build i…"), so it is indexed, and the partial rule ("a word that is part of only one value of a dimension") linked "will" to it with no check of value shape, word kind or rarity.
3. **The pack's value synonyms are not approved.** All 76 pack synonyms in `rag.value_synonyms` are `pending_review`, and only approved ones link. "UAE" worked by a partial match on "GCC - UAE"; "Saudi", "Dubai", "capex" would not. No screen or health check says so.
4. **Glossary terms need their exact words in order.** "active projects → Open projects" missed "project will be active"; "active" then matched three fields exactly (current state, status, request state) and the planner silently filtered current state = Active on top of Open projects, whose rule already includes Active.
5. **Confidence checks the plan against itself.** `confidence.check_rules` back-translates from the plan's own linked words, so a wrong link counts as coverage; how a value was found (partial 0.8 vs exact 1.0), a filter on a free-text field and a filter that repeats the measure's rule never lower the score.
6. **Sanity catches "no effect" but not "everything filtered away".** `_no_effect` (US-205) checks only filters that change nothing; a filter that takes a non-zero total to 0 went out certified.
Also: "will be active" is answered as today's count without saying Tela doesn't forecast; and the gold and paraphrase sets use clean wording, so none of this shows as a failing test.

**Working positions:**
- **W1 — Unknown is not a filter.** A word Ask can't place with an exact or approved match is left unused and lowers confidence; it is never turned into a filter by a weak match alone.
- **W2 — Fix the class, not the question.** Every story is accepted on a noise set built from approved gold examples (US-220), not on EXEC-139's question alone.
- **W3 — Weak evidence asks.** Partial, spelling and meaning links, free-text filters and a filter that empties the answer lower confidence below the threshold, so Ask asks (US-195 options) instead of answering.
- **W4 — No loss on clean wording.** The US-190 originals and paraphrases, the gold set and the planner unit gold stay at or above today's scores.
- **W5 — Rules first, still.** The fixes are in the rules planner, value linking, confidence and sanity; no new LLM call on the plain path (ENH-017 W4 stands).

Order: **Wave 1** US-220 (baseline first), US-213, US-214, US-215 · **Wave 2** US-216, US-217, US-218 · **Wave 3** US-219.

#### US-213 · Grammar words never become filters · S · FEAT-072 / FEAT-066 — `[x]` *(EXEC-140, branch `enh022-wave1`, no migration)*
As a person asking in my own words, I want words like "will", "should" or "number of" ignored, so that they never change my answer.
- [x] One curated English function-word list (auxiliary and modal verbs, pronouns, determiners, prepositions, conjunctions, quantity fillers "number of", "count of", politeness "kindly", "please") replaces the hand list, shared by the rules planner, value linking (`knowledge/values.link`) and confidence; words the model itself uses (labels, synonyms, values) stay known even when they are on the list. *(`ask/planner.GRAMMAR` (modal and auxiliary verbs, pronouns, fillers such as kindly, like, know, forward) joins `STOP`, so every user of `STOP` — rules planner, value linking, confidence, rewrite, free-hand — drops them. Words that carry meaning stay out: negation, comparison, time words and words labels use (need, share, expected, done, open, active). Exact phrases still match whole ("on hold").)*
- [x] A word that is neither known nor linked by an exact or approved synonym match is left in `unused`; it is not sent to partial or spelling matching if it is a function word. *(`values.link` sends only non-`STOP` words to partial, spelling and meaning matching.)*

Noise set: 184 → 240 of 278 right, confident wrong 21 → 9; every polite, filler, "number of" and future variant now right.)*

Depends: US-213.
As a person, I want "projects that will be active" read as "active projects", so that the approved meaning applies however I order my words.
- [x] Glossary and trap matching on the question's content words (function words dropped, singular and plural alike) within a short span in any order, not only the exact phrase; an exact phrase still wins. *(`planner.span_of`: the name's words other than grammar and question words (`SPAN_SKIP`; "project(s)" kept) side by side in any order once grammar words are dropped (`SPAN_SLACK = 0`: "projects deployed late" is not "late projects"). Used for the measure's own label and synonyms when cutting them out of the question (`_without_names`: "project will be active" names Open projects, so "active" filters nothing), for traps (`find_trap`: every phrase said in a row is tried first, so "projects with active requests" is not "active projects") and for glossary terms (`freehand.expand`: "number of project will be active" → "number of open projects will be").)*
- [x] A word that matches values of several fields exactly ("Active": current state, status, request state) and no glossary term becomes a clarify option (US-195) instead of a silent pick. *(`confidence.link_doubts`: a filter value that another field of the same table also holds for the same words, with none of the fields named (a distinctive word of a label names it: "reported Amber" is Reported status), caps the score at 0.55 and offers the plan on each other field as a reading: "How many amber projects" asks Reported status or Schedule KPI. Fields of other tables (Region / Request region) don't count: the measure's own table is the default.)*
- [x] A filter whose values are already inside the measure's own rule (current state = Active on Open projects) is reported in "Interpreted as" as narrowing the measure, and is a reading to offer, not applied silently. *(When the value is in the rule's literal values and its words are the measure's own (label or synonyms), and the question doesn't name the field: "Current state: Active narrows Open projects, whose rule already counts Active", score 0.55, the plan without it offered. A field named outright ("in current state Active", EXEC-115) is the person's criterion and is left alone; a filter on the rule's own field that every rule value covers changes nothing and says nothing (US-205's no-effect check covers it).)*
- [x] Tests: "How many number of project will be active in UAE region" → Open projects, Region = GCC - UAE (35 on the dev data); US-190 sets no loss. *(New `test_link_confidence_unit.py` (14); offline the plan is exactly Open projects + Region GCC - UAE at 1.0. Noise set 240 → 242 of 278, confident wrong 9 → 6 (EXEC-139 and "How many active project do we have" right; the 6 left are typos). Paraphrases: rules 31 → 33 of 125, confident wrong 15 → 12; with the retrieval stand-in 33 → 35, confident wrong 14 → 11, clarified 63 → 64; originals 63/63; EXEC-102 probe unchanged (6/38, 2 confident wrong). Gates raised to these. Live (dev Dataverse, master a401338): EXEC-139 → **35**, Open projects in GCC - UAE, certified.)*

#### US-218 · Zero-result check before answering · M · FEAT-072 / FEAT-065 — `[x]` *(EXEC-142, branch `enh022-wave2`, no migration)*
As a person, I want Ask to notice when its filters wipe out the answer, so that a 0 caused by a misread word is never certified.
- [x] Beside US-205's no-effect check: when a count or sum plan with filters returns 0 (or no rows), the total without its weakest filter (lowest link score) is read; if that is non-zero, Ask re-plans without it or asks which reading was meant, and the answer is not badged certified until the person chooses. *(`Orchestrator._zero_result` after planning, before `_decide`; `sanity.zero_result` takes `weak_refs` from `link_doubts` (partial 0.8, spelling 0.7, meaning ≤ 0.6, a sentence or a value the question never says 0.5; exact and synonym links are never weak, so a zero on them is a true zero and nothing is probed). `confidence.zero_result` makes the plan the one without that filter, keeps the original as a reading, caps at 0.55 and adds "Left out Delivery manager: Lina Farouk: with it nothing matched": at "balanced" Ask asks with the re-plan first; "assume first" answers it with badge `filter_left_out` ("Not certified · a filter was left out", web `ContentBadge`). Choosing a reading answers it certified as before.)*
- [x] One extra cached query at most; additive measures only, as US-205. *(The first probe is the plan's own total, which the cache serves to the answer; the second is the one without the filter. Additive measures, not multi-step plans or recipes.)*

As a person, I want to be told when I ask about the future, so that I know a figure is today's and not a forecast.

The note is the answer card's first data note (`fact: future`); when a forward measure of the same table has all its own words in the question ("How many open projects will go live") the note carries the action "Show go-lives in the next 30 days" — new refine op `set_measure` (filters and breakdowns it can't reach dropped, extra and derived measures cleared). A short horizon ("next month", "next week", "upcoming", "coming up", "soon") with a forward measure's words plans that measure outright, nearest window first, and says "Read “next month” as Go-lives in the next 30 days"; "next week/month/quarter/year" is no longer read as a grain (there is no next-month range).)*
- [x] Tests: EXEC-139's question carries the note; "go-lives next month" still plans the date measure. *(New `test_future_words_unit.py` (14): EXEC-139 → Open projects + UAE with the note and no offer; "go-lives next month", "Which projects will go live next month", "upcoming go-lives" → Go-lives in the next 30 days, no grain, no note (before: Go-lives per month, a past 12-month average, by month); present tense and a range ahead carry no note; `set_measure`. API `test_ask_api.py::test_future_question_says_today_and_offers_the_date_measure` (note, action applied as a refinement → certified go-lives). Noise set 242 / 278 and paraphrases 33 unchanged.)*

#### ENH-023 · Widget filters, card looks by layout, card and chart colours · FEAT-073 *(new, 2026-11-05)*
**Why.** The user asked for (1) filters inside a widget that can be configured, (2) widget cards that change with the layout, (3) a card colour set on the widget and (4) chart colours set on the widget. They chose: both fixed filters and filters readers use; palette swatches for cards; a scheme plus a colour per series or bar; cards that follow boards A, B and C of the EXEC-138 design canvas.
- **W1 — Palette only.** Card and chart colours are palette swatch names, never free colours, so light, dark and a tenant palette (US-151) carry over and the US-150 contrast pairs still hold. Brand-filled and dark cards hold a single figure only, so no chart axis sits on them.
- **W2 — The widget's, not the chart type's.** Card colour, chart colours and reader filters are kept in `spec.visual` and survive a new chart type when it can show them; they are frozen into published dashboards with the spec.
- **W3 — Narrow, never widen.** A reader's widget filter is checked against the fields the Creator offered and applied like a page filter, inside the reader's scope.

- [x] Canvas toolbar › Style › Chart colours: schemes with a strip of their colours, and a colour per series (several series) or per bar / slice (one series), listed from the widget's last run.
- [x] Tests: Vitest (helper, Recharts/Plot data, ECharts option, dialog); API as US-223.
#### ENH-024 · Agent run log with LLM prompts and replies · FEAT-074 *(new, 2026-11-05)*
**Why.** The user asked for (1) a log of every AG-01 – AG-10 run in the Admin screen with all its steps, (2) the text that went to the LLM and (3) what the LLM replied. Today only metadata reaches storage: `chat.turn_events` holds Ask's state changes, `ops.llm_invocations` (DATA-046) holds agent, task, model, tokens and status, and the gateway's ledger and logs are forbidden prompt text (S02 §1, TEST-080). Screens outside Ask (AG-04, AG-09, AG-10, packs) carry no run id at all. Mapping the agents found BUG-024: AG-09 and AG-10 call the gateway with task names it refuses, so they have only ever used their fallbacks — nobody could see it.
- **W1 — One run, every step.** An agent run is one row (`ops.agent_runs`: agent, surface, turn or object, person, start, end, outcome); its steps are ordered rows (`ops.agent_steps`: kind `rule`, `retrieve`, `llm_call`, `validate`, `retry`, `fallback`, `decision`, `error`; label; duration; small JSON detail). Rules-only agents (AG-01 routing, AG-05, AG-06, AG-07 lite, AG-03's rules planner) log their steps too, so a run with no LLM call still reads end to end.
- **W2 — The text the model really saw.** The prompt is captured in the gateway after masking (what left Tela), and the reply before parsing (raw), with provider, model, tokens, latency, scanner verdict and the schema-check result. Blocked calls keep the masked prompt and the block reason, never the unmasked text.
- **W3 — A store apart from logs (DEC-25).** Text lives only in `ops.agent_steps` under row-level tenant isolation: never in application logs, the ledger, OTel spans or audit events, so TEST-080 and the log-redaction suite keep passing unchanged. Admin only; opening a step's text is an audit event; a tenant switch turns text capture off (metadata still kept); text purged after the retention window.
- **W4 — Never slows or breaks an answer.** Capture is written after the call, best-effort and bounded (prompt and reply each capped at 64 KB, marked "cut"); a failed log write is counted in metrics and never fails the agent.

Order: BUG-024 first (small, makes AG-09/AG-10 show real runs), then US-225, US-226, US-227, US-228, US-229.

- [x] Instrument: Ask orchestrator (AG-01 route, AG-02 clarify, AG-03 rewrite / rules plan / LLM plan / votes / link / repair, sanity and zero-result checks, AG-07, AG-08 narrate and explain), AG-04 model assistant and pack mapping suggestions, AG-05 report planner, AG-06 canvas edit, AG-09, AG-10. Non-Ask surfaces get a run id of their own (`object_ref` = model, pack, item or draft id). *(Ask: every stage event (`Orchestrator.emit`) is a step, plus rules planner, plan path, confidence decision, no-effect and zero-result checks; AG-04 assistant and pack mapping, AG-05, AG-06, AG-09, AG-10 open their own runs with a rules or check step. Not covered: the AI settings test call (AG-01 "Reply with OK"), which is a connection test.)*

#### ENH-025 · Pack Studio: author packs, knowledge and blueprints in the app, with AI help · FEAT-075 *(new, 2026-11-05)*
**Why.** The user asked for (1) an Admin screen to see and create packs, (2) a screen to add business knowledge (context) and report blueprints, (3) AI that reads the introspected schema and drafts the entities, fields, links, measures and dimensions, (4) the PMO pack as a ready-made template for widgets, reports and dashboards, (5) chart patterns, dashboard rules and analysis recipes working with the new pack, and (6) less hand work in the logical mapping. Today a pack is YAML in `packs/<key>/`, loaded into the global `model.starter_packs` by `tela-admin load-packs` (owner role only; app roles have no INSERT, migration 0007; released versions frozen by SHA). Its knowledge files are read from disk by pack key (`knowledge/patterns.py::_pack_dir`, `knowledge/guidance.py::_load`, `content/wording.py`), so a pack made anywhere else would get no chart patterns, rules, recipes, blueprints or glossary. An Admin can apply a pack (Admin › Starter content, US-153/154/199–201) and build a model with AG-04 (Model Studio: names and types only, ≤ 60 ops a patch), and can upload knowledge documents (US-102) and review definitions (US-203), but cannot author a pack.
- **W0 — Packs live in the database.** A pack draft and its released versions are tenant rows: the manifest plus each knowledge file (glossary, value synonyms, dashboard rules, chart patterns, analysis recipes, report blueprints, gold examples) as a validated JSON document. System packs (PMO) are still loaded by the CLI, stay global and read-only, and can be cloned. One `PackSource` replaces the file reads, so install, pack updates (US-120), indexing and the wording check read a DB pack exactly as they read a file pack.
- **W1 — Start from a template, not a blank page.** "New pack" offers *Clone a pack* (PMO as template), *From an approved model* (the model, its certified fields, reports and dashboards become a pack draft) or *Blank*. Cloning onto a new schema re-points the template: the existing matcher (`packs/manifest.py::match`) and the US-200/201 value and LLM suggestions map template entities and fields to the new source, and every measure, dimension, link, blueprint widget, chart pattern and recipe that refers to them follows. Items that can't be re-pointed are listed with the reason, as Unavailable is today.
- **W2 — AI drafts, the Admin decides.** AG-04 grows a pack-author mode: from the catalogue it drafts entities, fields (labels, types, semantic and sensitivity tags), links (FK first, then name-based, cardinality checked by two counts), then measures and dimensions **entity by entity in batches**, with the template pack as worked examples. Tela, not the LLM, profiles text columns' frequent values (US-199 `profile_definition`) so filter values are real values. Every proposal is validated (manifest schema, compile against the catalogue, US-205 sanity check, a dry-run count) before it is shown, is marked "AI proposed", and is only kept on Accept. Business rules stay a person's decision: a proposed rule is uncertified until certified (FEAT-047).
- **W3 — Release like code.** Draft → checks → release (frozen, semver) → install or update on connections, with a diff against the previous version. Export and import as the same YAML layout as `packs/<key>/` so a pack can live in git.

Order: US-230 (DB packs, no UI change) → US-231 (parity) → US-232 (Pack Studio) → US-235 (clone and re-point: the biggest saving) → US-233 and US-234 (knowledge and blueprint editors) → US-236 (model to pack) → US-237 (AI pack author) → US-238 (release checks). DEC-26 before US-232; DEC-27 before US-237.

#### ENH-026 · LLM review on every agent run: rules draft first, the LLM confirms, corrects or asks · FEAT-076 *(new, 2026-11-06)*
**Why.** The user asked that AG-01 – AG-10 all call the LLM on every run, irrespective of the confidence score, with the rules' result as input, so that output quality goes up; AG-05 and AG-06 included. Today the agents are rules-first and the LLM is a fallback: AG-01 (`ask/planner.py::route_intent`, `ask/routing.py::route`), AG-05 (`content/planner.py`) and AG-06 (`content/chat_edit.py::parse`) never call it (the gateway already allows `intent`, `report_plan` and `canvas_edit`, `settings.py:44`); AG-03 is called only when the rules can't plan, the filters change nothing, or a definition needs words (`orchestrator.py::_plan_for`); AG-02's model back-check is off (`ask_backcheck_model = False`); AG-08 `explain` runs only with knowledge hits. Measured cost of the LLM on the dev stack: AG-03 on qwen3:8b median 73 s, max 307 s a question (EXEC-113); llama3.2:3b ≈ 44 s a call (EXEC-102). Measured accuracy: rules 36/36 originals; LLM paraphrases 3/38 with 7 confident-wrong (EXEC-113). So the LLM must review, never silently override (DEC-28).
- **W1 — One review step for every agent.** The rules run as today and produce a draft. The LLM gets the request, the draft in plain words, the candidates the rules considered and the retrieved knowledge, and replies `confirm`, `correct` (with a replacement in the agent's own typed output) or `ask` (2 – 4 options). Its output goes through the same checks as the rules' (spec and scope validation, RULE-008, wording check, SQL safety, layout guards). A failed, slow, refused or invalid reply leaves the rules draft standing, marked "LLM review: fallback", so an answer is never worse than without review.
- **W2 — The score still decides (DEC-28 point 1).** "Irrespective of confidence" means the LLM is always called, not that the score is ignored. A confirm is one more signal that lifts the score; a correction that differs from the rules draft caps the score below the person's threshold and both readings become AG-02's options. Write refusals (AG-01) and every validator always win over the LLM.
- **W3 — One call where the rules planned (DEC-28 point 2).** AG-03 makes one review call when the rules produced a plan; the three-sample vote stays for questions the LLM plans itself.
- **W4 — A tenant switch (DEC-28 point 3).** AI settings get "LLM review": on by default when the tenant's provider is hosted, off for a local provider (Ollama), so the local stack keeps answering in seconds. No provider → rules only, as today. `narrate` stays behind "send totals to the model" (DEC-01, RULE-019) whatever the switch says.
- **W5 — Kept only if it helps.** The gold suite runs with review off and on; the default stays on only if right answers go up and confident-wrong answers do not (US-246).

Order: US-239 (setting and shared review step) → US-240 and US-241 (Ask: AG-03, AG-02) → US-246 Ask gate → US-242 (AG-01) → US-243 and US-244 (AG-05, AG-06; independent of Ask, can run in parallel) → US-245 (AG-04, AG-08) → US-246 final gate. DEC-28 decided before US-239.

#### ENH-028 · Ask answer modes and business context: understand the question, then answer from definitions, fields, measures or SQL · FEAT-078 *(new, 2026-11-12)*
**Why.** Agent run `01a1ca5c` (2026-11-11): "Show the List of open Projects" was answered "Open projects: 103", certified at 1.0, after three LLM reviews (≈ 70 s of 72 s). "list" is a stop word, nothing plans one row per record, and every check compares the plan with its own words. The analysis (EXEC-186, `project-docs/spikes/ask-answer-correctness.md`) found the same pattern behind BUG-015/017/025/026 and EXEC-102/113/115/139/171/172/180: `ask/` has 82 commits (41 to `planner.py`), 62 regexes and 33 word lists; original wording passes (36/36) and paraphrases mostly don't (1–7 of 38). Root causes: (R1) the planner matches words, not meaning; (R2) no step decides the kind of answer (number, list, breakdown, trend …), so the default is one number; (R3) checks compare the plan with itself; (R4) confidence is a stack of incident caps; (R5) the LLM reviews catalogue choices instead of reading language; (R6) gold has no answer-shape coverage. Without a pack (EXEC-186 follow-up) Ask can only use Admin-made measures, and AG-04's rules never propose a count, so Ask's meaning lives almost entirely in the pack. A measure bundles entity, aggregation and business condition, so every combination needs its own measure and the planner has to guess one by name.
The user's plan (2026-11-12, reviewed and accepted as revised):
- **W1 — Business context per source.** Admin → Knowledge already takes Markdown, text, PDF and Word per connection with a review gate (US-102) and proposes the definitions they state (US-203); it also takes **.xlsx** with a definitions template (term | meaning | table | field | operator | values), whose approved rows become runnable named conditions.
- **W2 — Context first, with an order of authority.** Every Ask mode reads approved definitions first, then certified measures; raw document text is only a hint to the LLM. A definition counts as found only when it matches the question's words (retrieval always returns something). A definition that disagrees with a certified measure is shown to the Admin, never settled silently.
- **W3 — One answer-kind step for every mode.** A `QuestionFrame` (shape, subject, measure phrase, conditions, groupings, time, order, follow-up, words used/unused) is built before any catalogue matching. One small LLM call fills it, cross-checked by the deterministic parser. It replaces the AG-01 and AG-03 reviews and the AG-02 back-check in the frame modes.
- **W4 — One "Ask answer mode" per connection** (the user's switches B/C, "use measures" and "Direct SQL" merged into one choice, so no meaningless combinations): **Certified measures** (C, today), **Fields + definitions** (B: entity, aggregation or rows, named conditions, fields), **Hybrid** (B, using certified measures as trusted shortcuts and checks), **Direct SQL** (AG-10 writes SQL, the Custom SQL pipeline checks and scopes it). Each mode's latest gold score is shown beside the choice; the mode is recorded on every agent run.
- **W5 — Certified means approved.** A B or Hybrid answer is certified only when every definition it used is approved and its measure part is certified or equals a certified measure; otherwise it carries "not certified" with its rule shown. Direct SQL answers always carry the Custom SQL badge and show the SQL.
- **W6 — Measured, not assumed.** Gold gets an answer shape on every example and a frame gold; each mode is run on the same set; Hybrid becomes the default only if it is at least as right as Certified on originals, better on paraphrases and lists, and confident-wrong does not go up.

Order: DEC-29, DEC-30 → US-250 (baseline) → US-251 and US-252 (in parallel) → US-253 (frame, shadow) → US-254 (Plan B) → US-255 (Hybrid) and US-258 (verify, confidence, reviews off) → US-256 (mode setting) → US-257 (Direct SQL, after DEC-30) → US-259 (gate per mode, clean-up). Migrations: next free numbers after 0054, taken in merge order.

It runs after "Answered", so the answer is never held up; the run's duration includes it. `TELA_ASK_FRAME` = `shadow` (default; LLM only when the tenant has a provider), `rules`, `off`; LLM wait `TELA_ASK_FRAME_TIMEOUT_S` 30 s. #### US-254 · Fields + definitions planner (Plan B) · L · FEAT-078 / FEAT-019 — `[x]` *(EXEC-195, merged into master, no migration; full suite after merge 2,141 passed)*
Depends: US-252, US-253.
As a Viewer, I want answers built from the fields and our business terms, so that any combination of terms works without a measure made for it.
- [x] Ground each frame slot separately: subject → entity, conditions → named conditions or field + value (value linking, US-173), groupings → dimensions or fields, time → date field, measure phrase → aggregation and field (count of records when none). Each slot gets a score and a reason (named, synonym, meaning, value, definition).
  *(EXEC-195: `ask/fields_planner.py` (`ground`, `Slot`); reasons also `partial`, `spelling`, `chosen`, `only`, `default`. Conditions are read word span by word span, longest first, so "open critical" is two conditions; the approved conditions are the stored ones plus the rule of every certified count measure with no stored row for its term (`measure_conditions`: named conditions are only written on knowledge indexing, so a newly approved model has none). Words the value index misses are looked up in the scoped value lists of the subject's fields. A subject that is an approved term ("overdue go-lives") gives its table; "which region has the most open projects" makes region the grouping. Ages on dates: "on hold for more than 90 days" → before today − 90 days. Words the frame left unread are read as conditions too; those still unread lower the score ×0.6 and the certification.)*
- [x] Compile one template per shape: number (aggregation + conditions), list_of_records (one row per record: name and key display fields, conditions, table, limit 500), breakdown, ranking, trend, comparison/share; built on ad-hoc measures (US-163) and `field:` breakdowns, so scope, hidden fields and limits apply as today. A shape with no template → "Tela can't answer this kind of question yet", never another shape.
  *(EXEC-195: `compile_plan`. A list breaks down by the record's name/title and number fields (`scoring.is_record_ref`) with `having count > 0` (fixes the EXEC-189 finding: records outside the condition were listed with 0) and a table visual. **Deviation:** no `limit: 500` on a list — a limit makes US-250 scoring read the answer as a ranking; the connection's row cap still applies. Ranking: sort by the measure, limit N or 10, records without the value filtered out (PostgreSQL sorts NULL first). Share without a grouping on one value ("what share are in UAE") gives every value's share. Distribution, record detail and definition refused. A term whose rule reads a calculated value (health, a ratio) answers through its certified measure alone and is refused when combined (ad-hoc rules can't read calculators). Ad-hoc measures run under the answer mode, not the person's free-hand grant: `QueryContext.fields`, set by the orchestrator and by the stream / turn read-back of a `planner = fields` turn.)*
- [x] An ungrounded slot asks about that slot only ("Which field do you mean by region?") or refuses naming it.
  *(EXEC-195: subject, grouping, time and measure field ask (`kind: field`, key `fields:<slot>:<phrase>`, the choice kept in the conversation's memory by the existing clarification endpoint); a condition is refused naming its words ("Tela couldn't tell what “zorbified” means for projects …").)*
- [x] Certified badge per W5; the "How this was answered" panel lists the conditions used and where each came from.
  *(EXEC-195: certified only when every slot was named, a synonym, an approved term, a stored value or a choice, and the measure part is a certified measure (the one whose rule is exactly the conditions first, else the count/total with no rule); `complete.fields` (`conditions_used` with source and rule, `certified_by`, `not_certified_because`); a certified Plan B answer says "Built from approved terms and fields: …" instead of "Not certified · built from your question". Ask side panel: "Conditions used" section (Aurora layout, ≥ 1280 px).)*
- [x] Tests: the `01a1ca5c` question gives a 103-row table on the TPG fixture; "How many open projects in UAE" = 35; negation and time; every shape template.
  *(EXEC-195: the TPG fixture has **65** open projects (103 was the live Dataverse source) and **11** open in GCC - UAE (35 was live too); the tests assert the fixture's figures. `test_fields_planner_unit.py` 28 (every shape on DuckDB, negation, numeric, total of a field, age on a date, ranking of records and of groups, trend on a named date, comparison, share, refusals, asks and the remembered choice, hidden fields, order of authority and an unsettled conflict, certification, calculated terms, the US-250 lists: **36/44 lists and 35/44 count twins** — rules 0/44 and 40/44); `test_fields_planner_api.py` 7 (the `01a1ca5c` question → 65 rows, certified by Open projects; UAE = 11; negation under the Creator's scope; breakdown and trend; no-template refusal; unknown term; a scoped Viewer without free-hand access asks, chooses, and reads the turn back); Vitest `enh027-ask-panel.test.tsx` +1. Offline on all 622 US-250 gold examples (rules frame, no LLM): 246 right, 65 confident-wrong, 171 asked, 123 refused (rules planner 447 / 120).)*

Direct SQL graded by rows (`gold.evaluate_direct` + `scoring.direct_match`: rows as value sets, ordered only for sort + limit, a list as the same records without the 0-count rows, midnight timestamps as dates, an empty/zero expected result never matches) and kind of answer; a wrong one is confident-wrong; queries logged as `evaluation`. Connections > Ask counts a source's questions in all-source runs; mixed-mode runs aren't any mode's score. `gold-report` shows the mode. Fields and Hybrid answers (ad-hoc measures, never a spec match) are graded by value for a figure and as the same records for a list (`scoring.frame_match`; before, every Fields figure and list failed the row check). **Offline per mode** (`test_gold_modes_offline_unit.py`, master da57dc7 + this branch, 622 US-250 examples, rules frame, no LLM; right / confident-wrong): Certified **447 / 120** (originals 124 / 1, paraphrases 37 / 46, lists 0 / 44, counts 40 / 4); Fields **240 / 54** (originals 66 / 16, paraphrases 10 / 6, lists 35 / 1, counts 34 / 2); Hybrid **244 / 50** (originals 67 / 15, paraphrases 10 / 6, lists 35 / 1, counts 34 / 2). Direct SQL needs a model: no offline score. **Open:** the live runs on dev Dataverse per mode (dev stack to 0057 first).)*
- [~] Hybrid becomes the default for new connections only if: originals ≥ Certified, paraphrases and lists better, confident-wrong not higher, median time ≤ 20 s.
  *(EXEC-204, offline evidence only: Hybrid misses — originals 67 vs 124, paraphrases 10 vs 37 (62 asked, 47 refused); lists better (35 vs 0) and confident-wrong lower (50 vs 120). Certified stays the default (`modes.DEFAULT`) unless the live run with the LLM frame says otherwise; time not measured offline.)*
- [ ] Clean-up: word lists and confidence caps that no longer change any gold result in Hybrid are removed one at a time, each removal gated by the full gold run.
- [~] Results and the default decision in implement_log and DEC-29. *(EXEC-204: offline results in the log and noted under DEC-29; the decision after the live runs.)*

#### US-260 · Direct SQL answers get every answer action · S · FEAT-078 / FEAT-021 / FEAT-018 — `[x]` *(EXEC-207, 2026-11-12; migration 0058)*
Depends: US-257.
*(Asked 2026-11-12 by the user: "Allow the Direct SQL mode also with all option need to show. like pin, drill data, widgets, suggestions, gold example, helpful, not helpful and this isn't not what i asked.")*
As a Creator, I want a Direct SQL answer to offer the same actions as any other answer, so that I can keep, explore and correct it the same way. It stays uncertified.
- [x] Chart and **Suggestions**: the answer is drawn as a chart when one fits (`direct_sql.answer_viz`: the `viz` event, plus `visuals` and one `charts` entry per suitable visual); Suggestions lists the other suitable charts and switches the chart without another query; Show as table / Show as chart.
- [x] **Pin** (widget): `complete.can_pin` is now true. The card checks the SQL again (`/custom-sql/check`) and saves it as a Custom SQL widget (`/widgets/custom-sql`, `drafted_by_ai`), in the library or on one of your reports, with the chosen visual. It keeps the Custom SQL badge, and the publish check still blocks it until certified (RULE-009). Needs `widgets.create` and `customsql.use` (Creators have both).
- [x] **Drill to records** and **Refine**: follow-up questions in the same conversation and source ("List the records behind: <question>", and "— where <column> is <value>" from a clicked mark; quick chips Top 5, By month, Compare with the previous period; free text). Direct SQL reads a follow-up with the earlier question (US-257b) and writes new SQL; "As table" switches the view only.
- [x] **Save as gold example**: a Direct SQL answer has no Query Spec, so `POST /knowledge/curation/from-answer {turn_id}` (`widgets.create`, own answer or an Admin) adds a gold-example proposal with no spec to Admin → Knowledge → From misses. Its evidence is the turn and its SQL (source `direct_sql`, migration 0058; detail "SQL: …", first 500 characters). The Admin writes the spec there and approves it through the gold gate. The same question again adds evidence to the same proposal; a question that is already a gold example, already reviewed, or held out is refused. Admins are notified once.
- [x] **Helpful / Not helpful / This isn't what I asked** (`AnswerFeedback`): the feedback API already took any finished turn; the flagged answer has no spec.
- [x] Tests: `test_direct_sql_actions_api.py` 3 (viz with a chart per suitable visual, `can_pin`, pin as a Custom SQL widget, a down-vote; curation proposal with the SQL, idempotent, queue counts, approve needs a spec then makes the gold example, a second suggestion conflicts; viewer 403, unknown turn 404, a certified answer 409); `test_ask_mode_api.py` and `test_curation_api.py` updated (`can_pin` true, `pending_by_source.direct_sql`); Vitest `us257b-direct-sql.test.tsx` +1 (chart, Suggestions → Donut, Pin dialog request, gold suggestion, Drill and Top 5 as follow-ups in the same conversation and source) and the first test now expects Pin, Drill, gold and feedback.

## Track UX — Experience redesign (runs alongside P2 and P3)

**Goal:** one coherent, modern, accessible interface for all three levels: **Viewer** (read and ask), **Creator** (build and share) and **Admin** (connect, govern, publish, operate). **Exit:** every Admin, Creator and Viewer screen runs on the new design system; the designs are signed off by STK-008 and STK-001; axe finds no serious or critical issues on any redesigned screen; the existing Playwright journeys and the scope suites pass unchanged.

> **Status (EXEC-007, 2026-10-21): US-132 v1 delivered** — the [Tela design system](https://claude.ai/artifact/CAwNDH34udx9cBS87bXfC3)
> is published in Claude Design and its tokens are in `apps/web/src/design/tokens.json`.
>
> **Status (EXEC-008, 2026-10-21): US-133 screens drafted** — 20 screens for Viewer, Creator and Admin plus a states
> board on the [Tela screens](https://claude.ai/artifact/XjJncHdMit2ZWe3MCMzJnv) canvas.
>
> **Status (EXEC-010, 2026-10-21): Tela logo applied; US-133 complete apart from sign-off** — colours re-keyed to the
> logo, 5 more boards (report planner, share dialog, version history, notifications, add-a-connection flow) and the
> J1, J5, J9 and connection click paths. Next: review with STK-008 and STK-001, then US-134.
>
> **Status (EXEC-011, 2026-10-21): US-134 done on branch `ux-foundation`** — Tailwind v4 + shadcn/ui (Radix) generated
> from `tokens.json`, light/dark/system theme saved per user, token-driven ECharts theme, route-level code splitting,
> `/dev/ui` catalogue. Old screens unchanged. Next: US-135, US-136, then the shell (US-137).
>
> **Status (EXEC-012, 2026-10-21): US-135, US-136 and US-137 done on `ux-foundation`** — forms on React Hook Form + Zod
> (schemas tested against the API's OpenAPI document), one `DataTable` on TanStack Table v9 + Virtual, and the new app
> shell (grouped sidebar, breadcrumbs, ask bar, notifications, account menu, ⌘K palette). Admin data and access screens,
> the audit log and the pin/share/publish dialogs now run on the new system. Next: Wave 2 (US-138 – US-140) and the
> EPIC-07/08 screens (US-078, US-080, US-087), which also bring the Govern items into the sidebar.
>
> **Status (EXEC-013, 2026-10-21): Wave 2 and the EPIC-07/08 screens done on `ux-foundation`** — Home (US-138),
> dashboard viewer (US-139, print route deferred to US-096) and Ask (US-140) on the new system; Build → Fields
> (US-078) and Custom SQL (US-080, CodeMirror 6) and Govern → Certification (US-087). Small API additions: Home
> suggestions and tile owners, a `kpi` block on figure widget runs, entity fields, links, tables and engine in the
> catalog. Next: Wave 3 (US-141 – US-146), then US-147/148.

> Reports list on the new system; dnd-kit added (library drag-in, section reorder); one API addition: a certified
> measure can be added to a report as a figure (`POST /reports/{id}/items` with `kind: "measure"`). Next: US-142.
>
> **Status (EXEC-015, 2026-10-21): US-142 done on `ux-foundation`.** Composer, publish steps, version timeline and share
> dialog on the new system; publish requests are now recorded and handled under Govern → Publish requests (migration
> 0012). Next: US-143 – US-146 (Admin console), then US-147/148.

> **Status (EXEC-018, 2026-10-21): US-147 – US-148 done on `ux-foundation`; every EPIC-UX build story is done.** Motion
> presets with reduced motion; axe, keyboard-only J1/J5/J9, screenshot, bundle-budget and LCP gates run in the
> Playwright suite on the live stack; `styles.css` is gone. US-132/133 still wait for STK-008/STK-001 sign-off.

> **Status (EXEC-016, 2026-10-21): US-143 done on `ux-foundation`.** Admin overview at `/admin` from one
> `GET /admin/overview`, with the Admin sub-navigation on every Admin and Govern screen and filtered links. Found on
> the dev stack: the worker has no handler for `dashboard.published`, `model.version_approved` or
> `certification.decided`, so those outbox events die after 10 attempts (shown on the overview). Next: US-144 – US-146.
> *(Resolved: handlers shipped with EXEC-020/022; EXEC-029 added a handler-coverage test and `tela-admin
> outbox-requeue`, and replayed the 27 dead-lettered dev events.)*
>
> **Status (2026-10-21): planned.** Added at the user's request after EXEC-005. The UI today is
> functional but plain: one hand-written `styles.css` (≈ 200 lines), a single top bar with every link, plain
> HTML tables and forms. This track rebuilds the look and the interaction patterns without changing any API
> behaviour, scope rule or figure.

**Ground rules (from the design pack; the redesign must not break them)**

- The web app is an untrusted client: it hides UI by permission but never enforces it (COMP-001). Every action stays checked by the API.
- The web app **never computes figures** (COMP-001). KPI cards, deltas and sparklines show values returned by the widget run API; previous-period change comes from the server (US-050).
- Charts stay on **Apache ECharts** from the internal chart spec (ADR-007). No second chart engine. *(Amended 2026-10-28: D3 joins ECharts for the gallery visuals in US-187 / US-188, behind the same `tela_chart_spec`; see ENH-014.)*
- Exports stay **server-side jobs** that re-check scope and are audited (REQ-107, US-095 – US-097). No client-side PDF or Excel generation.
- Certified, personal, stale and as-of badges stay visible wherever a figure is shown; every chart keeps a table alternative (NFR-021).
- Building is supported from 1280 px; reading down to 768 px (NFR-028). WCAG 2.2 AA (NFR-021, REC-017).

**Design method — Claude Design first, then code**

1. Build the Tela design system in **Claude Design** (a design-system artifact): tokens, components, chart palette, light and dark themes.
2. Design every screen in Claude Design from that system, per role, including empty, loading, error, no-access and out-of-scope states. Review clickable prototypes with STK-008 (PMO owner) and STK-001 before any code.
3. Export the tokens to `apps/web/src/design/tokens.json`; Tailwind and the ECharts theme read from them, so design and code share one source.
4. Build screens from the approved designs; Playwright screenshots at 1280 px and 768 px are compared with the designs at review.
5. Link each Claude Design artifact URL in the story it belongs to.

**Library choices (working position; confirm with DEC-22)**

| Need | Adopt | Not adopted, and why |
|---|---|---|
| Components and screens | **shadcn/ui** (Radix UI + Tailwind CSS); components are copied into `apps/web/src/components/ui` and owned by us | MUI, Mantine, Ant Design — the technical design already chose Tailwind + Radix (technical_document §stack); Ant/MUI bring their own styling systems |
| Dashboard building blocks | KPI cards, stat tiles and layouts built on shadcn/ui, following Tremor's and shadcn blocks' patterns | **Tremor** library — its charts run on Recharts, a second chart engine against ADR-007 |
| SQL editor (custom SQL, US-080) | **CodeMirror 6** with `@codemirror/lang-sql`, lazy-loaded on the SQL screen only (added EXEC-013) | Monaco (≈ 2 MB, web workers, harder under the strict CSP); a plain textarea (no highlighting or completion) |
| Charts (standard and heavy data) | **Apache ECharts** (already in use), with a token-driven light/dark theme and a colour-blind-safe palette | Recharts, Nivo, Chart.js, Visx, D3 — ADR-007; one engine keeps chart parity between widget, chat and PDF |
| Data tables and reports | **TanStack Table** (sorting, filtering, grouping, column visibility) in one shared `DataTable` | AG Grid Community — reconsider only if pivoting or Excel-like editing becomes a requirement |
| Large lists | **TanStack Virtual** (drill records, audit log, model fields, notifications) | react-window |
| Animation | **Motion** for dialogs, sheets, popovers and list changes; ≤ 200 ms; honours `prefers-reduced-motion` | GSAP (not needed); AutoAnimate (Motion covers it) |
| Icons and micro-interactions | **Lucide** icons (shadcn default); **Lottie** (`lottie-react`) only for empty states and first-run, if the design calls for it, lazy-loaded, ≤ 50 KB each | Rive |
| Server state | **TanStack Query** (already in use) | SWR |
| Forms | **React Hook Form + Zod** (already in the planned stack, summary.md §technical) | Formik |
| Drag and drop | **react-grid-layout** (already in use) for the 12-column canvas; **dnd-kit** for ordered lists (report order, sections, plan boxes) with its keyboard sensor | Gridstack.js |
| PDF / Excel export | Server-side as designed: Playwright print route (PDF) and openpyxl (Excel). This track styles the print route only | react-pdf, jsPDF, pdfmake, SheetJS in the browser — would bypass the scoped, audited export job |
| Also | `cmdk` (shadcn Command) for the command palette; `sonner` (shadcn Toast) for toasts and Undo | — |

Deviations recorded in US-134:
- **React 18, not 19:** shadcn/ui v4 sources assume React 19 (`ref` as a prop). Our copies use `forwardRef`, which Radix
  `asChild`, portals and presence need; a unit test fails on any "cannot be given refs" warning.
- **Strict CSP kept** (`style-src 'self'`): `sonner` and `react-style-singleton` (Radix scroll lock) inject `<style>` tags,
  which the CSP blocks. Both are patched with `pnpm patch` (`apps/web/patches/`): sonner's CSS is imported as a file and
  the scroll lock uses a constructable stylesheet. Re-check the patches when upgrading either package.

Deviations recorded in US-135 – US-137 (EXEC-012):
- **TanStack Table v9, not v8:** v9 (`useTable` + `tableFeatures`) is the maintained line; the last v8 release is from
  April 2025. Screens never touch it directly: they pass simple column definitions to `components/ui/data-table.tsx`.
- **Native select in forms:** short option lists use a styled native `<select>` (shadcn/ui NativeSelect): it works with
  React Hook Form as is, allows an empty "Choose…" option (Radix Select does not) and keeps the platform picker on touch.
- **Zod schemas tested, not generated:** `src/api/openapi.json` is a checked-in snapshot of the API's document
  (`pnpm openapi`); a web test compares each Zod request schema with it, and an API test fails when the snapshot is stale.

Deviations recorded in US-078, US-080, US-087 and US-138 – US-140 (EXEC-013):
- **CodeMirror 6 for the SQL editor (added to DEC-22 in EXEC-013):** no pinned library highlighted SQL; CodeMirror is
  lazy-loaded with `/sql` only. Its styles come from `style-mod`, patched (`apps/web/patches/style-mod@4.1.4.patch`) to
  mount a constructable stylesheet on the document, as the strict CSP blocks `<style>` tags; re-check on upgrade.
- **Server data added for the redesign (no new endpoints):** `GET /home` returns suggested questions and tile owners;
  `POST /widgets/{id}/run` returns a `kpi` block (change and sparkline) for single figures; `GET /query/catalog` returns
  entity fields, approved links, source table and column names and the engine. The web app still computes no figures.
- **Left out of the designs:** Home's "At a glance" KPI row (keeps chart code out of the entry chunk), the viewer's Stale
  badge (no server freshness rule yet), the certification "Ask for changes" option (not an API decision) and the
  builder's look-alike warning (look-alikes are computed for the reviewer only).

**Sequencing.** Wave 1: US-132 – US-136 (design system, screen designs, foundations). Wave 2: US-137 – US-140 (shell, Viewer). Wave 3: US-141 – US-146 (Creator, Admin). US-147 – US-148 run from Wave 1 onwards. Screens of epics still open (EPIC-07, EPIC-08, EPIC-10 part 2, EPIC-11, US-106) are built directly in the new system once US-134 is done, so they are not built twice.
